## The Victorious Ranvir: (<term>Astrology</term>)
<note>original: "Shri Ranvir Vijaya : (Jyotishyam)". This title likely commemorates Maharaja Ranbir Singh of Jammu and Kashmir (reigned 1856–1885), a great patron of Sanskrit learning. "Jyotish" is the traditional Indian science of astronomy and astrology.</note>

**Folios 18 + 64 = 82 (Complete)**
<note>original: "Patrani ... Sampurnam". This note by a scribe or librarian indicates the manuscript consists of two sections totaling 82 individual leaves and is physically complete.</note>

# Page 9

<warning>The bottom right corner of the page is damaged and partially missing text. Faded yellow highlights appear on certain syllables throughout the text.</warning>

**Om! Salutations to the illustrious Ganesha.** <note>original: "ओ श्रीगणेशाय नमः" (oṃ śrīgaṇeśāya namaḥ). It is traditional in Sanskrit manuscripts to begin with an invocation to Ganesha, the remover of obstacles.</note> **[Salutations also to] Ganesha, Shiva-Bhaskara, and Ramachandra.** 

Having offered prostrations, Dharmeshvara now speaks this commentary <note>original: "टीका" (ṭīkā)</note> for the sake of awakening the understanding of the *Chamatkara Chintamani* <note>The title "Chamatkara Chintamani" translates to "The Jewel of Astonishing Thought," a significant historical text on Vedic astrology.</note> || 1 || Now, for the purpose of understanding the knowledge of the fruits—both favorable and unfavorable—of the houses for a person born with a birth chart <term>Lagna-kundali; the map of the sky at the exact moment of an individual's birth</term>, Narayanacharya explains the *Chamatkara Chintamani*. For the sake of completing the work without obstacles, he performs an auspicious opening prayer <term>Mangala; a traditional prayer to ensure the success of a literary work</term> in the form of a salutation to Shri Krishna, which he records for the instruction of his students. 

In this context, at the time of the beginning of the <unclear>Ashwinay</unclear> season, Narayanacharya, with his body, speech, and mind, bows to Shri Krishna—who is as delightful as the moon, embraced by Radha like a dark cloud <note>original: "घनं" (ghanam)</note> embraced by lightning, and who wears garments of yellow silk. Having offered this salutation, he says: "I shall now properly compose the work named *Chamatkara Chintamani*." || 2 || 

Furthermore, through the practice of worshipping the young Lord Govinda <note>An epithet for the youthful Krishna</note>, he reveals the previously mentioned Mukunda <note>Another name for Krishna, meaning "Giver of Liberation"</note>.

<margin>9 verso</margin>

->**|| Om! Victory to the illustrious Ganapati ||**<-

> **Bowing deeply to the dark-hued Lord Krishna-chandra, who wears beautiful, shimmering yellow garments and is joyfully embraced by Radha as if by a flash of lightning against a rain cloud, the author named Narayana shall now speak the *Chamatkara Chintamani*. || 1 ||**

> **I worship Mukunda, whose color is as dark as a rain cloud, who is adorned with a tinkling web of small bells <note>original: "किं किणी" (kiṃkiṇī)</note> that create a joyful clamor, who wears shimmering yellow robes, and who is seen walking in the courtyard of Yashoda—though he remains unreachable even to the greatest of meditators. || 2 ||**

> **From the midst of the great ocean of four hundred thousand verses of astrological knowledge, I compose this treatise for the delight of the wise. It is rendered in the *Bhujangaprayata* meter <term>A poetic meter in Sanskrit literally meaning "the serpent's gait," known for its rhythmic, undulating flow</term>, characterized by excellent reasoning and concise language. || 3 ||**

Regarding the "tinkling" <note>original: "क्वणतः" (kvaṇataḥ)</note> of the bells: this refers to the sound produced by the "web of small bells," a collection of ringing bells creating a "clamor" or a rhythmic jingling. He is "endowed with" <note>original: "आढ्यं" (āḍhyam)</note> this sound. He wears "shimmering yellow robes," meaning he is dressed in beautiful yellow silk garments. He is "unreachable even to yogis," meaning difficult to attain through meditation alone, yet he is "walking" or moving about in "Yashoda's courtyard." I "worship" this Mukunda, who has the "dark color of a rain cloud." This means I remember Shri Krishna in my mind, who wanders in Yashoda's courtyard despite being beyond the reach of ascetics. This is an expression of the author's intense devotion. || 2 || 

Now, he distinguishes the purpose of the book. From the "great ocean" of the "four hundred thousand" verses of the science of "astrology" <term>Jyotisha; the traditional Hindu system of astrology and astronomy</term>, which is the source of "great awakening" or knowledge—having thoroughly reflected upon it—he creates this for the "delight" or happiness of "wise men" and "astrologers." It is "excellent," "beautiful" due to its logical reasoning and the elegance of its words, and written in "concise language," without excessive length <unclear>...</unclear>

<summary>This page introduces the "Chamatkara Chintamani," an astrological treatise. It includes traditional invocations to Ganesha and Krishna, identifying the author as Narayana and the commentator as Dharmeshvara, while establishing the text's goal: simplifying vast astrological knowledge for practitioners.</summary>
<keywords>Chamatkara Chintamani, Narayanacharya, Dharmeshvara, Krishna, Ganesha, astrology, Jyotisha, Lagna-kundali, Bhujangaprayata meter, Yashoda</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the introductory section of the manuscript, moving from the opening salutations to the technical definition of the poetic meter used and the foundational principles of the astrological readings that follow. It establishes why precise mathematical calculation is necessary before predicting a person's fate.</meta>

<warning>The left margin of the manuscript is slightly damaged or cut off, resulting in partial characters at the beginning of the first few lines. The overall image is clear but contains some water staining.</warning>

The definition of the *Bhujangaprayata* <note>original: "भुजंगप्रयात" (bhujangaprayāta); literally 'the crawl of a serpent.' It is a poetic meter in Sanskrit consisting of four 'ya' ganas (short-long-long feet), creating a rhythmic, undulating sound.</note> meter is formed by four repetitions of the 'ya' syllable group. In these poetic verses, I create a composition—the *Chamatkara Chintamani*—which is arranged beautifully like a collection of jewels. According to the rules of Pingala <note>Pingala was the ancient mathematician and author of the *Chhandas Shastra*, the foundational text on Sanskrit prosody.</note>, there should be no break or flaw in the meter. The first quarter of the verse should be read as "The four-hundred-thousand verses of Astrology contain many divisions." || 3 || Now, the author declares his intent: "I shall now describe the results of the planets in the various houses."

-> If the planets <note>original: "खेचराः" (khecarāḥ); literally 'sky-goers.'</note> were not established in the zodiac, what would be the point? <-
-> If they were not calculated to their true positions, what use would those planets be? <-
-> Without the clarity provided by the houses, what is the basis for prediction? <-
-> Therefore, I shall explain everything through their results. || 4 || <-

This means: what is the point of merely placing the planets in the zodiacal circle <note>original: "राशिमंडले" (rāśimaṇḍale)</note> if their positions are not calculated accurately? Similarly, if the planets are not determined through precise mathematical calculation <term>Drig-ganita; a system of astronomical calculation intended to align with observed positions</term>, they serve no purpose. Without the clarification of the "Houses" <term>Bhava; the twelve divisions of an astrological chart representing different areas of life like wealth, health, or marriage</term>, even the precise calculation of a planet's degree is of little use. Therefore, the true positions of the planets and the houses must be calculated first. Now, the reason or purpose for clarifying these planetary houses is stated: Since everything regarding a living being is known only through the "fruits" or results of these positions, I shall therefore speak and explain those results. || 4 || <note>The numbers and symbols "रः रॅरॅ रॅरॅ २३" likely refer to scribal notations or rhythmic counting for the meter.</note>

### || The Root Text ||

> The Sun situated in the first house <note>original: "तनुस्थो रवि" (tanustho ravi); 'Tanu' means body, referring to the first house which represents the self and physical form.</note> causes the body to be tall and slender. It brings mental distress arising from the circle of the wife, heirs, and kinsmen. The body is afflicted by an excess of wind and bile <term>Vata-Pitta; two of the three 'doshas' or humors in Ayurvedic medicine that govern bodily functions</term>. Such a person will constantly experience both decline and growth in their fortunes while wandering. || 1 || ऽ ऽ : ॥ ऽ ऽ : ॥

Here, the author first describes the results of the houses starting from the Ascendant <note>original: "लग्न" (lagna); the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>. For a person who has the Sun in the first house, it determines the appearance of their face and the stature of their body. They may experience mental suffering caused by their family group, including their wife and children. Their body is pained by bile agitated by the wind (the *Vata* and *Pitta* humors). Furthermore, that person will certainly experience the rise and fall of wealth—constant fluctuations in their financial state—while traveling or going to other lands. || 1 || 

Now, the result of the Sun in the second house: For a person whose birth chart shows the Sun <note>original: "भानु" (bhānu); another name for the Sun, meaning 'the Radiant One.'</note> in the house of wealth <note>original: "धने" (dhane); the second house, which governs speech, family, and accumulated assets.</note>, that person will be exceptionally fortunate and possess great destiny. They shall obtain the happiness of possessing elephants and horses <note>A traditional indicator of high social status and wealth.</note>. Furthermore, they will spend their own wealth on matters of virtue and religion <note>original: "धर्मादि" (dharmādi)</note>. The accumulation of wealth becomes the cause for...

<summary>This page defines the poetic meter of the text and emphasizes the necessity of precise mathematical astronomy before making astrological predictions. It begins the specific readings for the Sun's position, starting with the first and second houses.</summary>
<keywords>Bhujangaprayata meter, Pingala, Jyotisha, planetary positions, Vata-Pitta, Sun in the First House (Tanu Bhava), Sun in the Second House (Dhana Bhava), wealth and fortune</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the specific astrological predictions based on the placement of the Sun in various houses of a birth chart. The previous page concluded the general introduction and began the results for the Sun in the second house. This page completes the second house, covers the third and fourth, and begins the commentary for the fifth house.</meta>

In the family <note>original: "कुटुंवे" (kuṭumbe)</note>, specifically regarding one's own relatives, strife or quarreling arises. This is the intended meaning.

Furthermore, for the sake of profit, even bold or energetic actions become fruitless. One should know that due to excessive pride or ego <note>original: "स्वाहंकारेण" (svāhaṅkāreṇa)</note>, these efforts result in nothing. || 2 ||

### Now, the Results of the Sun in the Third House

When at the time of birth the Sun <note>original: "अर्हंशिः" (arhaṃśiḥ), likely a variant or scribe's error for "अंशुमाली" (aṃśumālī) or simply "सूर्य" (sūrya)</note> is in the third house, that person becomes exceedingly majestic. He manifests great valor and courage <note>original: "विक्रमं पराक्रमम्" (vikramaṃ parākramam)</note>. Yet, on the other hand, he suffers great distress or torment on account of his brothers. 

He becomes a traveler to holy places <note>original: "तीर्थचारी" (tīrthacārī)</note>. In the midst of battle, having destroyed his enemies, he receives welfare and blessings from the king. || 3 ||

### Now, the Results of the Fourth House

When the Sun <note>original: "दिनेशो" (dineśo); literally 'Lord of the Day'</note> is present in the fourth house—the house of happiness and comforts—the person gains a high degree of dignity and authority. Because of this position of respect, however, he experiences friction or disputes with his relatives and kin...

---

> If the Sun <note>original: "भानुः" (bhānuḥ)</note> is in the house of wealth, one becomes prominent in assemblies, yet does not find peace or happiness in the accumulation of riches. Strife arises in the family through the wife; actions taken for the sake of profit become fruitless. || 2 || 
> 
> When the Sun is in the third house at birth, one manifests great majesty and valor. At that time, one is tormented by brothers, yet becomes a traveler to holy sites. Through the destruction of enemies in battle, one gains favor from the king. || 3 ||
> 
> In the fourth house, the Sun makes a person highly dignified; here, one gains much, yet also faces disputes from relatives. One becomes a traveler, suffers a blow to his honor in the heat of battle with enemies, and his mind never finds peace. || 4 || 
> 
> If the Sun is in the house of children <note>original: "सुत स्थानगे" (suta sthānage)</note>, one suffers the loss of the first-born child. He is learned in the science of mantras and scriptures, but is prone to error. His mind is deceitful, and he should be cautious of diseases of the belly or heart. || 5 ||

---

...quarrels and disputes with them. Furthermore, in the conflict with enemies <note>original: "विपक्षा हवे" (vipakṣā have)</note>, he faces an incomparable blow to his honor or a broken spirit. He becomes a traveler, inclined to go to foreign lands. Additionally, his heart or mind never experiences peace; it is always anxious or troubled. This is the meaning. || 4 ||

### Now, the Results of the House of Children

When the Sun <note>original: "भास्करे" (bhāskare); literally 'The Light-Maker'</note> is in the fifth house, the house of children, a man suffers the grief caused by the death of his first-born offspring; this brings him great sorrow. 

He is one who possesses "the knowledge of mantras," meaning he is a knower of the sacred traditions <note>original: "आगमवेत्ता" (āgamavettā)</note> or a scholar of political ethics. He may be prone to error or carelessness <note>original: "प्रमादी" (pramādī)</note>, and is someone who gathers things with a sense of hesitation or reserve. 

Furthermore, his intellect is as sharp as the tip of *kusha* grass <note>original: "कुशाग्रातिसूक्ष्म" (kuśāgrātisūkṣma); a traditional metaphor for a very penetrating or "pointed" intelligence</note>, capable of discerning very subtle matters. However, he takes pleasure <note>original: "रतिः" (ratiḥ)</note> in using his speech to deceive or manipulate others.

<summary>This page details the astrological effects of the Sun in the 2nd through 5th houses, noting that while it brings majesty and intellect, it often causes family strife, loss of honor in battle, and grief regarding children.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis of the Sun's placement in the twelve houses of a birth chart. It begins by concluding the section on the fifth house (likely regarding health) and proceeds to detail the effects of the Sun in the sixth (enemies/debts), seventh (marriage/partnerships), eighth (longevity/mysteries), and ninth (fortune/spirituality) houses.</meta>

...arising from bad diseases <note>original: "दु रोगादिजा" (du rogādijā)</note>. One should understand this as the manifestation of a loathsome illness; this interpretation applies to all such cases. || 5 ||

### Now, the Results of the Sixth House

For an individual who has the Sun <note>original: "भास्करः" (bhāskaraḥ); literally 'The Light-Maker'</note> in the sixth house—the house of enemies <note>original: "रिपुभवने" (ripubhavane)</note>—he becomes a "destroyer of his foes," one who strikes down his enemies. However, he also incurs expenses <note>original: "व्ययम्" (vyayam)</note> on account of the king <note>referring to government taxes or legal penalties</note> or for the sake of his friends.

Furthermore, during travels or journeys, or in the midst of disputes, he experiences great sorrow <note>original: "निर्विषादं" (nirviṣādaṃ)</note> <margin>2</margin>. This means that while on the road, he may suffer distress due to being plundered by thieves. There is also a risk of misfortune or accidents <note>original: "विप्रत्तिः" (viprattiḥ)</note> involving his maternal family or caused by four-legged animals, such as horses. In this commentary, it is alternatively suggested that grief may arise from a fall from a vehicle or from the death of livestock within the maternal lineage. || 6 ||

### Now, the Results of the Seventh House

If the Sun, the Lord of the Day <note>original: "द्युनाथः" (dyunāthaḥ)</note>, is situated in the seventh house at the time of birth, that person suffers from a "burning" of their knowledge, distress regarding their wife <note>original: "स्त्री क्लेशः" (strī kleśaḥ)</note>, bodily pain, and constant anxiety. He becomes mentally agitated when he cannot obtain the things he desires. <insert>(suffering in his own body)</insert>

->॥ He who has the Sun in the sixth destroys his enemies, ॥<-
->॥ But incurs expenses from the king or from his friends. ॥<-
->॥ In the maternal family, or from four-legged animals, or on a journey, ॥<-
->॥ He experiences despair through disputes. ॥ 6 ॥<-
->॥ When the Lord of the Day is in the seventh for a man, ॥<-
->॥ There is distress of knowledge, bodily pain, and anxiety. ॥<-
->॥ There is gain in family matters, in buying and selling, ॥<-
->॥ Yet he is competitive and sometimes loses sleep. ॥ 7 ॥<-
->॥ Though he acts for dharma, he is a vessel for hardship; ॥<-
->॥ He may resort to foreign women, possessing little wealth or many wives. ॥<-
->॥ These sorrows are caused by thieves or by the lord of the ascendant; ॥<-
->॥ The Sun ordains another kind of suffering. ॥ 8 ॥<-

Regarding the accumulation of goods, in the matter of selling a purchased item, there is only a meager <term>gain (ladhiḥ)<gloss>a small profit</gloss></term>. Additionally, due to rivalry or competition with an opponent, he sometimes finds no rest or sleep. || 7 ||

### Now, the Results of the Eighth House

When the Sun is in the eighth house—here called the house of the mind—it makes a person behave in three ways: as a debauchee, a merchant, or as someone exceedingly shrewd. Therefore, it makes him a "vessel of hardship," meaning one who suffers.

<margin>of</margin> He may follow women in foreign lands, or perhaps he enjoys the company of low-born women. Due to thieves, legal disputes, or laziness, he faces a lack of wealth and the misery of misfortune. This distress is mostly born of sensual desires related to women. || 8 || <gloss>vi</gloss>

### Now, the Results of the Ninth House

When the Lord of the Day is in the house of fortune <note>the ninth house</note>, that man, though perhaps lacking controlled thoughts, attains high status through his penance or the excellence of his actions. Through his conduct, he attains greatness and worship. Even if he is a hypocrite <note>original: "दांभिकत्वे" (dāmbhikattve)</note>, he will be respected by the public. Furthermore, with his siblings...

...is the cause of gain; because of that, he may be distressed. Since wickedness prevails, his mind never attains peace or a cessation of worry. Here, by the word "angle" <note>original: "कोण" (koṇa); referring to the trine houses: 1st, 5th, and 9th</note>, the three trines are to be understood. || 9 ||

### Now, the Results of the Tenth House

For a man born with the Sun <note>original: "भानु" (bhānu) or "सूर्य" (sūrya)</note> situated in the tenth house, it brings suffering and pain arising from disease to his mother. To expand: his power and valor will be king-like, and he will be a successful achiever of his tasks, like an excellent **king**. However, during the Sun's transit <note>original: "संक्रमे" (saṅkrame); refers to the Sun moving from one zodiac sign to another</note>, there may be separation from loved ones—friends, sons, wife, and others—leading to distress and exhaustion. This is the essential meaning. || 10 ||

### Now, the Results of the Sun in the Eleventh House

If the Sun is in the house of gain <note>the 11th house is traditionally associated with income and the fulfillment of desires</note>, that person acquires wealth from the "king's gate" <note>meaning through government service or royal favor</note>. Through the authority of the royal seal, he obtains various blessings, such as elephants, horses, and great prosperity. Furthermore, his enemies fall into the fire of his majesty. As it is said regarding the sight of authority...

---

->॥ When the Lord of the Day is in a trine, his mind attains no peace <-
->from worry due to wickedness. || 9 || Through penance, <-
->the Sun acts as a destroyer of obstacles; however, he suffers <-
->among siblings in battle. || **9** || Now, for the Sun in the <-
->tenth house <note>original: "मेसूरणे" (mesūraṇe); a Sanskritized form of the Greek 'Mesouranema', meaning the mid-heaven or 10th house</note>, it grants success like a king to a man, <-
->but it extends pain and exhaustion to his mother. <-
->During transits, there is separation from those **hard** to part with. || 10 || **When** <-
->**the Sun** transits there, gain comes from the king’s gate <-
->through the authority of the royal seal. Enemies fall <-
->into the fire of his majesty; he gains various riches, <-
->but there is sorrow **for children**. || 11 || The Sun in the twelfth house <-
->causes eye defects, but in the assembly of enemies, <-
->he attains the Goddess of Victory. <-
->His physical pain dissolves, but there is loss <-
->regarding paternal uncles and in foreign lands. || 12 || Auspiciousness. ||<-

---

...this is the sentiment. Likewise, one should understand the connection to the sorrow regarding children <note>original: "गो देवा" (go devā); here used idiomatically for offspring</note>. || 11 || 

### Now, the Results of the Sun in the Twelfth House

The Sun situated in the twelfth house causes eye defects, such as dim-sightedness <note>original: "मंद दृष्टि त्वं" (manda dṛṣṭi tvaṃ)</note>, and brings troubles to paternal uncles. However, in the battle with enemies, he attains the Goddess of Victory <note>original: "जय श्री" (jaya śrī)</note>. "Position" means staying in one place; in "half-regions" <note>interpreted here as transit zones or foreign lands</note> or on the road, loss and damage occur. 

Furthermore, physical pain "dissolves," meaning it is destroyed. This should be interpreted according to one's experience on the path. Regarding the paternal uncle and the road, it means the death of the uncle or loss due to being plundered, etc. || 12 || 

### Thus ends the results of the Sun in the houses beginning with the Ascendant.

# Now, the Results of the Moon in the Twelve Houses, Starting with the Ascendant

**If the Moon** is in Taurus, Cancer, or Aries <note>original: "gāu, kulīra, aja"; the Moon is traditionally exalted in Taurus and rules Cancer</note> and is located in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> of a noble person, he becomes an overseer of wealth and finance, filled with joy and beauty, and endowed with happiness and skill. In any other zodiac sign in the Ascendant, the Moon makes the man irreligious, of a frail body, lacking in strength, poor, dull-witted, mute, or hearing-impaired. **|| 1 ||** When the **Moon**, the "cool-rayed one," is in the second house <note>original: "vasula-sthāna"; the house of wealth and family</note>, there is great comfort for the body. He experiences the various delights and playful charms of women, and finds happiness among his own kinsmen. He attains great intelligence and favor at the "king’s gate" <note>meaning official or government recognition</note>. Furthermore, such a man is so handsome that even a celestial nymph would fall under his influence, let alone an ordinary woman. **|| 2 ||** When the Moon is in the house of **valor** <note>the 3rd house, "Vikrama-bhava"</note>...

---

-> ॥ If the Moon is in Taurus, Cancer, or Aries, it makes one an overseer of wealth, <-
-> full of beauty and joy. Otherwise, it makes a man irreligious, <-
-> with a weak body, poor, dull, and hearing-impaired. **|| 1 ||** <-
-> When the Moon is in the second house, there is gain of grain and bodily <-
-> comfort, and delight among women. In the **family**, <-
-> love arises; he is worthy of honor by men, and even a celestial nymph <-
-> is drawn to his sight. **|| 2 ||** With the Moon in the third house, he attains much wealth; <-
-> he becomes a practitioner of penance even though he is delighted by a **beautiful woman**. <-
-> Why worry? His happiness regarding siblings is certain; <-
-> he is bright with majesty and the banner of righteousness. **|| 3 ||** <-
-> When in the house of relatives, he is born to be honored by kinsmen <-
-> and is always an official in the king’s court. <-
-> In the first part of life, such happiness is not seen, <-
-> but later he is satisfied by sons and wives. <-
-> || It comes truly. **|| 4 ||** <-

---

...with the Moon in the third house, this man attains wealth. Even while being charmed by a beautiful woman, he remains a practitioner of penance; he is bright with majesty and adorned with fame, like one carrying the banner of righteousness. Why should he worry? He will surely obtain all happiness arising from his brothers and siblings. **|| 3 ||** When the Moon (born of the sage Atri) is in the **fourth house** <note>the house of "Bandhu" or relatives/home</note>, the person finds happiness with his kinsmen. He receives complete satisfaction from his group of sons and wives. He is always an administrator of all affairs at the king's gate. In the first part of his life, he does not attain such status, but later he becomes one who possesses authority and a commanding gaze. **|| 4 ||** If, at the time of birth, the Moon—the Lord of the Stars—is in the **fifth house**, he grants the joy of children, a pure mind, and the gain of jewels; he bestows a brilliance like that of a lord of the earth. Furthermore, through money-lending or long-term business, he finds his vocation or enterprise...

<summary>This page describes the astrological influences of the Moon when placed in the first four houses of a birth chart, noting effects on wealth, physical appearance, social status, and family life. It emphasizes that the Moon's placement in specific signs like Taurus or Cancer significantly improves the outcomes for the first house.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the analysis of the Moon's (Chandra's) influence as it moves through the astrological houses, specifically covering the fifth through the beginning of the ninth house. It follows the previous discussion of the Moon in the fourth house.</meta>

...from these sources, many and various profits will arise. **|| 5 ||** When the Moon is in the **house of enemies** <term>Ripau: the 6th house, governing enemies, debts, and illness</term>, one shines with splendor even through royal conflict. He remains radiant and beautiful through his own brilliance. His enemies are conquered; though they may try to rise again, they remain powerless in his presence. Like a mother's nature, so is his character. **|| 6 ||** In the **seventh house**, the "cool-rayed one" <note>original: "shita rashmi," a poetic epithet for the Moon</note> grants happiness through a wife and women. He attains wealth through **commerce** <note>original: "vanijya"</note> and trade in foreign lands. However, if the birth occurs during the **dark fortnight** <term>Krishna-paksha: the waning phase of the moon</term>, he has a lustful heart and seeks pleasure with women, yet he lacks true happiness; he becomes weak and is overcome by his adversaries. **|| 7 ||**

### Now, the Moon in the Eighth House

In his house, there is a gathering of **physicians** <note>original: "bhaishaji bhishajam"; this suggests a life plagued by illness requiring constant medical attention</note>. There is always the boiling of medicinal water.

---

> || When the Moon is the lord of the birth star and occupies the **fifth house**, it grants only satisfaction through **children** <note>original: "santana"</note>. It provides a pure intellect, the gain of gems, and land acquired through **usury** <term>Kusida: the practice of lending money at interest</term> and various other means. **|| 5 ||**
> In the **sixth house**, he shines even in conflict with kings. Even if the Moon is waning, his enemies are conquered again and again. In his presence, they are rendered lusterless and lose their splendor, yet his own character remains steady. **|| 6 ||**
> Attached to his wife, when the "cool-rayed one" is in the **seventh house**, he becomes wealthy through trade. During the **dark fortnight**, however, he is merely a seeker of pleasure among women, greedy of heart, emaciated, and overcome by his opponents. **|| 7 ||**
> A gathering of physicians is found in his house, where they often boil medicinal waters. Fear of great diseases, water-born illnesses, or drowning exists. The Moon in the eighth house is a creator of distress and hardship. **|| 8 ||**

---

If he is overcome by fever or other ailments, many physicians will be present to ward them off. Sometimes there are great diseases or "kingly diseases" <note>original: "raja-rogadayah," often referring to chronic conditions like tuberculosis</note> caused by water, or fears of drowning. There may be suffering caused by imprisonment or bonds created by wicked people. The Moon <note>original: "shashi"</note>, where <unclear>m</unclear> exists in this house, is indeed a source of hardship. **|| 8 ||**

### Now, the Results of the Ninth House

For a person whose Moon is in the **house of penance** <term>Tapo-bhava: the 9th house, associated with grace, fortune, and philosophy</term>, those of the priestly and warrior classes, as well as bards who sing praises, will extol his virtues. Because of his youth, wealth, character, and conduct, he is considered highly fortunate...

...it truly comes to pass. Likewise, there is comfort in the body, courage, and a mature, resolute mind. One becomes beautiful like the Moon. Or, occasionally, there is a sense of religious honor <note>original: "dharma-manam"</note>; this is the established connection. **|| 9 ||**

# Now, the Results for the Tenth House

When the Moon—born of the ocean <note>original: "Abdhisu-te," an epithet for the Moon based on the myth of the churning of the ocean</note>—is the celestial body situated in the **tenth house** <term>Dashama-bhava: the house of career, social status, and public life</term>, the person performs righteous deeds and earns merit. He receives happiness from his relatives. Even among rulers and high-ranking officials, he possesses power. He attains great lordship and becomes exceedingly beloved among young women. However, regarding the first-born child in the horoscope <note>original: "praro-jatake," referring to the first progeny</note>, he experiences only a small amount of happiness. **|| 10 ||**

# Now, the Results for the Eleventh House

With the Moon situated in the **eleventh house** <term>Labha-bhava: the house of gains, profits, and aspirations</term>, one obtains a position of authority and great recognition from the King or government. Success comes through a steady progression.

->In the inner quarters of the home, women and wealth<-
->remain steady for those born during the day.<-
->Great fortune truly arises from youth,<-
->along with bodily comfort and courage. || 18 ||<-
->There is happiness from relatives when the planet is in the tenth,<-
->as well as righteous deeds and merit. From rulers,<-
->and among young women, there is great favor and glory.<-
->For the first-born, however, it creates little happiness. || 10 ||<-
->Through the Moon in the house of gains, recognition<-
->and authority from the King are granted in due order.<-
->In the inner chambers, there is rest; and through<-
->actions or a daughter, there is the gain of objects. || 11 ||<-
->When the Moon is in the twelfth, one always worries<-
->about enemies and eye troubles. Success is not<-
->found in auspicious events. From paternal uncles and<-
->mothers, there is distress in the heart. One finds<-
->little pleasure in what is usually dear. || 12 ||<-

This means that within the inner quarters of the home, women and riches become stable and lasting. Furthermore, there is gain from many different types of objects. There is a possibility of a daughter being born who may be difficult or have some physical irregularity <note>original: "vaikriti," which can mean a deformity or a change in natural state</note>. **|| 11 ||**

# Now, the Results for the Twelfth House

If the Moon is in the **twelfth house** <term>Dvadasha-bhava: the house of loss, expenses, and liberation</term>, one must consider the anxieties arising from the fear of enemies or disorders of the eyes. Success is not easily achieved in auspicious matters like marriage. One might give away wealth to family members. Due to paternal uncles <note>original: "pitrivyadi," referring to the father’s brothers and their families</note> or from the mother’s side of the family, there is sorrow and a heavy heart. One may feel a lack of affection for those usually dear, or experience a sense of detachment from them. Finally, it means that one does not attain the desires of the heart. **|| 12 ||**

## || Thus ends the results of the Moon situated in the twelve houses. May there be auspiciousness. ||

# Now, the Results of Mars in the Ascendant and other Houses

**When Mars is in the Ascendant** <term>Lagna: the first house, representing the self and physical body</term>: One experiences fear of punishments or iron <note>original: "daṇḍa-loha-nibhītiḥ," likely referring to injuries from weapons or legal penalties</note>. There is harm to the spouse and others, the loss of wife and sons, and afflictions to the head and eyes. Regarding the "ripening" or final results of one's actions <note>original: "vipāke," the karmic result</note>, one should always provide a remedy, just as one handles a great disturbance or agitation. This is explained further in the sequence. Would such a person be as industrious as a lion <note>original: "kesarī," literally a lion; here implying a bold or restless nature</note>? This is the intended meaning. **|| 1 ||**

**Now, the results for Mars in the Second House** <term>Dvitīya-bhāva: the house of wealth and family</term>: For one who has Mars <note>original: "Angārako," the glowing coal, an epithet for Mars</note> in the house of wealth, even if wealth is acquired or present, there is little to no benefit for the family or kinsmen. This is compared to a **monkey** <note>original: "markaṭo"</note> who finds a necklace made of pearls or other gems and puts it around his neck; he guards it and protects it, yet he is a miser with that wealth. The meaning is that those jewels are never actually used for the benefit of the family. Furthermore, if such a person is defeated in a debate, he will likely not face his opponent for a dispute again.

<margin>|| Of Mars ||</margin>
->|| When Mars is in the Ascendant, there is fear of punishment and iron. <-
->The mind suffers; is he a lion? Second: harm to the spouse, <-
->pain in the head and eyes. In the ripening of results, there is always <-
->an affliction. || 1 || For one who has Mars, what is the use of <-
->family or wealth being present? Even when wealth is obtained, what of it? <-
->Just as a monkey protects a necklace, he does not <-
->face his opponent again after being defeated in debate. || 2 ||<- <margin>Rest: || 2 ||</margin>
->Whence comes the strength of arms? Whence comes family fortune? <-
->If Mars is not present for men in the third house, <-
->the pain of siblings is like ashes; their penance <-
->becomes a matter of ridicule. || 3 || <-
->When the Son of the Earth is in the <-
->fourth house, are the planets favorable to people? <-
->Happiness from friends and dharma is not to be expected; <-
->yet, one might receive land from a king through mercy. || 4 || Fortune. ||<-

### Now, the Results for the Third House
From where would the strength of the arms <note>original: "bāhu-vīryam," physical prowess</note> come? Or from where would family fortune—wealth earned by one's own hands—arise? The "pain of siblings" <note>original: "sahottha-vyathā"</note>—who can say how it happens? No one can say. Furthermore, how could such people perform penance without being ridiculed by others? This is the result for men when Mars <note>original: "Mangalo"</note> is not situated in the third house <term>Sahaja-bhāva: the house of siblings and courage</term>. **|| 3 ||**

### Now, the Results for the Fourth House
When the **Son of the Earth** <note>original: "Bhū-sutaḥ," another name for Mars</note> is situated in the fourth house <term>Turya-bhāva: the house of home, mother, and lands</term>, of what use is it if other planets are favorable or giving good results? Therefore, happiness derived from friends, dharma, relatives, and one's home or mother is not to be contemplated <note>meaning: it is unlikely to manifest</note>. However, by the mercy, strength, or favor of a "Protector of the Earth" (a King), one may obtain land, villages, and the like. **|| 4 ||**

<summary>This page details the astrological effects of Mars (Bhauma) when positioned in the first four houses of a horoscope, using metaphors like a lion's industry and a monkey's hoarding to describe character traits and financial behaviors.</summary>
<keywords>Mars, Bhauma, astrology, first house, second house, third house, fourth house, wealth, family, siblings, kingly favor</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the analysis of Mars (Kuja/Bhauma) through the astrological houses, moving from the results in the 5th house through the 8th house. It maintains the structure of a root verse followed by an explanatory commentary.</meta>

<page-num>5</page-num>
<header>5 || Chamatkara Chintamani ||</header>

**Now, the results for Mars in the Fifth House** <term>Panchama-bhāva: the house of children, intelligence, and past merits</term>: When Mars is in the fifth, the digestive fire <term>Jāṭharāgni: the internal fire responsible for digestion</term> becomes very strong and powerful. Even if there is some sin or vice present <note>original: "kilviṣe pāpepi"</note>, the mind remains very studious and diligent. However, one's own wealth is destroyed like milk <note>original: "dugdhavannashyate," implying it vanishes quickly or spoils</note>. This means the person will always remain anxious. Furthermore, Mars alone in this position destroys the offspring as soon as they are born. **|| 5 ||**

**Now, the results for Mars in the Sixth House** <term>Shashtha-bhāva: the house of enemies, debts, and diseases</term>: When Mars <note>original: "Angārako," the glowing coal</note> is in the sixth house, which is the house of enemies, even powerful and strong enemies—though incited by ministers—cannot stand in the theater of war. They flee the battle. This means they lose the capacity for strategic thought and judgment. Consequently, the person remains naturally happy. On the other hand, one's wealth and property may dissolve or be destroyed. Also, one may encounter many great obstacles. **|| 6 ||**

**Now, the results for Mars in the Seventh House** <term>Saptama-bhāva: the house of marriage and partnerships</term>: Mars in the seventh house of passion causes frequent defeats and pain at the hands of rivals and litigants through oppression and harassment.

---

> -> Mars in the fifth: the digestive fire is strong; he alone destroys the child as soon as it is born. At that time, though the mind is studious, wealth is destroyed like milk, causing constant worry. || 5 || In the sixth, even powerful enemies incited [against him] do not stand in battle. Though happy, the maternal uncle's wealth is lost, and he encounters many obstacles. || 6 || By a marriage that brings distress rather than joy, one is never free. He causes total ruin through rivals; Mars in the seventh is a "spouse-slayer." || 7 || What can other auspicious planets do if the Son of the Earth is in the eighth house? Even a friend acts like an enemy, and despite great efforts made for the King, obstacles arise in everything. || 8 || Sri Durga [Salutation]. <-

---

He is a "spouse-slayer" <note>original: "dampatīghna," meaning it causes the loss or death of the partner</note>. Specifically, if Mars is in the seventh house of a man's birth chart, it destroys the wife; in a woman's chart, it destroys the husband. Furthermore, due to a marriage that is determined by such an alignment <note>original: "pāṇigraheṇa," the taking of the hand</note>, one experiences great distress. Because of business motives or the fear of disputes, the person may reside in foreign lands for a long time, abandoning their home. **|| 7 ||**

**Now, the results for Mars in the Eighth House** <term>Ashtama-bhāva: the house of death, longevity, and sudden changes</term>: If the Son of the Earth <term>Bhūmisūnu: an epithet for Mars, born of the Earth</term> is in the eighth house, what can other auspicious planets <note>original: "kheṭāḥ," moving stars/planets</note> accomplish? Because Mars gives such wicked results here, he obstructs the good influence of other planets. Even a friend <note>original: "sakha," companion</note> begins to behave like an enemy. Even when great effort is made, obstacles <note>original: "upasarge," disturbances or afflictions</note> occur, resulting in the ruin of the work one has started. **|| 8 ||**

<summary>This page describes the astrological effects of Mars in the 5th through 8th houses, highlighting themes of strong digestion, the loss of children, victory over enemies, marital discord/widowhood, and the obstruction of good fortune by obstacles.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the astrological study of the planet Mars (Bhauma/Mangala), following the previous page's discussion of the fifth through eighth houses. Here, the text concludes the analysis of Mars’s influence by covering the ninth through twelfth houses. The format remains consistent: a prose commentary followed by the poetic root verses.</meta>

<page-num>15</page-num>

# Now, Mars in the Ninth House <term>Navama-bhāva: the house of fortune, dharma, and father</term>
When Mars <note>original: "Mangala"</note> is situated in the ninth house, the house of penance <note>original: "Tapo-bhāva"</note>, he makes that man "wealthy through fortune" <note>original: "Bhāgya-vitta," meaning his riches come through luck or destiny</note>. He makes the person very fierce and radiant. Furthermore, because he is "very fierce" <note>original: "Mahogra," implying a sharp or intense temperament</note>, the person’s intellect becomes extremely cruel. This individual will not have an "elder" <note>original: "Ādimah," first-born</note>, a brother-in-law <note>original: "Vanitā bhrātā"</note>, or even a "uterine brother" <note>original: "Sodhara," a brother from the same womb</note>. In the "time of fruition" <note>original: "Vipāke," the time when astrological results manifest</note> for his valorous efforts, there will be "paltry gain" <note>original: "Tuccha-lābha," a very small or insignificant profit</note>. **|| 18 ||**

# Now, the results for Mars in the Tenth House <term>Dashama-bhāva: the house of career, status, and public life</term>
If Mars is in the tenth house, which is the "middle house" <term>Madhya-bhāva: the zenith or mid-heaven</term>, then what need is there for any other auspiciousness in his family? <note>This is a play on words: "Mangala" means both "Mars" and "Auspiciousness." The author suggests that Mars alone in this high position provides all the "mangala" (blessings) the family needs.</note> This is an exaggeration to show his power here. He will be "served by people," meaning he will have many servants. Therefore, the person becomes successful in his tasks through his own efforts, and he achieves this "quickly through others." Even if he is born into a lowly family, he is like a "second lion" <note>original: "Dvitiya-kanthīrava," implying he possesses lion-like power and majesty regardless of his origins</note>.

<margin>In this world, the pair is praised. 15</margin>

> Being in the house of penance, Mars makes one very fierce in intellect but wealthy through fortune. He makes one radiant. There will be no elder brother or even a sibling. Why expect valor? In the end, the gain is small. **|| 18 ||** If Mars is in the middle house, what use are other blessings to the family? He is served by people. He is self-made and achieves success quickly. Though humble, is he not like a second lion? **|| 19 ||** Mars in the house of gain <term>Lābha-bhāva: the 11th house</term> troubles the children and enemies. He is harsh-faced but stands before others with great majesty. Wealth increases through cattle or vehicles. Sometimes there is a loss of wealth for the sake of a daughter or through other means. **|| 19 ||** <note>The numbering in the manuscript repeats 19 and then jumps.</note> When Mars is in the twelfth, the house of loss, he acts as a destroyer of wealth and a striker of the father. He causes wounds from weapon-strikes. One should contemplate the sorrow caused by false rumors, fear of thieves, or quarrels with hunters. **|| 12 ||**

Even if born into a [humble] lineage <note>original: "Nakulodbhava," perhaps a reference to a lower clan or even a mongoose (nakula) vs. a lion</note>, he possesses the prowess of a lion. This is the meaning. **|| 19 ||**

# Now, Mars in the Eleventh House <term>Ekādasha-bhāva: the house of gains and social circles</term>
Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> in the house of gain <note>original: "Lābha-ga"</note> troubles the children, enemies, and brothers. Furthermore, he is "harsh-faced" <note>original: "Durmukha," meaning foul-mouthed or prone to outbursts</note>, yet because of his great "majesty and splendor" <note>original: "Pratāpa-tejas"</note>, he becomes an impressive speaker whom people wish to see. Wealth comes through "cattle, riches, or vehicles" such as chariots, elephants, or horses. However, "loss of wealth" <note>original: "Dravya-hānita," financial depletion</note> or the loss of famous assets may occur "once" <note>original: "Sakṛt," a single time</note>. **|| 21 ||**

# Now, Mars in the Twelfth House <term>Dvādasha-bhāva: the house of loss, liberation, and expenses</term>
Mars in the twelfth house, the house of expenditure <note>original: "Vyaya-bhāva"</note>, causes the "destruction of wealth" <note>original: "Arthasya dravyasya nāshakam"</note>. He is also a "striker" <note>original: "Ghātaka"</note> who causes injury through weapon-strikes or wounds. This person may be a "killer of enemies." There are "false rumors" <note>original: "Mṛshā vādantī," lying reports</note>, fear of thieves, or "quarrels" <note>original: "Kali"</note>. One should also consider the "sorrow caused by hunters" <note>original: "Pāradhī-hetu-duḥkham," which could imply trouble from low-life characters or a specific historical caste of hunters</note>. **|| 12 ||**

-> Thus ends the results of the houses as told for Mars. || 12 || <-

# Now, the Results of Mercury in the First and Subsequent Houses <term>Bhāva-phalam: the fruits or effects of the houses</term>

Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note>, when situated in the first house <term>Lagna: the ascendant or physical body</term>, wipes away or destroys any misfortune <note>original: "Arishta," meaning planetary evils or ill-luck</note> caused by other planets. Such individuals possess superior intellects and their bodies are like refined gold <note>original: "Chāmīkarībhūta-dehā," meaning glowing with health and radiance</note>. They are experts in the medical arts <note>original: "Chikitsā-vido," skilled in healing</note>. They possess the quality of eloquent speech and professional conduct <note>original: "Vaikharī-vṛtti," referring here to skill in writing and business dealings</note>. They take pleasure in performing charitable acts, such as building wells or water reservoirs. Or, it may be said that by relying on their professional speech, they find success in whatever they set their minds to. **|| 1 ||**

# Now, the Results for the Second House <term>Dvitīya-bhāva: the house of wealth and speech</term>

If Mercury is in the second house, the house of wealth <note>original: "Dhane"</note>, the person is very intelligent. He possesses "strength of arm" <note>original: "Bāhu-tejā," meaning he is powerful or valorous</note>. In a gathering of scholars <note>original: "Sabhā-saṅgataḥ"</note>, he shines like the sun <note>original: "Vāsarāvabhāsate," literally "shining like the day"</note>. This is a metaphorical ornament of speech. Just as a bee <note>original: "Aṣṭapado" or "Shatpada," meaning the six-legged one</note> is capable of extracting the essence of all enjoyments, so too is this person. He is a great giver, like a wish-fulfilling tree <term>Kalpavṛkṣa: a mythological tree that grants all desires</term>. How do the wise <note>original: "Budhaiḥ," the learned ones</note> describe this? **|| 2 ||**

> || Mercury in the body <note>the 1st house</note> wipes away other misfortunes; the intellect
> is superior and they possess eloquent speech. Their
> bodies are like gold and they are experts in medicine, though
> they are difficult to treat <note>when they themselves fall ill</note>. || 1 || In the house of wealth, one is intelligent
> and possesses powerful arms; he shines in the assembly like the sun.
> He is extremely generous, like a wish-fulfilling tree,
> and scholars say he enjoys life like a bee. || 2 ||
> In the third house, he is friendly with merchants, of a refined
> character, and through his intellect, he gains mastery over those
> who are difficult to control. He is modest,
> enjoys many pleasures, and is surrounded by younger brothers
> like a tree is supported by creepers. || 3 || In the fourth house, Mercury
> moves <note>functions</note> as a charming friend, granting a special abundance of land
> and wealth. He becomes a writer, or is written about <note>famous</note>;
> others say he does not receive paternal wealth. || 4 ||

# Now, the Results for the Third House <term>Tṛtīya-bhāva: the house of siblings and courage</term>

When Mercury is in the third house, he follows the lifestyle and business practices of merchants <note>original: "Vaṇijām"</note> and those who live by their wits. He is devoted to helping others through friendship and affection. Being consistently modest and well-behaved, he uses his own intelligence to gain "mastery" <note>original: "Vashitvam"</note> over people who are otherwise uncontrollable. Surrounded by his younger brothers <note>original: "Anujaiḥ"</note>, he is like a sturdy tree entwined by creepers. Among them, he enjoys many worldly pleasures. After these enjoyments, he may eventually renounce <note>original: "Sanyaset," to take up the life of an ascetic</note> these worldly matters. Alternatively, when faced with a crisis, he should enjoy these subjects while supported by his children, as if by creepers. **|| 3 ||**

-> || This concludes the results for Mercury in the Third House <note>original: "Sahaja-sthāna"</note> || <-

# Now, the Results for the Fourth House <term>Caturtha-bhāva: the house of home, mother, and fixed assets</term>

If Mercury, the son of the Moon <note>original: "Chandraja"</note>, moves through the fourth house, then the individual possesses excellent friends. Existing without enemies, he may become a servant or official of the king, specifically a high-ranking scribe or a general secretary <note>original: "sarvādhyaya-lekhaka"</note> who is known for beautiful handwriting. Alternatively, some say that under this planetary period, one’s livelihood depends on managing the records and writings of others. This is confirmed by the words issued from the mouths of the sages: <unclear>7</unclear> <unclear>7</unclear>. Furthermore, there may be an absence of paternal wealth; meaning, the individual does not obtain riches inherited from his father <note>original: "paitṛka-dhana"</note>. **|| 4 ||** This concludes the results for Mercury in the fourth house.

# Now, the Results for the Fifth House <term>Pañcama-bhāva: the house of children, intelligence, and past merits</term>

When Mercury, the offspring of Rohini <note>original: "Rohiṇeyapatyam," an epithet for Mercury</note>, is situated in the fifth house, in the early years of life, pregnancies resulting in sons may not remain stable <note>original: "putra-garbho na tiṣṭhet," implying miscarriage or early loss</note>, or if a child is born, he may pass away. He may have few children. However, the individual possesses great intelligence <note>original: "medhā-buddhi"</note> and is highly skilled in the acquisition of wealth.

->|| In the early stage of life, pregnancies resulting in sons do not last<-
->The intellect is the provider of wealth. Mercury acts as a sustainer<-
->When Mercury is in the fifth, how much knowledge is there?<-
->Because of the influence of deceptive spells <note>original: "kaitavasyābhicārāt," referring to malefic influences or trickery affecting the house of children</note>. || 5 ||<-
->Conflict with people, the restraint of enemies, the awakening of ascetics<-
->And the blockage of the bodily winds. When Mercury is present<-
->Wealth is handled immediately; one becomes a creator of wealth through power<-
->occurring in the sixth house. || 6 || Shri Krishna.<-

The individual is clever in earning money. Regarding malefic influences <note>original: "sābhicāram," referring to sorcery or malevolent spells</note> used for harming or killing, the experts in horoscopy <note>original: "horā-vidbhir"</note> state that Mercury provides an immeasurable amount of protection or strength. **|| 5 ||**

# Now, the Results for the Sixth House <term>Ṣaṣṭha-bhāva: the house of enemies, health, and service</term>

When Mercury is placed in the sixth house, there is conflict with people or quarrels with others. There is a restraint of enemies, meaning they are rendered uncooperative or kept at a distance. Regarding the bodily winds <note>original: "anilānām," referring to the Vata dosha</note>, there is a lack of obstruction, which indicates the person may have an "excessive digestive tract" <note>original: "bahu-koṣṭha," likely referring to digestive issues or gas</note>. For ascetics <note>original: "yatīnām," those who have renounced worldly life</note>, an awakening of spiritual knowledge occurs. Furthermore, such a person handles treasures and various forms of wealth immediately. By the strength of his own capability, he becomes a generator of wealth. **|| 6 ||** This concludes the results for the sixth house.

# Now, the Results for the Seventh House <term>Saptama-bhāva: the house of marriage and partnerships</term>

If Mercury, the son of the Moon <note>original: "Shītagoshuta," literally 'son of the cold-rayed one'</note>, has risen in the seventh house, the individual faces a woman...

During the time of sexual enjoyment <note>original: "rati-samaye"</note>, he possesses meager and short-lived virility and semen. Likewise, the splendor of his body, which is like gold, is such that the wealth or beauty of women cannot imitate or match it; the meaning is that he shall be capable of surpassing them. The intent is that his beauty and grace <note>original: "kānti-lakṣmī"</note> will be supreme. Mercury, when not associated with other planets, produces all these results. **|| 7 ||**

## || Now, the Results of Mercury in the Eighth House <term>Aṣṭama-bhāva: the house of longevity, transformation, and hidden things</term> ||

If the Prince <note>original: "Rājaputra," an epithet for Mercury</note>, the son of the Moon, is situated in the "Hole" <term>Randhra: a technical name for the eighth house</term>, men live for a hundred years <note>original: "śata-jīvinaḥ"</note>. They are well-known and famous both here in their own country and in foreign lands. Such men obtain a hoard of wealth <note>original: "nidhānaṃ dravya-sañcayaṃ"</note> through transactions, commerce, or dealings with kings. They find delight and happiness in their hearts through the play and dalliance arising from young women. **|| 8 ||**

## || Now, the Results of Mercury in the Ninth House <term>Navama-bhāva: the house of fortune, dharma, and father</term> ||

> The son of the Moon <note>original: "Sutaḥ sītagoḥ"</note> in the seventh house produces meager virility during enjoyment. 
> Mercury, if unassociated, makes the body's splendor like gold, such that women’s wealth cannot match it. **|| 7 ||**
> When the Prince is in the eighth house, men live for a hundred years and become famous in foreign lands. 
> They obtain treasures through transactions with kings and find pleasure and love in young women. **|| 8 ||**
> With Mercury in the house of Dharma, one becomes virtuous, very wise, and a consecrated performer of the Soma sacrifice <note>original: "dīkṣitaḥ"</note> or one who bathes in the celestial river <note>original: "svardhunī-snātakaḥ," referring to the Ganges</note>.
> He brings light to his lineage like the sun, possesses more majesty than a king, and is a hinderer of the wicked. **|| 9 ||**

If Mercury is in the ninth house <note>original: "Dharmage"</note>, the individual is very wealthy, virtuous, and a consecrated performer of the Soma sacrifice <term>Somayājī: one who has performed the prestigious Soma ritual</term>. He is a "bather in the celestial river," meaning he is devoted to the vow of bathing in the Ganges. Like the sun, he brings light to his lineage, illuminating everything. Because of his majesty, he is superior even to a king. He is a hinderer or a destroyer of the "foul-mouthed"—that is, of wicked people. **|| 9 ||**

## || Now, the Results of Mercury in the Tenth House <term>Daśama-bhāva: the house of career, social status, and father's influence</term> ||

If Mercury is in the tenth house <note>original: "Karmaṇi"</note>, one obtains the father's wealth in a special way. He is worthy of honor by the people and maintains ethics...

# Om! The Curiosity of Lakola-Hala 2

<margin>Ke</margin>

->The one whose form is an unbroken circle, by whom the moving and unmoving universe is pervaded,<-
->To that Guru, who revealed that state to me, I offer my salutations. 1<-
->The Guru is Brahma, the Guru is Vishnu, the Guru is the Great Lord Shiva;<-
->The Guru is indeed the Supreme Absolute. To that Guru, I offer my salutations. 2<-

...[for this] reason. || Through his grace and other factors, he possesses the favor and kindness of the planets. || In a prosperous kingdom <note>original: "su-rājye"</note> or under a good government, he is a person skilled in worldly affairs and management. || He should speak in a measured way <note>original: "mitaṃ saṃvadet"</note>; he should not be loquacious. Yet, what he receives is not measured, but rather immeasurable <note>original: "aparimitam"</note>; that is, he shall obtain wealth, excellent horses, and other such riches. **|| 10 ||**

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Eleventh House <term>Ekādaśa-bhāva: the house of gains, income, and desires</term>

When the one born of the Lord of the Stars <note>original: "tārāpati-putra," an epithet for Mercury as the son of the Moon</note> is situated in the House of Gains, there is no lack of profit, wealth, or physical charm <note>original: "lāvaṇya"</note>. He possesses a conquered mind and is free from sluggishness or disease. He has the capacity to perform the marriage of his daughters <note>original: "kanyakodvāhanāṃ"</note> and to give appropriate gifts. From where does this wealth come? It is generated somehow through the class of the "Gods of the Earth" <term>Bhūsuras: a traditional term for Brahmins</term>. Here, his desires are fulfilled through great wealth. Indeed, when Mercury is in the eleventh house, all these results of gain and success occur. This is what is heard in the tradition. **|| 11 ||**

<note>The following lines appear to be a versified summary or a different recension of the preceding prose commentary.</note>

|| He speaks measuredly, but receives what is not measured. Through grace, he obtains a prosperous kingdom. When Mercury is in the tenth house, he is particularly worthy of honor and attains wealth and political status through his authority. **|| 10 ||** Without the Son of the Moon situated in the House of Gains, there is no profit, no charm, and there is sluggishness. How could there be the marriage of daughters or the giving of gifts? How would his desires be fulfilled by the Earth-gods? **|| 11 ||** For one whose Moon-born Mercury is in the twelfth house, how do the Earth-gods attend his home? Why do enemies feel fear in the midst of battle? What rogue would not experience a treasury of gold and other riches? **|| 12 ||**

### Now, the Results of Mercury in the Twelfth House <term>Dvādaśa-bhāva: the house of loss, expenditure, and liberation</term>

For a person whose Moon-born Mercury is in the twelfth house, if he does not commit violence <note>original: "vadha"</note>, then how do the Earth-gods (Brahmins) attend or come to his "heaven" (his home)? Why do his enemies feel fear in the heat of battle? What rogue or miser could experience a treasury of gold and other such wealth? <note>The implication is that the person is exceptionally generous or spends lavishly on religious and noble causes</note>. He should spend his accumulated wealth. This is the result for the one who has this placement. **|| 12 ||**

### || Thus ends the results of Mercury from the seventh to the twelfth house ||

# Now, the results of Jupiter in the twelve houses, starting with the Body, are told.
<margin>Ki 85</margin>
When Jupiter, the Priest of the Gods <note>original: "devapūjye," an epithet for Jupiter as the preceptor of the deities</note>, is in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna: the first house, representing the self and the physical body</term>:
The person is well-dressed, appearing magnificent with fine garments and ornaments. They possess a divine and beautiful body. They are happy and free from sorrow, yet may be a man of little vigor or physical strength. Furthermore, through scholarship and by pleasing the public, they attain a state of liberation and are highly respected. Their future destiny will be of the next world; that is, it will take the form of dwelling in heaven. However, their goods and wealth are spent on provisions and food. **|| 1 ||**

## Now, the results of Jupiter in the Second House.
When Jupiter is in the **Second House** <term>Dvitīya-bhāva: the house of wealth, speech, and family</term>:
The person has an intellect for poetry and the creation of literature. They possess the power of leadership and the authority to dispense punishment, showing capability in exercising royal duties. There may be an affliction in the mouth, or the person may be very talkative or suffer from a disease of the face. Or, their enjoyment of pleasures may end quickly due to a lack of personal vitality. The acquisition of wealth occurs through great hardship and effort.

-> Happy, with a divine body, but little vigor. His destiny <-
-> Belonging to the next world should be considered. Garments and treasures <-
-> Are depleted through daily sustenance. **1** An intellect for poetry, <-
-> Power in leadership and punishment; an affliction in the mouth, <-
-> A quick end to pleasures and the like. When Jupiter is in the Family House, <-
-> The acquisition of wealth is difficult; money does not rest <-
-> Even with great effort. **2** He for whom [Jupiter is in the 3rd]... Srikanta, <-
-> The God-minister, he is the smallest among the small; happiness for siblings. <-
-> Endowed with fortune, he becomes a friend to a circle of companions; <-
-> Friendship in the rise of pastimes; there is no useless profit like that. **3** <-
-> That which is heard from the earth or spoken by the tongue of the twice-born, <-
-> The sound of the Vedas is like that. Rivals <-
-> Perform service; when Jupiter is in the fourth, it is like that, <-
-> And through those who have gone there. **4** <-

Always, even with great effort, wealth does not rest or find stability. **|| 2 ||**

## Now, the results of Jupiter in the Third House.
He in whose **Third House** <term>Tṛtīya-bhāva: the house of siblings, courage, and communication</term> the Minister of the Gods, Jupiter, resides: 
That man is the "smallest among the small"—meaning quite insignificant among low-minded people. He brings happiness and welfare to his siblings <note>original: "sodarāṇāṃ," literally 'those from the same womb'</note>. He is one who has fulfilled his duties. Likewise, while he maintains friendship within his circle of associates, he does not gain profit uselessly (like inherited wealth) even when entering the royal assembly through a stroke of luck. **|| 3 ||**

## Now, the results of Jupiter in the Fourth House.
When Jupiter is in the **Fourth House** <term>Caturtha-bhāva: the house of home, mother, and happiness</term>:
Just like the sound of the Vedas uttered or recited by the "twice-born" <note>original: "dvija," referring to the priestly class or Brahmins</note>, or the sound of benedictory mantras mentioned in the Vedas...

Words heard from the mouth of the Preceptor <note>original: "gurudvārato," referring to Jupiter or a spiritual teacher</note> or from the tongue are highly regarded. Oil for the genitals <note>original: "liṅgtaila," likely a reference to a medicinal or ritual preparation</note>. **6**. Even if enemies act with familiarity, the internal mind remains satisfied; the idea is that satisfaction comes from within rather than from external lack of animosity. **4**

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the Fifth House.

When the Priest of the Gods <note>original: "devapūjye," an epithet for Jupiter</note> is in the **Fifth House** <term>Pañcama-sthāna: the house of intelligence, children, and creativity</term>, the person’s mind is inclined toward luxury and enjoyment. They are loquacious <note>original: "jalpaka," a talkative person</note>, a logical thinker <note>original: "kalpaka"</note>, and a scribe with beautiful handwriting. However, at the time when the "fruit" of their labor is ready to be harvested, there may be obstacles or disturbances to the work. Even if children are present to help, true prosperity and the fullness of wealth only manifest in the final stage of life. **5**

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the Sixth House.

When Jupiter is in the **Sixth House** <term>Ṣaṣṭha-bhāva: the house of enemies, debt, and disease</term>, even if the person is afflicted by disease, they attain the power to destroy their enemies. They are an exalted victor in battle; no one can cause their defeat. Such a person is a connoisseur of all pleasures, including those derived from livestock like buffaloes. The actions of the maternal uncles <note>original: "mātulānāṃ"</note>...

-> When the Priest of the Gods is in the intelligence-house, the mind turns to luxury; <-
-> he becomes a talkative thinker and a scribe. Prosperity exists even when children are present, <-
-> but there is a disturbance of the fruit just as it ripens. **5** <-

Disease or sickness arises for the mother. **6**

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the Seventh House.

For one who has Jupiter in the **Seventh House** <term>Saptama-bhāva: the house of marriage and partnerships</term>, the intellect is "retrograde" or crooked <note>original: "vakrī matiḥ"</note>. However, they possess great prosperity and wealth. They have a great fondness and love for women and find joy in sexual union. Though they may be proud among men, they are superior to their kinsmen in strength, wealth, and beauty. The person becomes like Cupid <note>original: "kandarpaḥ," the god of love</note>; this quality applies throughout their life. **7**

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the Eighth House.

For one whose Jupiter is in the **Eighth House** <term>Aṣṭama-bhāva: the house of death and longevity</term>, the person lives for a very long time in a "house of the dead" <note>original: "pretake gehe," which could mean an ancient house, a house near a cemetery, or a house where a death occurred</note>. At the end of the body's life, they go to the Abode of Vishnu <note>original: "vaikuṇṭha-gantā"</note>. Their physical body is essentially useless; even if they build a house, they will not reside there for long, meaning they are either long-lived elsewhere or do not stay in one place. Furthermore, their body will not remain free from disease for a long time <note>Note: the prose commentary here seems to qualify the "long-lived" verse, suggesting a complexity in health</note>. **8**

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the Ninth House.

When Jupiter is in the **Ninth House** <term>Navama-sthāna: the house of fortune, dharma, and father</term>, the person gains strength and favor from the King. They are always free from laziness in their understanding of man. However, this placement causes a deficiency in religious duties; it results in the neglect of daily rituals such as the twilight prayers <note>original: "sandhyāvandanādi," the obligatory daily prayers for a Brahmin</note>. **9**

### Now, the Tenth House...

...in a pavilion decorated with flags <note>original: "dhvajā maṇḍapa"</note>. In the presence of people, there is a hall for assembly, a picture gallery <note>original: "citra-śālā"</note> containing various paintings, and a house or temple building filled with children. Every day, they should cook food in the kitchen using a *prastha* <term>Prastha: an ancient unit of volume, roughly equivalent to 640 to 800 grams</term> of sea salt <note>original: "sāmudra-lavaṇa"</note> and feed many people. Alternatively, for the phrase "in a pavilion of flags," some read "Jupiter in the house of action" <note>original: "gurau karma-ge," a more technical astrological phrasing</note>. **10**

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the Eleventh House.

When Jupiter is in the **House of Gains** <term>Lābha-bhāva: the eleventh house, governing income, wealth, and elder siblings</term>, what base metals <note>original: "kupyam," referring to copper, brass, or anything other than gold and silver</note> or gold and silver would they *not* obtain? Rather, they shall obtain everything. Other famous sages <note>original: "munīndrāḥ"</note> and experts in grammar say that such a person is like Indra <note>the king of the gods</note>. 

<column-break/>

> 9. For one whose Jupiter is in the Ninth House, there are four levels of housing, and they are like a lord of the land. They are like a fire for the enemies of the gods <note>original: "bhūmi-devāḥ," literally 'earth-gods' or Brahmins</note>. When Jupiter is in the House of Dharma, relatives are humble, and the person is always wealthy and performs religious deeds. 
> 10. In the Tenth, there is a temple-like home with a pavilion of flags and a gallery of pictures. The person's splendor exceeds that of their father and ancestors. Through their deeds, their children are happy; every day, they cook a *prastha* of food with sea salt.
> 11. When Jupiter is in the House of Gains, what wealth is not obtained? So say the wise sages. The person supports their father, and has five biological sons. If their wealth is not used for others, for charity, or for spiritual merit, it leads only to miserliness. 
> 12. What kind of fame is it that comes with such pride? What kind of intelligence is it that seeks to deceive others? What kind of ritual or wealth-gathering is it that leads to the destruction of the next world? These three—expenditure on relatives, deceiving others, and the loss of wealth through improper means—occur when Jupiter is in the Twelfth.

<column-break/>

The person supports their father. They shall have five biological sons. Their wealth is intended for others—meaning for the enjoyment, charity, and religious merit of others—and not merely for their own existence. This implies that they should avoid being a miser. There is an alternate reading for "supporting the father" <note>original: "pituḥ bhārabhrṛt"</note> which suggests the family's wealth is protected. **11**

### Now, the results of Jupiter in the Twelfth House.

For one who has Jupiter <note>original: "jīva," another name for Jupiter meaning 'the living force'</note> in the **Twelfth House** <term>Vyaya-bhāva: the house of loss, expenditure, and liberation</term>, their fame is joined with great pride. Their intellect is inclined toward deceiving others. Even if they have wealth like Kubera <note>the god of wealth</note>, what is the use of a ritual or a method that causes the destruction of the "highest wealth"—that is, the afterlife or spiritual merit—or the loss of tangible assets? These three things will happen: expenses related to one's kinsmen, the tendency to cheat others, and the loss of money through improper or illegal means. **12**

Thus ends the section on the results of Jupiter in the various houses in the *Camatkāra Cintāmaṇi* <note>The Jewel of Miraculous Thought, a classic 14th-century astrological text</note>. **12** May there be auspiciousness.

<summary>This page concludes the astrological effects of Jupiter, highlighting themes of public status (10th house), wealth and large families (11th house), and the spiritual or financial dangers of pride and deception (12th house).</summary>

# Now, he speaks of the results of the twelve houses, beginning with the Ascendant.

For a person whose Venus <note>original: "śukra"</note> is excellent and occupies the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>, their body will be handsome and virtuous. Their company <note>original: "saṅgaḥ"</note> will be with the virtuous—those endowed with beauty, cleverness, and other fine qualities. Such a person is joined with the enjoyment of many excellent women. Their various religious actions, such as sacrifices and charity <note>original: "yajña-dāna-ādīni"</note>, are performed well. The fruits of these actions, consisting of the enjoyment of worldly objects in this life or the next, will surely come to pass. **1**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Second House.

For one whose Venus—the one worshiped by the elder gods <note>original: "pūrvadevasya pūjyaḥ," an epithet for Venus/Shukracharya as the preceptor of the Asuras</note>—is situated in the house of family and wealth <term>Kuṭumbasthāna: the second house, governing family, speech, and accumulated wealth</term>, there is beautiful speech and an eloquent voice in their mouth. Their intellect <note>original: "manīṣā"</note> is also beautiful and guided by the sacred scriptures. They are very righteous, finding pleasure in various beautiful things and wearing many types of fine garments. 

> For one whose Venus is in the Ascendant, the body is beautiful, the company is excellent, and they are joined with the enjoyment of beautiful women. Their deeds are good, their happiness is great, and they are blessed. **1** Their face possesses beautiful speech, their intellect is fine, and they have comfort and beautiful clothes. When Venus, the preceptor of the demons, stays in the house of family, the family is not unpleasing, but filled with those who desire beautiful-bodied women. **2** 
<margin>(of the...)</margin>
> He has a passion for women, and there is no loss of his relatives; he is a teacher even to those who are difficult to manage <note>original: "duścikyā," referring to the third house</note> or those who give cattle. Even if his happiness regarding sons is not complete, he is a commander of an army, though perhaps timid at the time of charity or battle. **3** For one who has the teacher of the gods <note>original: "surejya," usually Jupiter, here possibly used in comparison or as a title</note> in the fourth house, his greatness is increased. What do his own people or others, whether angry or pleased, matter to him?

With their family and kinsmen, the mind is unrivaled in its beauty and brilliance. There is a desire for beautiful-bodied women. This should be understood as the result wherever applicable. **2**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Third House.

For one whose Venus, the preceptor of the demons, is situated in the third house <term>Duścikya: a technical term for the third house, which governs siblings, courage, and desires</term>, there is a passion for women. There is no loss of relatives; how could there be anything but happiness? Similarly, if the desire for happiness from sons is not fully realized, even if the person is an army commander, they may become timid <note>original: "kātara"</note> at the moment of giving or in the time of battle. This should be applied generally. **3**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Fourth House.

When the preceptor of the demons <note>original: "asurejya"</note>—Venus—is in the fourth house <term>Turya-bhāva: the fourth house, representing the home, mother, and emotional happiness</term>, the person becomes great in honor or in their excellent heart. Whether his own people or others are joyful or satisfied, he remains like a compassionate person... <note>The text breaks off here due to ink bleeding and page transition.</note>

What is the use of being born if one is dependent? One should be filled with wealth <note>original: "upāyana," meaning offerings or gifts</note> presented by those who depend upon them. From the very moment of birth, how much happiness does such a person bring to their mother? They shall cause an abundance of joy. **4**

### Now, the results for the Fifth House.

For one whose Venus <note>original: "śukre"</note> is in the house of children <term>Pañcama bhāva: the fifth house, governing children, intelligence, and creativity</term>, what is the meaning of having a son if that birth does not bear fruit? If wealth is not earned even with great effort and labor, what is the use? In this house, the result is distinguished by success in battle and similar endeavors. One becomes content through the mixture of poetry and the chanting of mantras <term>Mantrajapa: the meditative repetition of a sacred formula</term>. Through teaching, their intellect becomes inclined toward composing poetry. If one studies but does not achieve this, what is the use of that learning? When Venus is here, it is fruitful. One becomes an earner of wealth through the birth of a son, a lord among people, and a performer of mantra-recitation who enjoys the finest food and materials. Through the power of poetry, study...
