### Now, the results of Venus in the Sixth House.

When the Preceptor of the Demons <note>original: "Dānavė jye," an epithet for Venus as the guru of the Asuras</note>, Venus, is in the house of enemies <term>Ṣaṣṭha bhāva: the sixth house, associated with enemies, debt, and illness</term>, one becomes like an immortal sprinkled with nectar <note>original: "sudhā sikta," meaning drenched in the drink of the gods</note>. This means they are like one who lives forever. Through the sacrifice of wealth <note>original: "dravya tyāga"</note>, they become excellent in their actions and virtues. This means wealth will arise even from the path of the enemy. Furthermore, regarding the deeds performed and the objects of the senses, one does not hold them firmly. From the counsel of a bad advisor, one might obtain distress. The heavy weight of worship or the happiness of a husband is not maintained. **6**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Seventh House.

When Venus is in the house of the spouse <term>Saptama bhāva: the seventh house, governing marriage and partnerships</term>, there is happiness from the wife <note>original: "kalatra," a term for spouse/wife</note>. Happiness is produced through the presence of a wife. According to the Amarakosha <note>a famous Sanskrit lexicon</note>, "Kalatra" and "Bhāryā" are synonyms. In the house of the spouse, she may be a "jewel-womb" <note>original: "ratna garbha," likely meaning she bears excellent children</note>, meaning she will be possessed of sons. The person will be excessively fond of play and dallying. They will be a traveler <note>original: "pravāsī"</note>, prone to going to foreign lands. They are described as being singular in their great efforts. By what means, or from what man...

### Now, the results of Venus in the Eighth House.

At the time of birth, if the Preceptor of the Demons <note>original: "daityapūjyaḥ," a title for Venus</note>, who is the enemy of Indra, is in the eighth house, the person may have a wretched face. Likewise, they may be an evil speaker <note>original: "duṣṭavaktā," one who speaks harshly or deceptively</note>. However, it is also said they may live for a long time with a beautiful face. Success and wealth are found where the influence of Indra’s enemy <note>Venus</note> is low or unfavorable. Wealth given to a debtor by a creditor is returned through a messenger. **8**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Ninth House.

When Venus <note>original: "bhṛgo," refers to the sage Bhrigu, the father of Venus</note> is in the ninth house, which is a trine <term>trikoṇa: one of the auspicious houses (1st, 5th, or 9th) in a birth chart</term>, the men who are citizens in that city would provide him with growth or a livelihood through usury <term>kusīdena: lending money at interest</term>. This means that in that city, such people do not live apart from that lack of righteousness. Furthermore, because he is a "religious flag-bearer" <term>dharmadhvaja: literally one who carries the flag of religion, but historically used to describe a hypocrite who makes a show of piety</term>, his reputation is actually a pretense. He finds comfort and bodily happiness through those who have risen with him or through those he previously feared. The *Amarakosha* <note>a famous Sanskrit lexicon</note> defines a "religious flag-bearer" as one whose profession is based on their religious attire—that is, those who wear the clothes of righteousness for a living. **9**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Tenth House.

When Venus is in the tenth house, also known as the house of action <term>karmaga: the house governing career and public life</term>, does he indeed hinder the strength of the lineage or the "seed of the family"? There is a confusion that takes the form of forgetting proper conduct. <margin>Wealth-Lord</margin> What is the use of being a lord of wealth? By daily taking the livelihood of a Brahmin <note>original: "vipra-hatyā," literally 'killing a Brahmin,' but the commentary glosses this as taking a Brahmin's means of living</note>, and by making a public display to gain prestige through attendants, or out of pride in disputes, he might acquire gold equal to a hundred *palas* <note>an ancient unit of weight</note> on a scale. Yet even then, the total result is very small. This is the decision regarding the results associated with one's ancestors and the weight of one's actions. **10**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Eleventh House.

For one who has Venus in the eleventh house from the Ascendant, which is the house of gains <term>lābha-ga: the house governing income, profit, and goals</term>, Venus becomes a bestower of profit and wealth. Such a person is of good character, endowed with virtues, and radiant with fame...

...[Venus] creates affection for women, and through fame, truth, and pleasing the public, it bestows great fortune and makes one exceptionally lucky. Due to his authority, it makes such a person a ruler who properly governs the earth. If the planet is very strong, these same results will manifest. **11**

### Now, the results of Venus in the Twelfth House.

When Venus <note>original: "sitaḥ," the bright one</note> is in the twelfth house, it brings amusement, good deeds, peace, and the happiness produced by sacrifices. However, the fame of one’s good qualities is destroyed. It always creates opposition and quarrels with friends and people. Furthermore, wealth is never quite attained; even if obtained, it vanishes or remains hidden. This also implies an "excess of phlegm" <term>kaphādhikya: an imbalance of the Kapha dosha in Ayurveda, often leading to lethargy or respiratory issues</term>. Some alternative readings <note>original: "pāṭha," a textual variant</note> say "constantly" here. **12**

### Now, the results of Saturn in the twelve houses, beginning with the First.

When Saturn, the son of the Sun <note>original: "arkaje," a common name for Saturn as the son of Surya</note>, is in the first house <term>lagna: the ascendant or the house of the physical body</term>, the person is lacking in wealth...

His sight is "uneven," which can act as a destroyer of enemies. Despite being tormented by envy—the inability to tolerate the success of others—he remains self-driven and industrious. **1**

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Second House.

When Saturn is in the second house, the house of family <term>kuṭumba: the second house in a birth chart, representing family, speech, and accumulated wealth</term>, that man—regarding his family—becomes separated or detached from his kinsmen. This implies he moves to another country. He does not merely possess wealth; rather, he becomes a consumer of all objects of enjoyment. Furthermore, he may obtain gold, iron, or other metals—whether through weapons or sudden falls of enemies—without needing to perform service for them.

...attains what is in his hand? This means, who would get it? He speaks truthful words to friends, yet he speaks harsh and unbearable sentences. **2**

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Third House.

When Saturn <note>original: "shani"</note> is in the third house <term>bhava: an astrological house representing specific life areas; the third house relates to siblings and courage</term>, one does not find a cool or peaceful mind despite the efforts and valor involving people and siblings. Fortune and gains are never steady or without confusion at any time. The person is a balanced speaker <note>original: "yuktabhāṣī"</note>—a man of few words. He is resolute and steady, yet because of this, he may be perceived as foul-mouthed or loquacious even when being honest. **3**

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Fourth House.

When Saturn is in the fourth house <term>caturtha-bhava: the house associated with the home, mother, and happiness</term>, paternal wealth <note>original: "paitṛkaṃ dhanam"</note> and the family home may go far away or be destroyed. There is criticism of relatives <margin>criticism of kin</margin> and blame within the family circle. There are losses regarding the home and vehicles—meaning both the house and the means of transport are ruined. It causes distress to both the father and the mother. Furthermore, the person will suffer from "wind-diseases" <term>vāta-rogī: an imbalance of the Vata dosha in Ayurveda, often causing joint pain or digestive issues</term>. **4**

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Fifth House.

When Saturn is in the fifth house <term>pañcama-bhava: the house of children, intelligence, and creativity</term>, the person suffers sorrow on account of their progeny <note>original: "prajā-hetu-duḥkha"</note>, meaning they endure distress regarding their children. They do not attain great prosperity or wealth. Their intellect is not steady, but rather "cruel," which means it is crooked. They have no delight or deep faith in matters regarding the gods, the scriptures of righteousness <term>dharma-shastra: ancient legal and social treatises</term>, or the Vedas. There are quarrels and conflicts with friends. This person remains burdened by various anxieties and struggles. **5**

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Sixth House.

If the Son of the Sun <note>original: "ina-suta," Saturn</note> is in the sixth house, which is the house of enemies <term>ripu-bhava</term>, should one fear enemies and thieves? Indeed, but they will not prevail. However, there will be confusion and disputes with opponents. An alternative reading <note>original: "pāṭha"</note> suggests that his intellect will lead him through these struggles. There is happiness to be gained from buffaloes, cows, and other four-legged livestock. However, the destruction or loss of maternal uncles <note>original: "mātulānāṃ vināśaḥ"</term> will occur. **6**

Blessings.

# Now, the results of Saturn in the Seventh House.

When Saturn <note>original: "shani"</note> is located in the seventh house <term>dyūna-ga: the house of partnerships and marriage</term>, the individual may have a good wife <note>original: "sudārāḥ"</note> and excellent women around them. They will have charming friends and helpful companions, and they will acquire fine wealth. However, this wealth may be "devoid of justice" <note>original: "nyāya-varjitaṃ"</note>—implying it is obtained through questionable means. It is said that this wealth and happiness will not endure for a long time <note>original: "na saheta"</note>. Furthermore, the couple—both the husband and the wife—may be afflicted by disease. There is also a lack of enthusiasm; the person is constantly unmotivated and possesses a "low mind" or a discouraged heart <note>original: "hīna-cetā"</note>. Such a person is ineffectual <note>original: "viddhalaḥ"</note> and greedy. Being so covetous, where would their strength and valor come from? This connection applies to all these traits. **7**

---

## Now, the results of Saturn in the Eighth House.

When Saturn is in the eighth house <term>randhra: the house of vulnerabilities, secrets, and longevity</term>, there is a separation from people who have ulterior motives or "limitations" <note>original: "upādhikānām"</note>. It implies a lack of good company. There is a destruction of wealth <note>original: "dhanānāṃ vināśo"</note>, such that no riches remain. Additionally, the person becomes diseased and "loses their vision"—meaning they only look for the faults in others <note>original: "para-doṣānveṣī"</note>. In the presence of such a person, what can one do against their deceit or roguery? The implication is that they become a great rogue or are stripped of their senses. **8**

---

### Now, the results of Saturn in the Ninth House.

<margin>
॥ He who travels for wealth, though attached to power, remains greedy and restless in the same way. ॥ 120 ॥
</margin>
<margin>
॥ One should know the distress of a restless master; at the age of twenty, his religious rites are lost. ॥ Wealth and fame are destroyed. ॥
</margin>

When Saturn is in the house of Dharma <term>dharma-ge: the ninth house, relating to fortune, philosophy, and the father</term>:
<note>The following lines seem to briefly recap the 7th and 8th house results before expanding on the 9th.</note>
Good wife and friends, but wealth is transient; in the seventh, the couple suffers from disease. Without enthusiasm, with a low mind, how can there be valor? He is ineffectual and greedy. **7** There is separation from people with motives and the destruction of wealth for those who have it. When Saturn is in the eighth house, one is diseased, searching for faults; what can deceit do before such a person? **8** 

The intellect becomes "bitter" or sharp <note>original: "tiktā"</note>, but the person's character is not necessarily bitter. They are devoted to the study of the *Yoga Shastra* <term>Yoga Shastra: technical treatises on meditation and philosophy</term> and are respected by teachers and kings. If the person is among friends, they feel distressed due to a "poor intellect" or humble disposition. When Saturn is in the ninth house, the desire to perform actions and duties arises. **9** For one born under this, the mother or father may experience vanity or sorrow because of the lordship over difficult tasks. The person prospers in the tenth house of action, but there may be "defects in dharma" or religious merit regarding the subjects of their livelihood. **20**

The intellect of the miserable is bitter, having a distaste for priestly wealth. The character is not bitter but harsh. There is the practice and study of books explaining the *Yoga Shastra*. The teachers are of a royal or passionate nature <note>original: "rājasa guru"</note>. Because of a humble intellect, one's circle of friends and kin becomes distressed or pitiable. The mind becomes troubled and weary, yet one remains clothes in a peaceful appearance that brings comfort. One may renounce or lose their status as a master. **9**

---

# Now, the results of Saturn in the Tenth House.

For one who has Saturn <note>original: "manda" (the Slow One)</note> in the tenth house <term>karma-ga: the house of profession and public action</term>, the mother may be a wanderer or follow a difficult path, and may not be present in the capacity of a mother <note>original: "mātṛ-bhāvena"</note>. The person might be raised on the milk of others. By the power of their own arms <note>original: "vahu-reva sva-bhuja"</note>, they become the earner of wealth. The father may have a short life, and there will be the destruction of friends. Furthermore, due to their position of authority or as a "protector of the treasury," they cause a certain effect: throughout the world, they are known for their focused work or perhaps for administering punishments, leading to a gradual mastery over their subjects. Everything increases and prospers. Regarding livelihood, the person provides life to others, though they themselves may possess "low consciousness" or a simple spirit. **110**
<meta>catchword: tyo (connecting to the next page)</meta>
->Thus ends the results of Saturn in the 10th House.<-

<summary>This page details the astrological influences of Saturn (Shani) when placed in the 7th through 10th houses, highlighting themes of transient wealth, domestic illness, sharp intellect, and self-made professional success often accompanied by parental loss.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Shani, Bhava, Seventh House, Eighth House, Ninth House, Tenth House, Yoga Shastra, Astrology, Karma</keywords>

--- Page 32 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page continues the astrological analysis of Saturn (Shani) through the houses of the zodiac, concluding its effects in the tenth, eleventh, and twelfth houses before beginning the analysis of Rahu, the North Node of the Moon.</meta>

# For the sake of understanding, another opinion regarding Saturn in the Tenth House is stated.

When Saturn <note>original: "vyomage"—literally "the one moving in the sky," referring here to Saturn</note> is in the tenth house <term>karma-bhāva: the house of profession, public life, and fatherhood</term>, does the mother find happiness? Oh learned one, it is not so indicated by the influence of the father or the child’s play. This means that happiness for the mother is not predicted here. 

Furthermore, treasures or stores of wealth established by sacred chants <note>original: "mantras"</note>, or agriculture well-sown by the father, may be visible. However, these will surely be destroyed <note>original: "praṇaśyet"</note> by external armies or conflicts, or by divine fears such as fire and water. **10**

---

## Now, the auspicious and inauspicious results for the Eleventh House.

When the son of the Sun <note>Saturn is traditionally known as "Bhanu-putra" or the son of the Sun god</note> is in the house of gain <term>lābha-bhāva: the house of income, gains, and elder siblings</term>, wealth remains steady and unchanging. The individual’s income is stable, and their mind is firm. Regarding diseases, it does not mean there is an absence of illness, but rather that one possesses "steady health"—a constitution that remains consistent. 

Regarding offspring, however, the progeny may perish; the implication is that there will be grief concerning children. Yet, among hundreds of men, such a person stands out as superior. They become a primary hero, a chief among others, and free from the faults typically associated with royal service. This should be understood as the true state of affairs. **11**

---

<margin>
The sense is: "Who among the departed shall be considered superior?" **12**
</margin>

## Now, the results for the Twelfth House.

> When the son of the Sun is in the house of expenditure <term>vyaya-sthāna: the twelfth house, associated with loss, liberation, and foreign lands</term>, a man has chronic or "given" diseases. He is agitated or fearful, and possesses "slow eyes" <note>original: "manda-netra"—meaning poor eyesight or a dull gaze</note>. He is happy while abroad <note>original: "vahiḥ"—outside or in foreign countries</note>, but not at home. If the lord of the ascendant <term>lagnapa: the planet ruling the first house</term> is also in the twelfth house, he becomes a destroyer of enemies. He is an "enjoyer of the sacrifice"—meaning his prosperity and success come through spending and charitable acts. **12**

When Saturn is in the twelfth house, the person may be cruel, cowardly rather than fearless, and lacking in luster or beauty. As stated, they are happy in foreign lands but unhappy in their own home. Furthermore, if the ruler of the first house is positioned in this twelfth house of the body, they will crush their enemies. Their wealth and abundance are linked to the "costs of sacrifice" <note>this suggests that their spiritual or charitable spending actually brings them more wealth</note>. **12**

*Thus concludes the twelfth section on Saturn in the **Chamatkara Chintamani** <note>original: "Chamatkara Chintamani" - a famous 17th-century astrological text</note>.*

---

# Now, the results of Rahu in the houses, starting from the Ascendant.

When Rahu <note>original: "Tamas"—literally "darkness," a common name for the shadow planet Rahu</note> is in the first house <term>lagna: the ascendant or house of self</term>, that person breaks the power of their enemies; they are a conqueror of foes. 

> In maintaining their word, they are capable due to the influence and power of others. Because of this external influence, they cover or protect their own people and even the interests of others. He who has the "Darkness" <note>Rahu</note> in his ascendant finds the strength of his enemies shattered. However, regarding his wife, he may have many wives or be troubled and anxious concerning his spouse. **12**

In the house of family <term>kuṭumba: the second house</term>, if the "Darkness" <note>Rahu</note> is present, the family may face destruction or become a source of great anxiety... <note>The text here transitions into the results for the second house.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the analysis of Saturn's placement in the final houses, noting its tendency to bring success abroad but grief regarding children, before introducing Rahu's effects on the self and family.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Shani, Rahu, Chamatkara Chintamani, Tenth House, Eleventh House, Twelfth House, Ascendant, Jyotisha, Vedic Astrology</keywords>

<meta>Continuity: This page transitions from the discussion of Saturn's effects in the final houses to the astrological analysis of Rahu, the North Node of the Moon (often called the "shadow planet"). <margin>13</margin>...one should go toward satisfaction. The meaning is that he will be excessive in desire. **11**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Second House**
If the shadow-planet Rahu <note>original: "Tamo-rahu"—the darkness of Rahu</note> is in the second house <term>kutumba-bhāva: the house of family, wealth, and speech</term>, it affects the family and all people. Because of wicked people <note>original: "khalebhyaḥ"</note>, one will certainly experience dependency on others, imprisonment, or fear from weapons. Furthermore, their speech will be false and devoid of sweetness or fearlessness. They will be a "protector of wealth," which is to say, a miser. The destruction of one's own kin and relatives also follows. **2**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Third House**
In this birth, if Rahu is in the third house <term>sahaja-bhāva: the house of courage, siblings, and efforts</term>, the person moves with the strength of their arms like an elephant or a lion. The entire world becomes like a sibling to them. Although fortune may be delayed or struck down initially, they eventually attain luck. Why? Because the cause of gain is present here, meaning profit will surely come. **3**

> He moves in this world with the strength of his arms
> like an elephant or a lion.
> In the third house, the world becomes like his brother;
> even if fortune is struck, wealth eventually arrives. **3**

**How can there be health for the brother in the fourth?**
**With a fire in the heart, can the exterior be cool?**
**If it is in Aries, Scorpio, Cancer, or the signs of Mercury,**
**the Demon <note>Rahu</note> becomes a friend to the King.** **4**

Regarding the fifth house, the son of Simhika <note>an epithet for Rahu, who in mythology was the son of the lioness-demoness Simhika</note> causes mental distress due to a passionate woman. When there is disease in the belly, what is the use of food? Why continue with the complexities of worldly life; is this not surely a curse? **5**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Fourth House**
In the fourth house <term>sukha-bhāva: the house of mother, happiness, and home</term>, how can there be health for the mother’s body when the demon Rahu <note>original: "Asuro-rahu"</note> is present? It means there is none. When the heart is burning with mental agony, how can the exterior of the body be cool? The person themselves will be possessed by disease. **However, if Rahu is in Aries, Scorpio** <note>signs ruled by Mars</note>, **Cancer** <note>sign ruled by the Moon</note>, **or the signs of Mercury** <note>Virgo and Gemini</note>, then Rahu gives results like the God of Love <note>original: "Manmatha"</note>. In those cases, Rahu is very auspicious and makes the person a close relative or friend of the King. **4**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Fifth House**
In the fifth house <term>putra-bhāva: the house of children, intellect, and creativity</term>, what does the son of Simhika (Rahu) do? There is mental distress caused by an angry or passionate woman <note>original: "bhāminī"</note> whom one relies upon. When there is "disease in the lap" <note>original: "kroḍa-roge"—referring here to the stomach or internal organs</note>, medicine and food provide no help. This refers to stomach ailments and a loss of appetite which are unavoidable. Without divine grace, why put in the effort for worldly expansion? One should understand the gain of results in this context. **5**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Sixth House**
For those people where Rahu is situated in the sixth house <term>śatru-bhāva: the house of enemies, debts, and obstacles</term>, it destroys the assembly of enemies even at the risk of death to the body. Furthermore, through bodily strength, intellectual power, and cleverness, one forcibly acquires wealth and food.

...a steady mind. Otherwise, there is a lack of support from one's mother and father; the mind becomes flickering, and there is a total absence of maternal or paternal connection. This applies everywhere in the remaining cases. **6**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Seventh House**
When Rahu is in the seventh house <term>saptama-bhāva: the house of marriage and partnerships</term>, it is especially like Rahu being placed in a "white cauldron" <note>original: "shveta-kaṭāhe" — an astrological metaphor for a specific planetary state</note> with the Moon. Just as Fire <note>original: "Jātavedāḥ," a traditional name for the god Agni</note> causes heat in iron, the person may suffer destruction or great physical affliction through diseases related to the humors <note>original: "dhātu-pāka" — an imbalance or 'cooking' of the bodily tissues</note>. Such is the nature of this planetary fault. One experiences separations <note>original: "viyogāḥ"</note> and specific sorrows involving kin. There is public scandal and localized infamy which does not find peace or resolution. **7**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Eighth House**
Rahu situated in the eighth house <term>randhra-bhāva: the house of longevity, death, and hidden things</term> brings the threat of murder and enemies. It creates confusion or dizziness <note>original: "bhramam"</note> and "knots" <note>original: "granthi" — can refer to physical tumors, cysts, or digestive blockages like a "wind-globe"</note> in the body caused by wind or gas, requiring great effort to resolve. Furthermore, other people tend to abandon such a person. Just as a person's birth chart might show wealth and honor given by kings and scholars, this person will instead be condemned by their own relatives. Any gain in fortune will be fleeting—occurring only once and then falling away. **8**

-> Strength, intelligence, vigor, and wealth are obstructed in the house of enemies <-
-> ...of the people. 21. ...the body of the gods <-

> <margin>
> **Rahu in the houses:**
> **6.** A steady mind is not for the wise if Rahu is elsewhere.
> **7.** They face destruction of the body through the cooking of the humors, like iron in a white cauldron. This is the fire of separation and scandal that finds no peace.
> **8.** Honored by kings and scholars but condemned by his own. Fortune comes but once. People abandon him; he suffers from the knots of confusion and the threat of enemies.
> **9.** The wise scholar does not abandon his kin; he always protects them. He shines in the assembly and is devoted to the gods and pilgrimages.
> </margin>

There is a repeated loss of work or purpose. **8**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Ninth House**
Where Rahu is in the ninth house <term>tri-trikoṇa: the "triple-trine" or house of fortune and dharma</term>, the person is a wise scholar <note>original: "manīṣī"</note> and highly intelligent. They are honored by their own teachers and are full of compassion. They are devoted to the gods and holy pilgrimage sites. Such a person becomes a "shiner in the assembly" <note>original: "sabhā-dyotakaḥ" — a person of public prestige</note> and a light to others. <insert>ink blot</insert> They do not abandon their duties and always provide for and protect their circle of relatives. **9**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Tenth House**
When the shadow-planet Rahu is in the tenth house <term>kha-ga: "sky-goer," here referring to the mid-heaven or house of career</term>, this person may spend their wealth at the time of a person's death <note>or "in ways that kill their capital"</note>. They may engage in cruel deeds and, consequently, feel agitated by other people. Despite this, they may accumulate wealth through hundreds of different treasures. They are often in constant contact with foreigners <note>original: "mleccha" — those outside the traditional Vedic social order</note>. They possess great pride and are highly regarded. They enjoy the company of proud and beautiful women and attain the fruits of their desires. **10**

### **Now, the results for Rahu in the Eleventh House**
If Rahu has reached the eleventh house <term>lābha-bhāva: the house of gains and income</term>, one finds satisfaction and happiness. One always acquires wealth from foreign pilgrimage sites or through foreign connections. Such a person is full of self-respect. They move about with attendants and servants; the meaning is that they never go anywhere without a retinue. **11**

<summary>This page details the astrological implications of Rahu in the 7th through 11th houses, contrasting the physical and social hardships of the 7th and 8th houses with the intellectual and material successes of the 9th, 10th, and 11th.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the astrological analysis from page 13, concluding the results for the shadow planet Rahu in the eleventh and twelfth houses before transitioning to the placement of Ketu (referred to here as "Shikhi") in the first and second houses.</meta>

...Why would one go to a foreign land? For what purpose would they travel? One should obtain everything while remaining right at home. This is the sense of the passage. He whose relatives and friends are rogues—because they desire misfortune for him—finds that they cause all manner of ruin. One might seize the wealth of others for oneself; it is implied that he speaks out of fear of that loss. **11**

### **Now, the results for the Twelfth House**

In the twelfth house <term>dvādaśa-bhāva: the house of loss, liberation, and the subconscious</term>, Rahu <note>original: "tamas," literally meaning "darkness," a common name for the North Node</note> brings a sense of wretchedness, pain in the sides of the body, and suffering in the chest or lap. It creates friendships with the wicked, but enmity and a spirit of hostility toward good and saintly people. Even when efforts are made, they result in misfortune or the reversal of a disciple's work. In the end, though there is mental anxiety, it eventually brings about the fulfillment of wealth—this principle applies throughout the reading. **12**

### **Thus ends the results for Rahu in the various houses.**

### **Now he describes the results of Ketu in the twelve houses. First, the results for the First House.**

If at the time of birth <term>Shikhi</term> (Ketu) is <term>tanustha</term> <note>situated in the first house or the "house of the body"</note>, he causes distress and quarrels with one's relatives and brothers. There is fear from wicked people and a state of mental confusion or agitation. One experiences constant worry regarding their spouse and family. There is persistent unease, and various types of wind-related <note>original: "mārutī," or Vata-type</note> pains and disorders will manifest in the physical body. **1**

### **Now, the results for Ketu in the Second House**

When Ketu is in the <term>dhana</term> <note>the second house, governing wealth, speech, and food</note> house, there is constant anxiety regarding money and wealth, perhaps involving the authorities or the king. There is a destruction of grain and the loss of one's place of shelter. It causes diseases in the mouth or face. There is opposition and conflict with family and friends. Even if one speaks kindly or uses "refined words," it may not help; however, if Ketu is placed in the house of a "gentle" or benefic planet <note>original: "saumya-gehe," likely referring to the signs of Mercury, Gemini or Virgo</note>, then happiness will manifest.

### **The Illustration of Lord Ganesha**

<note>The manuscript leaf features a vibrant, traditional illustration of the deity Ganesha, who is traditionally invoked at the beginning of texts to remove obstacles.</note>

*The illustration depicts Ganesha with his characteristic orange elephant head and four arms. He is seated in a meditative posture upon the coiled body of the five-headed serpent, Shesha Naga <term>Shesha Naga: the primordial serpent of infinity who supports the universe</term>. Ganesha holds a trident (trishula) symbolizing his power over the three worlds, a bowl (likely containing sweets/modaka), and a rosary (mala) for prayer. He is framed by ornate architectural elements and attended by two figures—one holding a staff and another playing a stringed instrument.*

--- Page 38 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the previously blank folio (which showed only ink bleed-through) into the formal opening of the manuscript. It includes the introductory commentary (Tika) titled "Jayalakshmi" followed by the primary root verses of the "Narapati-jayacharya-svarodaya."</meta>

->**Om! Salutations to the Illustrious Ganesha**<-

Having offered salutations to Ganesha, the destroyer of all obstacles, and to the Lord of Goddess Lakshmi <note>The Hindu god Vishnu</note> for the fulfillment of all desired goals. **|| 1 ||** The poet Harivansha, in this "Arising of the Breath-Sound" <note>original: *Svarodaya*</note>, explains the names and origins of the vowels and the alphabet. **|| 2 ||** Having heard much from my gurus according to my knowledge and intellect, I, the King, am composing this commentary called "The Victory of Lakshmi" <note>original: *Jayalakshmi-tika*</note>. **|| 3 ||** Now the poet offers salutations to the Brahman in the form of the deity Saturn <note>unclear: reference to Shanaishchara or a specific aspect of the Eternal</note> and the Unmanifest. 

The Unmanifest is that which is devoid of hands, feet, head, or eyes; it is peaceful, without qualities <note>term: *nirguna* - the concept of the Divine without physical attributes</note>, and is exceedingly dear to the yogis such as Sanaka and others. It is the very essence of universal bliss, all-pervading and omnipresent—to that Truth, I bow. **|| 1 ||** Again, he salutes the Goddess of Wisdom, Saraswati, through various descriptions of the gods. To the various gods and to the one who inspires them, the Goddess Saraswati; to the King who composes this, and to Lakshmi who is desired by the people—the poet known in the world as Narapati is creating this scientific treatise titled "The Victory of the Teacher Narapati." **|| 2 ||** Now the poet Narapati provides an account of his own country and lineage. He was a resident of the beautiful city of Dharapuri <note>An ancient city in India, historically a center of learning</note>.

---

->**Om! Salutations to the Illustrious Ganesha**<-

I bow to Shiva-Bhaskara <note>Shiva in the form of the Sun</note>, who, with the rays of his knowledge, rescues those people who are submerged in the darkness of delusion. **|| 1 ||** I worship that Essence <note>term: *Tattva*</note> which is unmanifest, imperishable, peaceful, the most beloved of yogis, the embodiment of universal bliss, and all-pervading. **|| 2 ||** Having bowed to Saraswati, the foremost among the gods, and desiring to speak to the people with great cleverness and wisdom, I, known in the world by the name Narapati, shall now declare this science titled "The Victory of the Teacher Narapati" <note>original: *Narapati-jayacharya*</note>. **|| 3 ||** 

In the Golden Age <note>term: *Krita Yuga*</note>, this was named "Victory." In the Silver Age <note>term: *Treta Yuga*</note>, it was called "The Union of Brahma" <note>original: *Brahma Yamala*</note>. In the Bronze Age <note>term: *Dvapara Yuga*</note>, it was known as "The Great Victory," and in the Iron Age <note>term: *Kali Yuga*</note>, it is known as "The Arising of the Breath-Sound" <note>original: *Svarodaya*</note>. **|| 4 ||** In this tradition, the first is the pure *Yamala*, the second is the *Vishva Yamala*, the third is called the *Rudra Yamala*, and the fourth is the *Adya Yamala*. **|| 5 ||**

---

There was a wise and famous man named Naradeva, who had seen the essence of the various ancient scriptures <note>term: *Agamas*</note>. **|| 1 ||** He was an expert in the essence of the body and the movement of breath, a knower of the science of omens, and a master of both Tantra and Mantra. He was a crown jewel among those who could distinguish between the essential and the non-essential. His son was named Narapati. **|| 2 ||** In terms of knowledge, he is all-knowing and worshipped by many kings; he has attained success through the grace of Saraswati. This work composed by him is free from many faults and full of virtues. **|| 3 ||** Use this science, O King and poet, for it is proven by logic. Victory belongs to Dharmabhisimha-dasa <note>likely the patron or a specific devotee</note>, as if seated on a lotus. **|| 4 ||** Having conquered the enemy, he undoubtedly gives wealth to his own king. At this time, the wise man considers the four types of victory in battle. **|| 5 ||** This system was composed by the poet Narapati in the famous city of Sovalli, during the reign of the illustrious King Ajayapala. **|| 6 ||** 

Even when seeing a beautiful young woman with a fair body, a moon-like face, her mouth filled with betel leaves, adorned with pearls and white garlands, and anointed with sandalwood—even if she is filled with desire—the practitioner should visualize a serpent before him. Thus, the mind of a man is restrained even in the presence of temptation. **|| 7 ||** 

Thus ends the praise of the poet in the *Narapati-jayacharya-svarodaya*. The meaning of this is easy to understand, and thus no further commentary is provided here as it is unnecessary.

<summary>This page serves as the formal opening of the Narapati-jayacharya-svarodaya, featuring an introductory commentary and the author's lineage. It establishes the text's divine authority by tracing its titles through the four cosmic ages (Yugas) and identifying the author, Narapati, as the son of the polymath Naradeva.</summary>

<keywords>Narapati, Naradeva, Svarodaya, King Ajayapala, Yugas, Shiva-Bhaskara, Saraswati, divination, astrology, Tantra, lineage, Sovalli, Dharapuri</keywords>

Ja. Ti. <note>likely abbreviations for Narapati-Tika and Jayalakshmi-Tika</note>
</margin>

Now the author describes his own mastery through study: "He should act," etc. **|| 7 ||** This refers to the meaning of the vowels. Next, he speaks of the primary importance of the power of vowels (Svara): "In truth," etc. **|| 8 ||** Indeed, this practitioner of Svara <note>original: *Svarodaya-svarajña*</note> who knows the science of sound/breath thoroughly is a master of astrology. A king who is devoid of this knowledge, even if he possesses a vanguard of elephants, a vast army, and is a conqueror of the world, will meet with total defeat in the midst of battle and flee, let alone a king with a small army. **|| 22 ||**

Next, he describes the power of a king who possesses the strength of Svara: "As long as the heroes cross," etc. Those brave warriors—the sons of the Svara-knowers, enriched by the power of sound—cross over the "Ocean of Conflict" <note>original: *Aahava-sagara*</note>, reaching the far shore of battle only as long as the state of Svara remains, which grants victory without obstacles. For that duration, through the strength of their arms, they are like the God Vishnu <note>original: *Vela-Vishnu*</note> at the shore; they are the lords of the fortresses, the masters of the neck, possessing peace, activity, and the power of the "Dunkara" sound <note>a mystical phonetic vibration</note>. They remain established in sound, form, taste, touch, and smell. For that long, they conquer even without a lord; how much more so when equipped with swords and other weapons? By the statement "As long as," the connection is explained: "Until" the state of Svara is achieved, which acts like the "Submarine Fire" <note>term: *Badvanala* - a mythical fire under the ocean that consumes everything</note>. 

> "Skanda-yamala, Vasishtha-yamala, and Kurma-yamala; the seventh Yamala is known as such—these are the seven Yamalas. **|| 6 ||**
> Having heard these seven Yamalas, O Goddess, as well as the 'Ocean of Victory in War' <note>original: *Yuddha-jayarnava*</note>, the 'Kaumari,' the 'Kaushela,' and the 'Circulation of the Yogini-web' <note>original: *Yogini-jala-sancharana*</note>. **|| 7 ||**
> The 'Rakshoghna,' the 'Trimunda,' the 'Lion of Sound' <note>original: *Svara-simha*</note>, and the 'Ocean of Sound' <note>original: *Svararnava*</note>. The chapter named 'Bhairava-bala' in the 'Svara-bhairava.' **|| 8 ||**
> These are the jewels of the Tantras, the established doctrines, and the path to victory. Thus, I have seen the astrology in the Shris-darshana. **|| 9 ||**
> The three mantras, many secret methods of warfare, the application of Tantra, the future, and the science of the Submarine Fire. **|| 10 ||**
> I have personally extracted the heart-essence of all these sciences; this will be the gateway to salvation <note>original: *Tara-dvara*</note>, out of compassion for all living beings." **|| 11 ||**
> Even with a vanguard of elephants, a king without the mastery of Svara meets with defeat in the army's movement. **|| 22 ||**
> The brave ones cross the terrifying ocean of battle only as long as they are not caught in the cycle of the "White Submarine Fire" <note>original: *Sita-badvanala*</note> of the vowels. **|| 13 ||**
> <gloss>When the Svara is dead, even so in the Submarine Fire.</gloss>

<margin>Pa.</margin>
The "Rising of the Master" <note>original: *Svammatrodaya*</note> refers to the measure or timing. The sequence of measures, the capture of life-force, and the count of sounds mentioned in the Svara science—the "Ocean of Being" is that ocean of battle. In that cycle of the Submarine Fire, like a whirlpool, they do not fall; as long as they possess the strength of Svara, they reach the far shore of the conflict. Or rather, when the time of the Svara is lost in battle, the warrior perishes in the ocean of conflict. Just as men who are strong through the power of their arms cross the ocean, they cross the "Ocean of Measures" <note>original: *Matra-samudra*</note> for as long as they have strength. If they fall into the land of the Submarine Fire, they are destroyed—this is well known. For a king, the "rising" of the Svara is the primary cause of victory. Therefore, those who wish to be victorious must study the science of *Svarodaya*. **|| 23 ||**

Next, he explains how victory can occur even without Svara: "Some," etc. This is easy to understand. **|| 24 ||** Furthermore, he sings about the one who is devoid of Svara...

<vocab>स्वरोदय (Swarodaya): The science of the rising breath or phonetic vibrations used for divination. यामल (Yamala): A class of major Tantric scriptures, usually eight in number. बडवानल (Badvanala): The mythical "submarine fire" at the south pole that drinks the ocean; here used as a metaphor for a destructive military formation or cosmic cycle. संग्राम (Sangrama): Battle or conflict. युद्धजयार्णव (Yuddhajayarnava): "The Ocean of Victory in War," a specific technical text. ज्योतिष (Jyotisha): Traditional Indian astrology and astronomy.</vocab>

# || Now, once again, the explanation of the etymology of the Sound-Illumination 26 || Now, the one possessing Sound speaks 27 ||

A person of great essence <note>original: *saaro*</note> shines forth; then, that person overcomes the enemy through his very presence. **|| 15 ||** Why say "one by ten"? Even a single king who possesses the power of sound/breath <term>Svara: literally 'sound' or 'vowel,' here referring to the mystical science of breath control and phonetic divination</term> can defeat ten kings with the ease of child’s play, for he conquers with the strength of a hundred. **|| 16 ||** With the strength of the "sound-forced" kings, he conquers a hundred enemies; thus with hundreds, he conquers thousands. This alone is the true measure of victory. **|| 17 ||** Now, the author provides proof for the aforementioned path of sound. Even with five—what does this mean? Even if a warrior is lacking in physical soldiers, if he is lacking in the knowledge of the "vowels" (Svara), he should not enter into battle. How can one who cannot even protect himself fight against an armed opponent? However, even if he is alone, if he is empowered by the harmony of the sound-energies, he becomes powerful. A master of the <term>Svarodaya: the science of the rising breath/sound</term> possesses the strength of mind and mantra; such a person can fight against millions of weapon-wielding soldiers. **|| 18 ||** Now, he speaks of the necessity of checking the sound-charts for diseases and other afflictions. He says "the Sound..." **|| 19 ||** The sound-charts **|| 20 ||** In the "Yamala" texts <note>a class of Tantric scriptures</note> **|| 21 ||** The string of measures **|| 22 ||** The twelve zodiac signs, etc. **|| 23 ||** The knower of earth and time **|| 24 ||** This is easy to understand. Thus are the names of the charts of the "Svarodaya." In this way, there are 25... eighty-one... **|| 26 ||**

---

How can a king be victorious in war without a knower of sound? Knowing this, it must be understood like the <term>Ghata-chakra: a 'pot-chart' used in astrology to determine stability</term>. **|| 24 ||** If a king does not have even one person in his household who has mastered the <term>Chara-shastra: the science of the movement of breath and planets</term>, his stability will fall like the trunk of a banana tree <note>which is hollow and easily toppled</note>. **|| 25 ||** The knower of sound is a speaker of truth and has conquered his senses. He who is the creator of his own destiny and country <margin>gives</margin> will be served by the unmoving Goddess of Victory. **|| 26 ||** One person kills ten, ten kill a hundred, and a hundred kill a thousand by the power of the Svarodaya alone; he kills ten-fold as if in play. **|| 27 ||** Without the five [vowels/elements], one should not engage in battle, as one is defeated in the Svarodaya. But with the power of the Svarodaya, one can fight against millions. **|| 28 ||** The knower of sound, the knower of omens, the astrologer, and the master of mantras; the "Kerala" system and the "Vishamadhara"—these five are proclaimed as the essentials. **|| 29 ||** The sound-charts and the various diagrams are powerful. In astrology and the study of omens, there are six limbs [of knowledge]. **|| 2 ||** In all the "Yamala" texts, other things are said regarding the Svarodaya. Now I shall speak of them: there are twenty sound-charts. **|| 1 ||** The *Matravali* (String of Measures), the *Rahu-jiva* (The Life of the North Node), the *Yogaka* (The Union chart); the chart of the twelve zodiac signs, the solstices, the seasons, and the monthly charts. **|| 2 ||** The seven-day chart, the three-fold union charts; the chart for the current time of war, and the "Rising Body" of sound. **|| 23 ||** These are the sound-charts. The *Simhasana* (Throne) chart, and the five kinds of *Kurma* (Tortoise) charts. **|| 25 ||** For the region, city, field, house, and the "Pot" <note>Kumbha</note> is the fifth. These are the "Colliding" and "Instant" Fire-of-Time charts. **|| 28 ||** Three types of constellations <term>Nakshatra: lunar mansions used in Vedic astrology</term>, and the three-fold "disturbed arts." The "Earth-roamer," the "Sky-roamer," and the three-fold "Nadi" (Channel) charts. **|| 30 ||** The Wheel of Time <note>Kala-chakra</note>, the elephant-steed, and the moon-born. The "Poet's Chart" <note>Kavi-chakra</note> is said to be two-fold, concerning local lordship. **|| 31 ||** Work to be done is two-fold, such as the place of birth and so on. The "Astrologer’s Station" chart and the "Music-Time" chart. **|| 35 ||** These are...

## Now, I shall speak clearly of the "Fixed Powers" by their names.

For a king seeking victory in the great battle involving the fourfold army <term>Chaturanga: the traditional Indian military division of elephants, chariots, cavalry, and infantry</term>, I now declare these powers. **|| 37 ||** When the enemy is equal or superior in strength, those seeking victory should utilize the strength of the "Fixed Powers" <term>Dhruvala: presiding mystical energies or fixed points of power used in divination</term>. **|| 38 ||** I shall state their names as they are famously known in the *Jayayamala* <note>a specific Tantric scripture dedicated to victory</note>. **Now, the praise of the Fixed Powers (Dhruvalas) is given.**
As for the text starting with "In one's own..." <note>verse 37</note>: This refers to the names of the powers. If the enemy is equal in wrestling or superior in strength based on the vowels of their name, the time, the direction, and so on, then the "Fixed Strength" is the only source of power for kings seeking victory. **38** 

The main point is that victory or defeat depends on the harmony or enmity of the "Svara" <term>Svara: the mystical science of breath and phonetic patterns</term> between oneself and the enemy. If the enemy's strength appears superior, then by the king’s own Svara-strength, he may still achieve victory over the enemy's kingdom. In this battle, just as a king is anxious for the victory of his horses—thinking, "How shall I conquer him?"—he should look to the Fixed Powers through the science of Svara and then make the effort for war. 

"Victory, victory!"—he thus lists the names of those Fixed Powers.

## The Science of the Stars

I shall now speak of the Three-Branched Astrology <term>Triskandha Jyotisha: the traditional division of astrology into mathematical calculation, omen-reading, and horoscopy</term> within the context of the "Rise of the Sound of Victory" <note>original: *Jayacharya-svarodaya*</note>, including the calculations for the day and night. **|| 61 ||**

The results of the unions of the Moon and Sun, the heights and depths of the wheel. The span of life, the planetary periods <term>Dasha: specific periods of time in a person's life ruled by a particular planet</term>, and the time of their entry. **|| 63 ||** 

From that, the form of the Ascendant, and the results of gambling, trade, and battle. **|| 64 ||** The Eight-fold Division <term>Ashtakavarga: a system of points used to predict daily results</term> is made clear; from that, the daily results shall manifest. **|| 64 ||** 

As stated in the Tajika system <note>original: *Tajika*: a branch of Indian astrology influenced by Perso-Arabic traditions</note>, the clarity of the birth-ascendant and wealth. The year, month, and day-ascendant, and the results of their respective planetary periods. **|| 65 ||**

**Thus ends the first chapter, the "Compendium of the Shastra," according to the Jayalakshmī <note>the name of the primary commentary on this text</note>. || 1 ||**

Now, after this <note>original: *athāta*</note>, following the Compendium of the Shastra, I shall explain the rising of the vowels <term>Svara: the vowels of the Sanskrit alphabet, viewed here as cosmic vibrations</term>, which are differentiated by their "measures" <note>original: *matra*, referring to the duration of the sound</note> and other qualities, as proclaimed in the *Brahma-yamala* <note>an ancient Tantric scripture</note>. This is the connection to the next section. **|| 2 ||**

Now, regarding the sixteen "risings" of the vowels: The vowels are the foundation of sound/resonance. It was said of old that there are sixteen vowels in the *Matrika* <note>original: *Matrika-cakra*, the "Mother Wheel" or matrix of the alphabet</note>. These vowels are: 
A, Ā, I, Ī, U, Ū, Ṛ, Ṝ, Ḷ, Ḹ, E, Ai, O, Au, Aṃ, Aḥ. 
Among these, the sounds 'A' and 'Ka' are to be set aside... <note>The text here describes technical phonetic manipulations used to calculate auspicious timings</note> 

...The measure of the day, the portion of the day already passed at sunrise, and the calculation of the light. **|| 66 ||** 

The light consists of sixty-three parts, and the visible light is twenty-three parts. The calculation of the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific time</term> should be done according to one's own location. **|| 67 ||** 

From that, one should determine the Ascendant's measure, then the *Hora* <note>half of a zodiac sign</note>, the *Drekkana* <note>one-third of a sign</note>, the *Saptamsha* <note>one-seventh</note>, the *Navamsha* <note>one-ninth</note>, the *Dwadashamsha* <note>one-twelfth</note>, and the *Trishamsha* <note>one-thirtieth</note>, along with their respective degrees. **|| 68 ||** 

I shall now speak on the section regarding the Lunar Day <term>Tithi: a lunar day, roughly 1/30th of a lunar month</term>, as well as the Weekday, the Constellation <term>Nakshatra: the 27 or 28 lunar mansions</term>, and the *Yoga*. 
Also, the *Karana* <note>half a Tithi</note>, the auspicious moment <term>Muhurta: a unit of time roughly 48 minutes long</term>, and the Solar Transit <note>original: *sankramo*, when the sun moves into a new zodiac sign</note>. **|| 69 ||** 

The strength of the Moon and the Stars, accompanied by the distinction of the lunar fortnights <note>waxing and waning</note>, as well as the good and bad results arising from the combinations of Lunar Days and Weekdays. **|| 70 ||** 

The effort for success, the nature of the weekdays, and the *Tripushkara* yoga <note>a specific alignment said to triple the effects of actions performed during it</note> occurring on "low" or "three-day" lunar dates. **|| 71 ||** 

The *Yamaghanta* <note>the "Bell of Death," an inauspicious time</note>, the *Yamadrishta*, the *Argala*, and the *Vata*. Also the "Kumarantara" gem and the evil results of the *Shruti-yogini* omens. **|| 72 ||** 

The *Yamakola* of the Weekday and Constellation, the *Kalavela* <note>appointed time of death or fate</note>, and the *Kulika* and *Kantaka* <note>specific astrological points or "thorns" that denote obstacles</note>. **|| 73 ||** 

The results of the planetary positions, the paths of the years, the "bite" or impact of the major planetary periods <term>Dasha: a system of planetary timing in Vedic astrology</term>, and the effects of the movements and appearances of the world. **|| 74 ||** 

The places of suffering for the planets, <margin>75 ||</margin> 
as well as the "Great States" <note>original: *mahavastha*, describing the condition of a planet, e.g., "sleeping" or "awake"</note>. The results of the astrological houses, the strength of the junctions, and the "piercing" <note>original: *vedham*, an astrological aspect of interference</note> of the stars. 

The progress of the country and the movement of the planets <term>Go-chara: the transit of planets through the zodiac signs</term>. The entry into a country, the taking of an image, initiation <note>original: *diksha*</note>, travel, and entering a new house. **|| 76 ||** 

The deity of the site, the arrangement of time, and the specific distinctions. The "Water-yoga," the half-year results, and the results of the months, days, and directions. **|| 77 ||** 

The five-fold *Nivya* and others, and that which is born of the immediate moment. The strength of the rising position, and the power of the Moon. **|| 78 ||** 

All the twitching of limbs, and whatever is purified by time. The place of the "breath-uprising" <note>original: *uddhara-sthana*</note>, the *Havali*, and the rituals for peace <note>original: *shantike*</note>. **|| 79 ||** 

Wise men who know all these "limbs of astrology" <note>original: *jyotishangam*</note> should be known as those who possess a lamp for the destruction of darkness. **|| 80 ||** 

Thus ends the astrology section. I shall now speak of the "Five Gems" as proclaimed in the Shastras <note>the sacred teachings</note>: *Kotaki*, *Pingala*, *Kaka* (The Crow), *Yaksha*, and *Shiva*. **|| 81 ||** 

Thus it is known by the wise. **|| 82 ||** 

**Thus ends the first chapter, the "Compendium of the Shastra," in the seventh section of the Six Limbs of the Svarodaya in the illustrious Narapati-jayacarya.**

--- Page 44 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of "Svarodaya" (the science of sound-breath divination). Following the previous mention of the *Brahma-yamala* scripture, the text now details the specific phonetic groupings of the Sanskrit alphabet and their correspondence with deities and cosmic forces. It establishes the metaphysical foundation for how sounds reveal fate.</meta>

<lang>English</lang>
<warning>The manuscript shows significant ink bleed-through from the reverse side (verso), making some areas slightly cluttered visually, though the primary text remains legible.</warning>

These are known as the sixteen vowels <note>original: *svara*</note>: a, ā, i, ī, u, ū, ṛ, ṝ, ḷ, ḹ, e, ai, o, au, aṃ, aḥ. Among these sixteen vowels, one should take them in pairs. Among them are a, i, u, ṛ, ḷ. The secondary ones are not equal to the primary elements. Now, regarding those ten that stand in pairs, the first two of each pair are grasped. It is said there: the letter **"A"** is Vasudeva <note>a name for Lord Krishna or the Supreme Being</note> because of his primacy; thus **"A"** alone is taken. The letter **"I"** is Lakshmi <note>the goddess of prosperity</note>; she is the beloved of Vasudeva, and therefore **"I"** is taken.

---

**|| Now, therefore, I shall fully declare the rising of the sixteen vowels, beginning with the letter "A," which are differentiated by their "measures" <note>duration of sound</note> and other divisions. || 1 ||**

**Those which are called the supreme *Matrika* <term>Matrika: "The Mothers," referring to the letters of the alphabet as the source of all creation</term> and the vowels are full of the power of the scriptures. Sages, both male and female <note>referring to the masculine and feminine aspects of the letters</note>, know their fundamental value. || 2 ||**

**In those sixteen vowels, each should be considered as a pair. Among them, the internal vowels are the five vowels used in the "Rising of Sounds" <term>Svarodaya: a system of divination based on which vowel or breath-stream is dominant</term>. || 3 ||**

**Gain and loss, happiness and misery, life as well as death, victory and even defeat—all of these are found within the "Rising of Sounds." || 4 ||**

**The pronunciation of the *Matrika* begins with the vowels; the three worlds are pervaded by the *Matrika*. Therefore, in the "Rising of Sounds":**

->**|| All the three worlds, including the moving and the unmoving, are contained. || 5 ||**<-

---

The letter **"U"** is Rudra <note>a fierce form of Shiva</note>; the letter **"E"** is the Sun; the letter **"O"** is the Moon. Therefore, following the lead of the letter **"A"**, only the five short vowels are taken. Because it is from these five that the others are manifested. Without the presence of the letter **"A"**, the assembly of the gods <note>or "the temple of letters"</note> cannot be attained. Because the letter **"A"** exists, therefore all the gods reside even within the letter **"A"**. Understand this also from the letter **"I"**. **|| 2 ||**

Regarding "The internal vowels among them" **|| 3 ||**: The text reveals this greatness twice. Regarding "Gain and loss, happiness and misery": One should recognize gain or loss through the "rising" of these vowels. Similarly, by the words "happiness and misery," one should understand health or the strike of disease, and by "life and death," one should understand the span of life and the occurrence of death. In this way, in every divination <note>original: *prashna*, a specific question asked of an astrologer</note>, the vowels alone are the revealers of the fruit <note>the result or outcome</note>. **|| 4 ||**

The text states the absolute primacy of the vowels: "The *Matrika* begins with the vowels." The pronunciation of all things, moving and unmoving, when grasping an object or desire, is only through the *Matrika* beginning with **"A"**. Truly, without the *Matrika* starting with **"A"**, it is impossible to utter a single syllable. Thus, the world is pervaded by the *Matrika*. Therefore, in a divination, the vowels alone are the revealers of the outcome. **|| 5 ||**

Now, the text again reveals the primacy of the vowels: "Those beginning with A." **A, I, U, Ṛ, Ḷ**—these five vowels are Brahma and the other deities. There, **A** is Brahma <note>the Creator</note>, **I** is Vishnu <note>the Preserver</note>, **U** is Rudra <note>the Destroyer</note>, **E** is the Sun, and **O** is the Moon, and so on. Another meaning: regarding productivity, in the rising of the vowel **"A"**, one gains clothing and performs works. In the vowel **"I"**, one should meditate on the divine Vishnu.

<summary>This page establishes the phonetic theology of Svarodaya, mapping the sixteen Sanskrit vowels to major deities like Vasudeva, Lakshmi, Brahma, and Vishnu. It asserts that the five short vowels (a, i, u, ṛ, ḷ) are the primary forces governing all worldly outcomes, including life, death, and victory.</summary>
<keywords>Svarodaya, Matrika, Phonetic Mysticism, Vowels, Vasudeva, Lakshmi, Brahma, Vishnu, Rudra, Divination, Sanskrit Grammar, Breath Science</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of the phonetic system used in divination. While the previous page established the primary vowels and their association with figures like Vasudeva and Lakshmi, this section introduces the "Kalas" (powers or phases) and the "Bhutas" (elements) that correspond to the five main vowels: A, I, U, E, and O.</meta>

When the sound **"U"** <note>original: *ukāra*</note> arises, one should meditate on Rudra <note>a fierce form of Shiva</note>; at the rising of **"E"**, on the Sun; and at the rising of **"O"**, on the Moon. Thus, in those instances, the five stages known as the **"Kalas"** <term>Kala: literally "part" or "digit," here referring to specific energies or phases of manifestation</term> are present, beginning with Cessation. At the rising of **"A"** is the phase called **Cessation** <term>Nivrtti</term>; for **"I"**, the phase of **Foundation** <term>Pratistha</term>; for **"U"**, the phase of **Knowledge** <term>Vidya</term>; for **"E"**, the phase of **Peace** <term>Shanti</term>; and in **"O"**, there is **Supreme Peace** <term>Atishanti or Shantyatita</term>.

By the word "Cessation" <note>original: *nivrtti*</note>, the cessation of a journey <note>returning home or a trip not happening</note> is meant. If a question is asked during the rising of **"A"**, one should say that the matter will constantly cease or return. At the rising of **"I"**, a counter-measure or remedy occurs. At the rising of **"U"**, the questioner should meditate and then seek knowledge. At the rising of **"E"**, having meditated on the Sun, peace and rest shall come. At the rising of **"O"**, having meditated on the Moon, the result is "Nishanta" <note>end of night/clarity</note> and supreme peace. One should observe the nature of the question and the "seal" <note>mudra</note> along with the five-fold Power of Will <note>Iccha-shakti</note>. The five "mouths" <note>openings/sources</note> beginning with **"A"** are joined with the five powers: **Will**, **Knowledge**, **Splendor** <gloss>prabha</gloss>, **Faith** <gloss>shraddha</gloss>, and **Intelligence** <gloss>medha</gloss>. Whatever arises then depends on that specific connection. || 6 || 

Now, regarding the fixed "measures" <note>matra</note> of the vowels: the eight vowels beginning with **"A"** are used in the sequence of travel. The "Four-fold Wheel" is identical to that Illusion <note>Maya</note>. In that context, at the rising of **"A"**, the circle is spherical...

---

->The five vowels and the five powers of the goddesses, beginning with Cessation,<-
->are the five phases and the five-fold Power of Will. || 6 || In the irregular order of the measures,<-
->there are the five lower lords. These are said to be the objects of the senses,<-
->such as sound, driven by desire. || 7 || The Body, the Word, the Form, and that which is Beyond Form—the Stainless.<-
->The knowledge situated in the differentiation of vowels is always learned from a Teacher. || 8 ||<-
->There are five vowels beginning with "A"; of these, the "Aham" <note>the 'I-consciousness'</note> is spoken.<-
->The "Living Self" <term>Jiva</term> is the measure and the color, joined with the body of the zodiac signs. || 9 ||<-
->By which the "Creeping One" <note>perhaps referring to the breath or the serpent power, Kundalini</note> speaks, and by which things are called and arrive;<-
->that is the "Circlet of Names," accompanied by the measures and vowels. || 10 ||<-
->Thus ends the Wheel of Vowel Measures. || 1 ||<-

---

Regarding the "Circle of Tastes" <note>rasa-cakra</note> mentioned previously: when **"A"** arises, one should worship in that circle. For **"I"**, a two-fold circle; for **"U"**, a triangle; for **"E"**, a hexagon; and for **"O"**, a perfect circle <note>vartula</note>.

In the vowels beginning with **"A"**, the five elements <term>Bhuta-panchaka</term> dwell, starting with Earth. At the rising of **"A"**, the question concerns Earth; for **"I"**, it concerns Water; for **"U"**, Fire <note>original: *nala*, meaning 'the burning one'</note>; for **"A"** <note>likely an error in the manuscript for "E"</note>, it concerns Air; and for **"O"**, it concerns the Ether or Space. If the breath or sound moves upward, one should not speak <note>avoid a prediction</note>. These vowels also relate to the steady sensory objects like smell: **"A"** is the object of **Smell**; **"I"** is **Taste**; **"U"** is **Form/Sight**; **"E"** is **Touch**; and **"O"** is **Sound**.

Therefore, when a vowel beginning with **"A"** arises, one should speak about the specific subject of the query as stated above. This meaning is known only from the instructions of a Teacher <term>Guru</term>. Shri Partha <note>the student or patron</note> has not explained anything for his own sake; rather, a person understands this through the guidance of a great Teacher and by the grace of one’s chosen deity. As he will say: "The Body, the Word," etc. Here, the "measure" is revealed. The author shows the nature of the **Body** <note>pinda</note>, the **Word** <note>pada</note>, and the **Form** <note>rupa</note> to explain the true meaning. Through this, what is achieved? The mastery of vowels through constant practice. || 7 ||

Now he teaches the primary purpose of the vowels beginning with **"A"**. Between the five vowels and the two types of "measures" <note>short and long</note>, there are various divisions, and the fifth becomes the two-fold circle. || 8 || There, he teaches this vowel-circle: "By which the Creeping One speaks..." by which name the collection of meanings is spoken, or by which knowledge is or is not communicated. That "Circlet of Names" involves the vowel measures... <unclear>atihe</unclear>

It explains how a practitioner can answer questions about travel, health, or success by identifying which sound or elemental breath is dominant at the time of the query.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of phonetic divination. Having previously established the elemental and deity associations of the five primary vowels (A, I, U, E, O), the text now shifts to the practical methodology of extracting these "vowel measures" from a person's name or a location to predict outcomes.</meta>

<page-num>73</page-num>
<warning>Mild staining and foxing throughout the page, but the text remains clearly legible. Red/orange ink is used extensively for emphasis and section highlighting.</warning>

-> 🌀 <-

In this system, the vowel measure <note>original: *mātrā svara*</note> of a name is said to be the vowel **"E"**. Wherever a name begins with the letter **"V"**, its characteristic is determined by the **"A"** sound that follows it; thus, the vowel measure there is **"A"**. This applies to all names. For subtle names and auspicious titles, which vowel measure should be grasped? In this teaching, for such names, the **"I"** vowel is the measure. This applies to names like **"Devi"** or **"Vena."** Furthermore, as demonstrated in the *Mahabhashya* <note>The "Great Commentary" on Sanskrit grammar by Patanjali, c. 2nd century BCE</note>, the vowel **"Ṛ"** <note>a vocalic 'r' sound</note> is composed partly of the **"I"** sound; therefore, the vowel measure for **"Ṛ"** is taken as **"I"**. ||10|| **Here follows the Vowel Measure Diagram** <note>original: *mātrā svara cakram*</note>. ||11||

**The first letters and vowel measures** from the letter **"Ka"** to **"Ha"** <note>the full range of Sanskrit consonants</note> are organized by the primary vowels: **A, I, U, E, O**. Between these, one should write the rows of consonants starting from **"Ka"** and ending with **"Ha"**. One should omit the fifth letter of each consonant group <note>the nasal sounds like ṅ, ñ, ṇ, n, m</note> and write the remaining letters in a grid of thirty-five cells. After creating these thirty-five cells ||35|| **the diagram is as follows:** ||12|| **Regarding the first letter of a person’s name:** In this method of letter-calculation, if the first letter of a name is the vowel **"O"**, then the vowel-sound for that person is **"O"**. If a name begins with the **"Ya"** group <note>the semi-vowels Y, R, L, V</note>, for instance the letter **"Y"**, it is placed under the vowel **"U"**; thus, the vowel-sound for such a name is **"U"**. ||12|| **Regarding the negation:** If the consonants from **"Ka"** to **"Ha"** are written in the thirty-five cells in horizontal rows ||13|| **the vowel under which the first letter of a person's name resides is known as the Vowel-Sound <term>Varna Svara: the specific vowel assigned to a consonant for divination</term> for that name.** ||12|| If the letters are not found in the primary positions, they should be understood in the order of the consonant groups (Ga, Ja, Da, etc.). ||13|| **If a name contains a conjunct consonant** <term>Samyukta Varna: a cluster of two or more consonants without intervening vowels, like 'str'</term>, then according to the *Brahmayamala* <note>An ancient and influential Tantric scripture</note>, the first letter of that cluster is to be used. ||14|| <insert>73</insert>

---

In different regions or subjects, when these five letters occur, the maturation of their effects is not equal across the groups of letters. ||13|| Now, another method is described regarding names: If anyone’s name begins with a conjunct consonant, which vowel-sound should be taken? In cases like the name **"Shripati"**, the letter **"Sh"** is the first. Because the vowel **"I"** is attached to the cluster, the vowel-sound for **"Shripati"** is **"I"**. This logic should be applied everywhere. 

Now, if a person has many names, which one should be used to determine the vowel measure? Regarding this doubt, those who know the secret of sounds <note>original: *svarajña*</note> refer to the teachings of **Lampatacharya** <note>A specialized authority or teacher of this specific divination tradition</note>. For those with many names, the one that is most common or easy should be chosen by the experts. Regarding the high realization of other vowel-sounds... [technical details on phonetic pairings follow]. 

Now, concerning the determination of the sound of a house: **Lampatacharya** states: When the birth name or other details are unknown, one should use the first letter of the name of the country, the village, the city, the palace, or the person. The result of the vowel-sound will then be clearly seen. The technical names for these vowel measures are found in the *Yamala* texts. ||14|| <gloss>99</gloss> <gloss>659</gloss>
Victory or defeat in these subjects is determined by these five. ||14||

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of "Svara Shastra" (the science of phonetic sound divination). Having previously discussed name-vowels, the text now introduces the "Dead Vowels" and the "Planet-Vowel Chart," which links phonetic sounds to the movements of the planets and zodiac signs to determine auspicious timing and measurements.</meta>

<page-num>5</page-num>

**The "dead vowels"** <note>original: *mṛtasvarā*, referring to vowels that lack potency in certain divinatory contexts</note> **are described as follows || 15 || Regarding the measures of the twelve-fold divisions and others, I shall state the definitions and measurements as found in the traditional sciences || 16 || These take the form of doubt || 17 || And the forms of the "Elephant"** <note>original: *gajarūpāṇi*, likely referring to a specific numerical pattern or architectural measurement</note> **|| 18 ||**
**Regarding these measurements: For the twelve-fold human rising** <note>original: *nrodaya*, the time it takes for a sign to rise on the horizon</note> **the measures in *palas*** <note>a *pala* is a traditional unit of time, approximately 24 seconds</note> **are 14, 13, 8, 9, 8. For the annual human rising, the measures in *palas* are 11, 10, 8, 9, 18. For the solstice movement, the measures are 5, 5, 20, 8. For the eight-fold division, the measures are 2, 3, 5, 6, 3. For the monthly human rising, the measures in *palas* are 9, 8, 9, 7, 7. For the general human rising, the measures are 4, 20, 5. At the end of the day, the measures are 3, 2, 8. The human rising measure for a *ghati*** <note>a *ghati* is 24 minutes</note> **is 30 *palas*. Thus ends section 18.**

->**Now begins the Planet-Vowel Chart** <note>original: *grahasvaracakram*</note>** ||**<-

**Here, the vowel itself is the key || 20 || Regarding the "A-vowel" || 21 || One should establish the vowel as the primary element 21 || The "A-vowel" corresponds to Aries, Leo, and Scorpio** <note>original: *meṣa siṃhālī*; *ali* is a synonym for Scorpio</note>**. What does this mean? It means the "A-vowel" takes the form of the signs Aries, Leo, and Scorpio. These signs are considered "lower" or nocturnal in nature, carrying the breadth of the night. Whichever vowel rules a sign, the lord of that sign becomes the ruler of the vowel. For example: the "A-vowel" takes the form of Aries, Leo, and Scorpio. The lord of Aries and Scorpio is Mars** <note>original: *Bhauma*</note>**, and the lord of Leo is the Sun** <note>original: *Sūrya*</note>**.**

> A vowel is "dead" when it is weak, but "young" or "princely" <note>original: *kumāro*</note> when it is strong. The "Prince" is said to be the most powerful here || 15 || In the sciences, that which exceeds twelve is considered highly desirable || 16 || Seven doubts are reached in the measurement || 16 || One should know the values and the "Elephant-form" in *palas* for the solstice; these are connected to the self and the eyes <note>metaphorical for observation</note> || 17 || The Elephant-form is declared for the measurement of the month. Nine full, nine divisions, and three-part measurements || 18 || This relates to the measurement of the day in Aries and the sequence of time. The measure of a *ghati* is the human rising vowel || 19 || **Now, the Planet-Vowels ||** The "A-vowel" is Aries, Leo, and Scorpio. The "I-vowel" is Virgo, Gemini, and Scorpio <note>The text likely means Cancer/Karka here, as Scorpio was already assigned</note>. The "U-vowel" is Sagittarius and Pisces. The "E-vowel" is Libra and Taurus || 20 || The Moon and Venus, the Sun and Mars, Mercury and Venus, and the Minister <note>Jupiter</note>, and Saturn, and Jupiter are the lords of Aries and the other signs || 22 || ->**Thus ends the Planet-Vowel Chart.**<-

**The Sun and Mars are the lords of the "A-vowel." The lord of Virgo and Gemini is Mercury. The lord of Cancer is the Moon. Therefore, Mercury and the Moon are the lords of the "I-vowel." The lord of Sagittarius and Pisces is Jupiter; thus, Jupiter is the ruler of the "U-vowel." Venus is the lord of Taurus and Libra, making it the ruler of the "E-vowel." Saturn is the lord of Capricorn and Aquarius; thus, Saturn is the ruler of the "O-vowel." Now, regarding the constellations** <note>original: *nakṣatra*</note> **of the twelve zodiac signs: in the "E-vowel" chart, the constellation Revati corresponds to the sign of Pisces. The "U-vowel" also relates to Pisces. The lord of Pisces is Jupiter, and Jupiter is the lord of the "O-vowel"** <note>The text here shows some variation in its internal logic, common in manuscript traditions</note>**. Similarly, for other vowels. Regarding the constellation Jyeshtha: its vowel is "A." The sign of Jyeshtha is Scorpio. Since Scorpio takes the form of the "A-vowel" and its lord is Mars, the planet-vowel for that person is "A."** ->**Thus ends the Planet-Vowel Chart.**<-

<meta>This page transitions from the general theory of vowels to the practical application of the three major "Chakras" or astrological charts. This continues the technical exposition of phonetic divination (Svara Shastra) from the previous page.</meta>

->**|| The Chart of Letter-Vowels ||**<- ->**|| The Chart of Planet-Vowels ||**<- ->**|| The Chart of Life-Vowels ||**<-
