Regarding the sixteen letters || 1 || The letters of the *avagadhadi* <note>original: *avagādyaya*, a traditional grouping of phonemes used for indexing</note> categories are illustrated using the name **Devadatta**. 

**The Calculation:**
For the letter **Da**, the number is 3. 
For the vowel **E** placed above the letter **Da**, the number is 11. 
For the letter **Va**, the number is 4. 
For the vowel **A** within the letter **Va**, the number is 1. 
For the letter **Da**, the number is 3. 
For the conjunct letter **Tta** <note>represented by two 'Ta's</note>, the number is 2. 
For the final vowel **A**, the number is 1. 

The sum of these is twenty-six (26). When this sum is divided by five <note>original: *pañcabhartte*, referring to the five main vowels A, I, U, E, O</note>, the remainder is 1. Therefore, for the person named **Devadatta**, the **Life-Vowel** <note>original: *jīva svara*</note> is **A** || 1 || 

**Thus ends the Life-Vowel Chart.**

**Now begins the Zodiac Chart.** <note>original: *rāśicakram*</note>

> The groups of sixteen letters are established; the five categories starting with 'Ka' follow. The categories of numbers are proclaimed in the sets of four. The eight-fold categories starting with 'A' and 'La' are determined by the numbers in the name || 1 ||
> By the number of the letter found in the first syllable of the name, known through the vowel of that letter: divide by five, and the remainder is known as the **Life-Vowel** || 2 || 

**Thus ends the Life-Vowel Chart.**

> Aries and Taurus are associated with the 'A' vowel; Gemini consists of six parts <note>original: *ṣaḍaṃśakā*, referring to Navamshas or ninth-divisions of a sign</note>. For the 'I' vowel, there are three parts of Gemini, along with Leo and Cancer || 1 ||
> For the 'U' vowel, Virgo and Libra, and the first three parts of Scorpio. For the 'E' vowel, the remaining six parts of Scorpio, and the first six parts of Sagittarius and Capricorn || 2 ||
> For the 'O' vowel, the remaining parts of Capricorn, along with Aquarius and Pisces. Thus the **Zodiac-Vowels** are proclaimed according to the rising order of the <term>Navamshas</term> <note>the 1/9th division of a zodiac sign, used for precision in Indian astrology</note> || 3 || 

**Thus ends the Zodiac-Vowel Chart.**

Two... meaning the **Aries** <term>Navamshas</term> are 9, **Taurus** are 9, and **Gemini** are 6, totaling 24. 
Regarding the three parts of Gemini: the 3 remaining parts of **Gemini**, **Cancer** (9), and **Leo** (9) total 21. 
Regarding Virgo and Libra: **Virgo** (9), **Libra** (9), and 3 parts of **Scorpio** total 21. 
Regarding the 'E' vowel: 6 parts of **Scorpio**, **Sagittarius** (9), and 6 parts of **Capricorn** total 21 parts. 
Regarding the 'O' vowel: the 3 remaining parts of **Capricorn**, **Aquarius** (9), and **Pisces** (9) total 21 parts.

**Now, an example using the quarters of the Lunar Mansions** <note>original: *nakṣatra caraṇe*, the four 'padas' or steps of each constellation</note>:
The **'A' vowel** is the lord of the quarters of the first three constellations starting with **Ashwini**. 
The **'I' vowel** is the lord of the quarters including the five constellations starting with **Punarvasu** <note>specifically including the final quarter of the preceding sign</note>. 
The **'U' vowel** is the lord of the quarters of the four constellations starting with **Hasta** (including three quarters of **Uttara Phalguni**) and two quarters of **Anuradha**. 
The **'E' vowel** is the lord of twenty-one quarters including the remaining two quarters of **Anuradha**, the four constellations starting with **Jyeshtha**, and three quarters of **Shravana**. 
The **'O' vowel** is the lord of the twenty-one quarters including the final quarter of **Shravana** and all constellations from **Dhanishtha** to **Revati**.

**Example:** For **Devadatta**, born under the quarter of the **Revati** constellation, the **Zodiac-Vowel** is **O** || 3 ||

**Thus ends the Zodiac Chart.**

The grouping known as the **"A-class"** consists of the stars Revati, Ashvini, Bharani, Krittika, Rohini, Mrigashira, and Ardra; for these seven, the vowel **A** is the ruler. In the sequence of the five vowels starting with A (A, I, U, E, O), the vowel **I** rules the next five stars beginning with Punarvasu. The vowel **U** rules the five stars starting with Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Chitra, Svati, and Vishakha. The vowel **E** rules the five stars starting with Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Mula, Purva Ashadha, and Uttara Ashadha. Finally, the vowel **O** rules the five stars from Shravana to Uttara Bhadrapada.

|| **Example Calculation** ||
For a person named **Devadatta** born under the star **Revati**, the Star-Vowel is **A**.

->The ruling vowel of the star is known by the sequence beginning with Revati.<-
->In groups of five, the vowel of the star arises. || 2 ||<-
->**|| This ends the Chart of Star-Vowels ||**<-

The count of letters in a name, the numerical value of the vowels, and the three measures of sound—these are known as the **Body-Vowels** <note>original: *Pinda-Svara*</note> when calculated for the purposes of success or defeat. || 1 || **|| This ends the Chart of Body-Vowels ||**

By distinguishing between the sounds of syllables and the sounds of the name, and calculating the union of these factors, the **Union-Vowel** <note>original: *Yoga-Svara*</note> is determined. || 1 || **|| This ends the Chart of Union-Vowels ||**

The Star-Vowel chart relates to the sound **A**. Regarding the letters in a name, here is an example: In the name **Devadatta**, the ruling vowel for the letter **D** is **O**, which has the numerical value 5. For the letter **D** in its phonetic group <note>the dental group</note>, the value is 1. The phonetic class of **D** is the **Va**-group <note>index 5</note>. For the letter **N**, the vowel is **U**, which has the value of **Shri** (3). For the second **N**, the vowel is **E**, valued at 4. Thus, for **Devadatta**, the three primary letters total 3. When the total sum of these name-values is calculated, it reaches twenty-four (24). Dividing this by 5, the remainder is 4. According to the chart, this indicates the ruling Body-Vowel is **E**.

Regarding the **Union-Vowel** <note>Yoga-Svara</note>: In the name **Devadatta**, we calculate the various vowel components. The vowel-measure for **O** is 4; the phonetic class vowel is 5; the planetary sound is 3; the life-sound <note>Jiva-svara</note> is 1; the zodiac sound for **O** is 5; the lord sound is 1; and the body-sound for **O** is 4. When all these values for the name **Devadatta** are added together, the total is 43. Dividing this by 8 <note>original: *ashtamudrako*</note>, the remainder is 3. Therefore, the **Union-Vowel** for **Devadatta** is **U**. 

In this way, the natural and acquired vowel charts based on names have been written. Thus ends the second chapter, titled "The Chapter on Vowel-Charts," in the *Jayalakshmi* commentary by **Harivansha Mahadeva** on the *Narapati-Jayacarya Svarodaya* <note>a famous 12th-century treatise on phonetic divination used for predicting victory in war and success in life</note>. || 2 ||

Now begins the explanation of the daily duties and time-based vowels for a person named Devadatta, starting from the twelve-year period beginning with the year *Prabhava* <note>original: "prabhavadi"</note> until the end. These vowels are categorized as "natural" <note>original: "naisargika"</note> and "name-born" <note>original: "namaja"</note>. It is said there are eight natural measures <note>term: matra; a unit of time in Sanskrit prosody</note> associated with the time-based names. Thus, the natural measure is 1.

For this reason, a 12-year cycle is established for everyone individually, divided into years, solstices <note>term: ayana; the six-month period of the sun's northern or southern course</note>, seasons, months, fortnights, days, and hours <note>term: nadika; a unit of 24 minutes</note>. To understand the cause of inauspicious times, one looks to the sequence starting with the year *Prabhava*. In the cycle of sixty years beginning with *Prabhava* and *Vibhava*, each successive group of twelve years is ruled by one of the five vowels: A, I, U, E, and O. This means that for the first twelve years starting from *Prabhava*, the vowel **A** is the ruler.

> The verses state that there are eight natural measures for vowels born of a name. These are applied to the periods of time starting from the beginning. <note>original: "shloka naisargika tvastau..."</note> Following the order of *Prabhava* and other years, the vowels rise. The first vowel rules for twelve years, then the next, and so on. Each vowel's rise affects the year, the month, and two days. The hours and other flows of time are also calculated in twenty-eight units.

### || The Twelve-Year Cycle Chart ||

This is how the "rising" of the vowels works: After the first twelve years, starting from the year named *Pramathi*, the vowel **I** rises and rules for the next twelve years. During this time, while the vowel **A** was the previous lord, now the vowel **I** is the king. Then, starting from the year *Shobhana*, the vowel **U** rises for twelve years; thus **U** becomes the king. Following that, from the year *Adhara* <note>likely a misspelling of *Abhijit* or another year in the 60-year cycle</note>, the vowel **E** rules for twelve years. Finally, from the year *Samvatsara* <note>referring to the end of a cycle</note>, the vowel **O** rules for twelve years.

Here is an example: In the year 1572 of the Vikrama era <note>approx. 1515 CE</note>, which corresponds to the year 1437 of the Shaka era, one must calculate which year of the *Prabhava* cycle has passed. The method for this calculation is taught in the *Mantramala Samhita*. By taking the Shaka year, multiplying it by 22, adding the constant 4249, and then dividing by the appropriate factors <note>the text describes a complex calendrical formula involving solar and lunar variables</note>, one finds the elapsed time.

By this calculation method, we determine the years, months, days, hours, and minutes. As of the first day of the bright fortnight of the month Chaitra in Shaka 1437, it was found that the vowel **A** was the ruler until a certain point. Then, beginning with the year *Pramathi*, the vowel **I** remains the ruler for the next twelve-year period. In the case of our example, Devadatta, the "Body-Vowel" <note>term: Pinda-Svara; a vowel assigned to a person based on their name's phonetic structure</note> is **U**. Because of the strength of this vowel's rise, he will achieve "Success in Action" <note>original: "karmasiddhi"</note> as predicted by the vowel charts. Now, regarding the current...

Regarding the vowels of the upcoming twelve-year periods <note>term: Daśā; a major planetary or temporal period in Vedic astrology</note>, within those, there are fourteen vowels <note>original: "caturdaśā svarā"</note>. Now, the measurement for the duration of the consumed and future sub-periods <note>term: Antardaśā; a sub-period within a major temporal cycle</note> of these vowels is stated:

Within this, the duration is:
**Year: 1 | Month: 1 | Day: 2 | Ghati: 43 | Pala: 38**
<note>A Ghati is 24 minutes; a Pala is 24 seconds.</note>

With these measurements of years and so on, one should calculate the "rising" vowels <note>original: "udaya svarā"</note> within the twelve-year sub-period annual chart. For the years beginning with *Prabhava* <note>The first year of the 60-year Hindu Jovian cycle</note>, each sub-period is governed by a "ruler." This divider of the sub-period must be written in the daily schedule.

### || 1 || Thus ends the Twelve-Year Cycle Chart.

Now, the author explains the vowel lords for each of the years beginning with *Prabhava*. For each one of the *Prabhava* years, there are five vowels starting with 'A'. Each vowel relates to three [years] <note>The text suggests a distribution of the five primary Sanskrit vowels—A, I, U, E, O—across the 60-year cycle</note>. What does this mean? It means the vowels starting with 'A' become the "lords" for each year.

For example:
When the year is **Prabhava**, the vowel **'A'** rises.
When the year is **Vibhava**, the vowel **'I'** rises.
When the year is **Shukla**, the vowel **'U'** rises.
When the year is **Pramoda**, the vowel **'E'** rises.
When the year is **Prajapati**, the vowel **'O'** rises.

In this manner, as the sixty years proceed, the five vowels starting with 'A' rise in sequence. This is the method for the vowels in the twelve-year periods of birth.

### || 2 || Thus ends the Annual Vowel Chart.

The vowel which is the "lord" follows the sequence of vowels. Based on the duration of the year's consumption, the "enjoyment" <note>term: Bhukti; the duration of time a specific planet or vowel exerts its influence</note> of the vowel lasts for six months.

### || 3 || # || Thus ends the Solstice Vowel Chart. ||

Wherever they rise, if the vowel 'A' rises first, then there will be the rising of the eleven [remaining] vowels. This is what is meant: in the one-year periods starting with *Prabhava*, there will be sub-periods of the eleven vowels ending with the lord of *Prabhava*. 

Now the time for the rising of the sub-period is stated: The consumption <note>term: Bhukti</note> of the twelve-year period vowels is one year, one month, and so on. Specifically, that consumption is: **Month: 1 | Days: 2 | Ghatis: 43 | Palas: 38.** This is the consumption for the sub-period vowels in *Prabhava* and the rest. 

When the *Prabhava* year is active, for the duration of that year, the period belongs to a specific vowel. Within that, the very first sub-period belongs to the vowel **'A'** itself, with the duration of one month and two days mentioned above. After that comes the sub-period of the vowel **'I'**, which has the same duration. After **'I'** comes the sub-period of **'U'** with the same duration. After **'U'** comes the rise of **'E'**, and then the rise of **'O'**. In this way, eleven vowels rise in the sub-periods. By this method, one should divide the sub-period time. This sub-period time is mentioned in the text and will be explained further ahead.

Now, the author explains the rise of the Solstice vowels <note>term: Ayana; the six-month period of the sun's northern or southern course</note>. The consumption of the sub-period is:
**Days: 16 | 12 | 45 | 9 | 5**

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Svarodaya (the science of sound-vibration cycles), shifting from the twelve-year Jovian cycle to the finer divisions of time: the seasons (Ritus), months, fortnights, days, and even the smallest units of time (Ghatis). Regarding the seasons <note>term: Ritu; the six seasons of the traditional Indian calendar</note> and their various divisions, we consider the six seasons beginning with Spring. Within these seasons, the rising of the vowels is explained.

As long as the Sun is in the signs of **Aries** and **Taurus**, or moves through the sign of **Gemini**, there are seventy-two degrees (72). For these seventy-two days, the **rising of the vowel 'A'** occurs. <note>In this system, the 360 days of the year are divided into five vowel-periods of 72 days each.</note>

For the remaining degrees of **Gemini** (18 degrees), the full 30 degrees of **Cancer**, and 24 degrees of **Leo**, totaling seventy-two days (72), the **rising of the vowel 'I'** occurs.

From the remaining 6 degrees of **Leo**, the full 60 degrees of **Virgo** and **Libra**, and 6 degrees of **Scorpio**, seventy-two days are formed. During this time, the **rising of the vowel 'U'** occurs.

From the remaining 24 degrees of **Scorpio**, the full 30 degrees of **Sagittarius**, and 18 degrees of **Capricorn**, totaling seventy-two (72), the **rising of the vowel 'E'** occurs.

From the remaining 12 degrees of **Capricorn**, and the signs of **Aquarius** and **Pisces**, seventy-two days are calculated. This is the **rising of the vowel 'O'**.

In this sequence, it is stated that the five vowels beginning with 'A' rise during the progression of the seasons starting with Spring. ||1||

Now, regarding these seasons, it is easy to understand that the sub-periods <note>term: Antardashā; a secondary cycle within a major time period</note> for each vowel last for six days each.

---

The rising of the seasons beginning with Spring corresponds to the vowels starting with 'A'. Each vowel is said to rise for seventy-two days. ||1||

A period of six days is known as the "major sub-period" <note>term: Dashā</note>. For the specific measurement of the internal rising of the vowels, it is said: ||2||

**Thus concludes the Seasonal Vowel Chart from the Yamala.** <note>The Yamalas are a class of ancient Tantric texts dealing with ritual, astrology, and divination.</note>

The rising of the three vowels <note>A, I, and U</note> occurs in the months of **Bhadrapada**, **Margashirsha**, and **Vaishakha**. The vowel **'I'** is known to rule over **Ashvina**, **Shravana**, and **Bhadra**. ||2||

The domain of the vowel **'U'** is **Pausha**; the vowel **'A'** <note>likely an error in transcription for 'E'</note> is in **Jyeshtha** and **Kartika**. The vowel **'O'** rises in the months of **Magha** and **Phalguna**. ||2||

For two days, adding a zero <note>meaning no whole days, or a specific placeholder</note>, the life-force or duration is thirty Palas. This internal rising is stated here regarding the **Monthly Vowel Cycle**. ||3||

**Thus concludes the Monthly Vowel Chart from the Yamala.**

The vowel **'A'** consists of fifty-three parts (53)... <unclear>portion regarding the fortnight calculations</unclear> based on the measurement of the half-month. ||4||

**Thus concludes the Fortnightly Vowel Chart.**

The sequence of vowels starting with 'A' follows a nine-fold division. The rising of the daily vowel occurs naturally from one's own lunar date <note>term: Tithi; a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</note> and other factors. ||2||

The internal rising here is five Ghatis and eight Palas. For the lunar date, it is six Ghatis and twenty-seven Palas. This is described by the wise as the internal rising of the **Daily Vowel**. ||2||

**Thus concludes the Daily Vowel Chart from the Yamala.**

For the Vowel of the Ghati <note>a unit of 24 minutes</note>, the duration is five Ghatis and twenty-seven Palas. Its internal rising is half of a Ghati. ||2||

**Thus concludes the Ghati Vowel Chart from the Yamala.** ||1||

Now the author explains the rising of the **Monthly Vowels**. "Bhadrapada, Margashirsha, Vaishakha," etc. [referring to the verses above]. The duration of 2 days is easy to understand. ||3|| This is the **Monthly Cycle**.

Now the **Fortnightly Vowel** is explained. "The vowel 'A'..." etc. Thus, 1 day, 2 Dandis (Ghatis), and 48 Palas constitute the Fortnightly Vowel Cycle. Now for the **Daily Vowel**, the verse starting with "Akaradi" spans one and a half stanzas. This is the **Daily Vowel Chart**. Now for the **Ghati Vowel**, beginning with the lunar date, 1 Ghati. This is the **Ghati Vowel Chart**.

<summary>This page details the mathematical and astrological correlations between the five primary Sanskrit vowels and various divisions of time, ranging from 72-day seasonal periods to individual 24-minute units (Ghatis). These "vowel risings" are used in Svarodaya divination to determine auspicious times and personal rhythms based on the sun's position and the lunar calendar.</summary>

Now is explained the sub-rising <note>term: Antarodaya; the emergence of sub-periods within a larger time cycle</note> within the twelve-year period. In this context of sub-risings, the measurement of time is said to be five-fold: years, months, days, *palas* <note>term: Pala; a unit of time, 1/60th of a Ghati or 24 seconds</note>, and again *palas*—thus five types of time measurement are used to determine the results. The method described here follows the sequence of years, months, and so on, calculated from the day of death or the specific day of the yoga. || 1 ||

Now, the calculation for the past and remaining sub-risings in the form of vowels is stated. In the cycle of twelve years, starting from the current year known as **Pramathi** <note>The 13th year of the 60-year Jovian cycle</note> until the end of the twelve-year period, the **vowel 'A'** is currently rising. In the **Shaka year 1437** <note>original: "शके १४३७"; equivalent to 1515 CE</note>, at the time of the first day of the bright half of the month of **Chaitra** <note>Chaitra Shukla Pratipad, the traditional Hindu New Year</note>, the years are multiplied by three hundred and sixty (360) and added to the days.

The total comes to 2495. When multiplied by the specific factors <note>The text uses "Sharago dhi sudhanshavah," a mnemonic for numbers</note>, the result is 89700. These are the *palas* of the sub-rising, totaling 1,413,760. By dividing this by the standard units, the elapsed vowels are determined.

The **vowel 'I'** has already been experienced <note>original: "bhukta"; consumed or passed</note>. The **vowel 'O'** currently acts as the lord of the period <note>term: Dashādhipati; the planetary or vocalic ruler of a time segment</note>. Its remaining duration consists of 316 days, while 151 days have already passed, leaving 75,152 units. By this calculation, the rising of the **vowel 'U'** exists from the first day of the bright half of **Chaitra** until the first day of the dark half of **Ashadha**. After that, the sub-rising of the **vowel 'A'** will occur.

Alternatively, here is an example of the sub-rising of the **vowel 'I'**: Currently, it is the sixteenth year of the cycle, known as **Chitrabhanu**. Seven years have passed since the start of the Shaka era, which serves as the marker. For instance, at the junction of the Sun's transition, the first day of the bright half of Chaitra is the forest of the past. When 97 days of the year's measure have passed, one year is written as completed on that New Year's Day. That serves as the marker for the second year to be experienced. Similarly for the Shaka era...

<meta>This page continues the technical mathematical and astronomical derivation of the Svarodaya system, specifically focusing on how to align the Shaka calendar years with the sixty-year Jovian cycle (Jupiter's cycle) to determine which mystical "vowel" governs a specific time. It references the Shaka year 1437 (1515 CE) to demonstrate the transition between the years "Subhanu" and "Tarana."</meta>

### Calculation of the Great Cycles

When twenty-two years have passed, the collection of Shaka years is established. The origin of the Jovian years <note>term: Barhaspatya varsha; a 60-year cycle based on the movement of Jupiter</note> and their mathematical proof is found in the *Siddhantas* <note>classical Sanskrit astronomical treatises</note> and the *Varaha Samhita* <note>The Brihat Samhita by the 6th-century polymath Varahamihira</note>. Here, the solar years of Jupiter and Mars are calculated in conjunction. 

In each sign of the zodiac, the movement of the planet exceeds the allotted time. According to the measurements of Varahamihira, the revolutions are placed at 97. Through these revolutions, the twelve-fold years of Jupiter are determined. Consequently, the solar years are greater than the years traversed by Mars. The difference between the two is calculated: the Jovian revolutions are 364,224 while the solar years are 4,320,000. The difference is 50,688. 

Using the multiplier for the beginning of the Shaka era, the remainder for the Jovian year and the solar years are found. Twenty-two stars are produced. In the absence of hidden divisors, the middle movement within the solar years results in a zodiacal position of 2 signs, 10 degrees, 21 minutes, and 17 seconds. 

### The Entry of the New Year

Regarding the year *Prabhava* <note>the first year of the 60-year cycle</note> and the subsequent years, the final minutes and seconds of the previous year must be calculated. The daily movement of Jupiter results in a specific gain of minutes. It is found that the current year began four days and thirteen *dandas* <note>term: Danda; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes, also called a Ghati</note> after the start of the first day of the waxing moon in the month of Chaitra <note>term: Chaitra Shukla Pratipada; the traditional Hindu New Year's Day</note>. 

Thus, the commencement of this year occurred after twenty *dandas* had passed. This method of determining the commencement of all years has been explained by us in this commentary. 

### Determining the Vowel Influence

In the beginning of the Shaka year, there are no defects in the days of the year being experienced; therefore, the results of the upcoming year must be stated. This is our specific opinion. While we have shown this through mathematical logic, others may also deliberate on it. 

One might ask: how can the days of the current year be calculated from the previous years? For example, in the Shaka year 1437 <note>original: "शाके १४३७"; 1515 CE</note>, many days—roughly fifty—had already passed. Therefore, at the time of Shaka 1436, during the fourth day of the month of Magha, the year *Subhanu* <note>the 17th year in the 60-year cycle</note> began. The days elapsed were fifty-five, with forty-five *dandas*. 

In the year *Subhanu*, the vowel "I" <note>original: "इकार स्वरः"</note> is the dominant vibration. Its calculated value is 200,700 *palas* <note>a unit of 24 seconds</note>. When we look at the annual vowel divisions, the "I" vowel transition is followed by the "U" vowel <note>original: "उकार स्वरः"</note> in the sequence. 

The remaining duration for this influence is nine days and forty-two *dandas*. What is the result of this? On the tenth day of the waxing moon in Chaitra, after forty-two *dandas*, the transition to the "E" vowel <note>original: "ए कार"</note> will occur. This will last until the twelfth day of the waxing moon in Vaishakha, up to twenty-five *dandas* and forty *palas*. 

Now, the establishment of the first day of the waxing moon in Chaitra is described...

<meta>This page continues the technical astronomical calculations from the previous folio, shifting from the large Jovian cycles to the specific timing of the "vowel rise" (svarodaya). This system correlates mystical sounds (vowels) with chronological divisions like solstices, seasons, and months. The translator notes that the text remains in the Shaka year 1436 (1514 CE) for its practical examples.</meta>

The rise of the vowel is known as the "subsequent rise" <note>term: antarodaya; the point at which one mystical vowel's influence ends and the next begins</note>. An example of this is taken from the source: "In the South..."

<margin>Narapati Jayacharya / Jayadipa 6</margin> Because of the statement that "The Northern Course <note>term: Uttarayana; the six-month period when the sun travels north on the celestial sphere</note> belongs to the Lord Ishvara," the rise of the vowel **I** <note>original: ikārodayaḥ</note> is observed on the first day of the month of Chaitra during the Northern Course. Likewise, according to the *Ayana-Nirnaya* <note>a treatise on determining the solstices</note> by Varahamihira: "The solstice is associated with the Southern Path starting from Cancer and the Northern Path starting from Capricorn."

In the Shaka year 1436 <note>1514 CE</note>, when the solar entry into Capricorn occurred on the 12th day of the month, the sun was situated in Capricorn. Starting from that point, until the sun enters Aries on the 13th day of the bright half of Chaitra, the solstice continues. Therefore, on the first day of the bright half of Chaitra, the rise of the vowel **I** is ordained.

Next, we provide an example of the duration. For the signs starting with Capricorn, the distance is 79 krosas <note>a traditional measure of distance, here used in a technical astronomical sense</note>, which equals 240,800 *palas* <note>term: pala; a unit of time equal to 24 seconds</note>. The *palas* for the subsequent rise of the solstice-vowel are 58,909. This is the period of enjoyment for the vowel's rise from the beginning of Capricorn. This figure serves as the divisor. By this, we know the "dissolution" of the interim rise of the vowel from the total *palas* of the solstice: 3 hours, 49 minutes, and 5 seconds <note>original: 3/49/5; calculated in dandas and palas</note>. Thus, on the first day of the bright half of Chaitra, the rise of the vowel **U** <note>original: ukāra</note> occurs after 49 *dandas* <note>term: danda; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>. This continues until 33 *dandas* on the second day of the dark half of Vaishakha.

## An Example of the Subsequent Rise of the Seasonal Vowel

Now, the commencement of the seasons <note>term: ritu</note> is determined by the Sun’s transit <note>term: sankranti</note>. As stated by the author of the *Samhita* <note>referring to Varahamihira's Brihat Samhita</note>: "The six seasons arise from the Sun's passage through two zodiac signs each, beginning with Capricorn." From the entry into Capricorn until the first day of the bright half of Chaitra, eighteen days have passed since the entry into Pisces. The Spring season <note>term: Vasanta Ritu</note> begins from the entry into Pisces. By this method, the rise of the vowel is ordained.

Here, the "subsequent rise"—meaning the period of the interim phase—is 60 days. The *palas* for this are 636,483. The divisor for the interim rise here is the "rising vowel." The *palas* consumed by the rising vowel are 64,800. This is the dividend. Dividing by the divisor, we find the subsequent rise of the vowel is 1 day, 39 *dandas*, and 9 *palas*. In this interim rise, the vowels **A** and **I** have already been consumed; now it is the interim rise of the vowel **U**. During its passage, on the first day of the bright half of Chaitra, the time is 38 *dandas* and 9 *palas*. This continues until the 38th *danda* on the third day of the bright half of Chaitra.

## An Example of the Subsequent Rise of the Monthly Vowel

Starting from the first day of the bright half of Chaitra until the second day, there are ten days <note>unclear: possibly referring to a specific subdivision of the month</note>. There, the division of the interim rise of the monthly vowel is as follows: in 6 days, the first period is 43 *dandas* and 28 *palas*. The divisor for the division of the signs is 9,899. The *palas* consumed by the rising vowel **U** are 57,600. This is the dividend. When the dividend is divided, the result is the 5th [vowel], which occurs after 19 *dandas*. Above that, the interim rise of the vowel **A** occurs, continuing until the 5th *danda* on the fourth day of the bright half of Chaitra.

## An Example of the Fortnightly Vowel

The vowel follows the sequence of the fortnights. In the bright fortnight <note>term: Shukla Paksha</note>, the vowel is **I**. Starting from the first day of Chaitra, the rise of the vowel **I** is ordained. On the first day of the bright half of Chaitra, the rise and the subsequent rise both belong to the vowel **I**. Similarly, the daily vowel rise also belongs to **I**.

On the first day of the bright half of Chaitra, the rise of the vowel **A** is the beginning...

<meta>This page continues the technical discourse on the Svarodaya system (the science of vowel-based divination), moving from the calculation of time-units like palas to the qualitative analysis of the "states" (avasthas) of vowels. The weight should be given as twenty-seven *palas* <note>term: pala; a unit of weight or time depending on context; here it refers to the physical measurement of a water-clock component</note> according to this specific method. **|| 1 ||**

Thus ends the **Wheel of the Vowel-Hour** <note>original: ghaṭī-svara-cakram</note>. It is said to begin with twelve and continue until the end. **|| 2 ||** This refers to the year, the month, and the day. **|| 3 ||** These are the twelve vowels. **|| 3 ||** And so on. **|| 4 ||**

Now, following this, the five states—beginning with the "Child" state—of the twelve vowels that have arisen at the moment of a man's birth <note>original: narodaye</note> are explained. One must certainly determine these states for the vowels of a name. There is no other reason for the difference in their states; the rule is universal. Therefore, the distinct states of all vowels are determined by the vowel of one's own name. For some, the state is designated as "Child" <note>original: bāla</note> based on the name-vowel; for others, it is "Adolescent" <note>original: kumāra</note>, and for others "Youth" <note>original: yuva</note>, and so forth. **|| 5 ||**

These are the five designations: Child, Adolescent, Youth, Old, and Dead. As it was said, the designation is determined by the name-vowel.

---

> Starting from the twelve signs and ending with the last, the time is determined. **|| 35 ||** At the end, once more, they follow in sequence starting from the eleventh. **|| 2 ||** In years, months, days, and *ghatikas* <note>term: ghaṭikā; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>, and in the constellations <note>original: nakṣatra</note> as well. I have spoken of the measurement of time just as it appears in this "Rise of Vowels" <note>original: Svarodaya</note>. **|| 2 ||**
> 
> The five-fold strength of the twelve vowels is explained hereafter. In that same way, the state is obtained through one's own name. **|| 3 ||** When the vowel is dominant, it is known as "extended" <note>original: lambve</note>; the others are simply "risen." In this system, the reason for the different states like "Child" is established. **|| 4 ||** Based on the vowel of the name that has arisen, those five states—Child and the rest—are determined according to their own time-measurements. **|| 5 ||**
> 
> These are: **Child**, **Adolescent**, **Youth**, **Old**, and **Dead**. There is no doubt that these vowels grant results according to their own inherent state. **|| 6 ||**
> 
> Does the **Child** vowel cause delay? The **Adolescent** grants strength. The **Youth** is said to bring all success, while the **Old** vowel gives loss. **|| 7 ||** In travel, war, marriage, or when something is lost or the hand is diseased: the **Child** vowel is seen as favorable for some, but not auspicious for weddings and similar rites. **|| 8 ||**

---

The designation occurs by the power of the vowel; it is not merely the name itself, but the vowel within the name. This is explained in one way: the vowel which is the "name-vowel" <note>the first vowel of a person's given name</note> is always the "Child" state at its initial rise. The second vowel arising from the time-calculation is the "Adolescent" vowel. The third is the "Youth," the fourth is the "Old," and the fifth is the "Dead" <note>original: mṛta; also referred to as 'vṛta' in the transcription's typo</note>. In this manner, the designations are conceived based on the name-vowel. These designations grant fruits according to their own natural state. Whichever vowel-state is active for a person, that state yields the result. **|| 6 ||**

What causes delay? **|| 7 ||** To explain how this result manifests, it is said: "In travel, war..." **|| 8 ||** And "All success..."

In all auspicious works, and likewise at the time of travel, the **Adolescent** brings success and victory in battle. **|| 9 ||** In all things, whether auspicious or inauspicious, and in the mastery of mantras and yantras <note>term: mantra and yantra; sacred chants and mystical diagrams used for ritual and meditation</note>, all success arises, especially in travel and war, from the **Youth** vowel. **|| 10 ||** 

In the acquisition of knowledge, the worship of gods, initiation <note>original: dīkṣā</note>, the commencement of a project, or the preparation of a mantra, the **Old** vowel is certain to be effective; it is also suitable for a king's pleasures or for a sacrifice <note>original: yagya/yajña</note>. **|| 11 ||**

But for marriage and all such things, or for battle, the **Dead** vowel is inauspicious. The wise, who possess intelligence, should not perform any such actions when the **Dead** vowel has arisen. **|| 12 ||**

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of vowel states (avasthās) within the Svarodaya system of divination. The previous page introduced the five states—Child, Adolescent, Youth, Old, and Dead—explaining that every vowel in a person's name corresponds to one of these states. This page defines their relative strengths and their specific impacts on victory or defeat in conflict.</meta>

<margin>
Na. Ti. 
Ma. Ja. Tī. 
1190
</margin>

<note>The abbreviation in the margin and header "Na. Ti. Ma. Ja. Tī." refers to the *Narapati-jayacaryā-ṭīkā*, a famous 12th-century commentary on the "Victory of the King" system of divination used for warfare and political success.</note>

### || Now, the section explaining the strength of the five states starting with "Child" ||

The concept of "Dead" or "Child" is easy to understand. **|| 22 ||** Whosoever's third vowel-constant is "Dead" <note>original: mṛta; symbolizing a state of total inactivity or powerlessness</note>, he attains victory. Whosoever's fourth is "Dead," he meets with defeat in battle. Whosoever's second is "Dead," he attains victory from all sides starting from the second position. If a vowel is the fifth, it is "Death-bestowing" <note>original: mṛtyudāyaka</note> for the two combatants. Since the fifth is designated as "Death," one should not engage in conflict with it. Even for one's own chosen vowel, it is "Old" <note>original: vṛddha</note>.

Between the "Dead" and "Child" vowels, the "Child" vowel is stronger. Between "Child" and "Old," "Old" is stronger. Between "Old" and "Adolescent," "Adolescent" is stronger. Between "Adolescent" and "Youth," "Youth" is stronger. Thus, the "Child" is more powerful than the "Dead," and the "Old" is more powerful than the "Child." This is how the strength is calculated, with each successive state being more powerful than the one before it. **|| 24 ||** 

Now, he speaks specifically regarding the equality of strength in battle and the neutralization of the enemy. Regarding the "Vowel devoid of death": Between two soldiers...

> The vowels are known as Dead, Child, as well as Old, Adolescent, and Youth. 
> Those who know the science of vowels should understand that each is stronger than the one preceding it. **|| 23 ||**
> For whomsoever a vowel falls in the fifth position, that vowel is a giver of death. 
> If it fails, there will be no success; it grants no results in the midst of battle. **|| 24 ||**
> With a vowel free from the death-influence, one conquers the enemy if their own vowel is superior. 
> If the vowels of the names are equal, there is a stalemate, leading to either a treaty, victory, or defeat. **|| 25 ||**
> A single vowel results in equality; a second vowel gives half the result. 
> A third vowel gives full results; the third results in destruction and defeat. **|| 26 ||**

When there is a vowel free from death-influence, the vowel of the origin <note>original: yoni; refers to the source or phonetic category of the vowel</note> which is superior overcomes the other. An "Old" vowel is overcome by an "Adolescent" one. A "Dead" vowel is overcome by a "Child" vowel in battle. A "Child" vowel is overcome by an "Adolescent" vowel. A "Dead" vowel is overcome by an "Adolescent" vowel. This is because it was said: "Dead, Child, as well as Old..." 

In terms of phonetic measure <note>term: mātrā; the time-unit taken to pronounce a vowel</note>, the vowel 'A' <note>short a</note> is less than 'Ā' <note>long ā</note>. 'Ā' is less than 'I'. 'I' is less than 'Ī'. 'Ī' is less than 'U'. 'U' is less than 'E'. 'E' is the superior vowel. This same logic applies to the groupings of letters.

Now, how should this meaning be interpreted? It is explained here: the phrase "Dead, Child, and Old" describes the vowels as being stronger in successive order. By calculating these, one determines if the name is inferior or superior. If the vowel of the name is superior, victory is achieved. Between two soldiers, the one whose name-vowel is inferior is himself overcome. Even a small difference produces a result here. **|| 1 ||** 

For one person, there might be only one vowel-strength in the name; for another, there might be two. The inferior vowel is "killed" by the superior vowel. If the vowel is superior, victory belongs to that person. If one has two vowels and the other has many, victory still goes to the superior one. Furthermore, vowels like 'Ā' are stronger than those preceding them in the sequence. Those of the "Monkey-Swing" lineage <note>original: markaṭa-ḍola-santāna; likely a specific school of practitioners or a mnemonic device for alternating fortunes</note> understand this. If the names are equal, the result is equal; the two soldiers get the same result. When the name-vowels of the two are equal in their 1, 2, 3, 4, or 5-measure counts, it results in equality, friendship, or a treaty in the war—or perhaps victory for both in the battle.

<margin>
Because here, the strengths and weaknesses must be understood in the **Science of Sound** <note>original: Svara Shastra</note>. In that very place: "From one measure <note>original: mātrā; a unit of time in prosody</note> to the end..." A single-measure sound is one letter; a double-measure is <unclear>two vowels</unclear>.
<insert>Initial and others.</insert>
<insert>|| 17 ||</insert>
</margin>

Defeat comes from strength alone; likewise, defeat occurs when there is equality. And so, in the *Essence of Battle* <note>original: Samara Sara; a classical text on military divination</note>, it is said that loss, increase, or victory depends on the "Vowel of the Wind" <note>original: Maruta Svara</note> being dominant. In equality, victory is also attained by the one who knows the "Battle-Knowledge" <note>original: Samara-vid</note>. In equal measures, victory is possible. And so, in the *System of Measures* <note>original: Mātrikā</note>, there are measures of one, two, three, four, five, and eight. What is the significance of the "Ketu" <note>the South Node of the Moon</note> or the "Vaila"? A five-measure sound. With other sounds, the numbering of the "Vajra" positions follows the number five. If the sound of victory is weak, there is a meeting or a coming together of the two parties. **|| 15 ||** If both possess the same single vowel, they are equal; neither attains victory nor defeat. One who is a "Child" <note>original: Bāla</note> or "Adolescent" <note>original: Kumāra</note>, and not a "Youth" <note>original: Yuvā</note> or "Old" <note>original: Vṛddha</note>, achieves only a little victory in battle. Between the "Eunuch" <note>original: Napuṃsaka; a category of neutral vowels</note> and the "Full Son," victory always goes to the latter. Between the Child and the Old, the strength of the body... one should write according to the greater measure. **|| 16 ||** Now, the enemy who desires victory is defeated. As it is said: "The death of the enemy through sounds." At whatever time the enemy's "Death-Vowel" <note>original: mṛtyu-svara</note> is active, if one begins the battle at that specific time, victory is certain and not otherwise. Indeed, this is the primary method for total victory. **|| 17 ||** Now, he explains the specific timing regarding the "Vowel of the Time" <note>original: Kāla-svara</note>. **|| 18 ||** Regarding the "Seasons" <note>original: Ṛtu</note>...

->When the enemy is in their Death-Vowel, in the residence of the Fire-God,<-
->Victory in battle is certain at the commencement of that time. || 17 ||<-
->In the performance of that moment, the vowel that remains throughout the day...<-
->|| 18 || The Vowel of the Planet should be known; the Vowel of the Life-Force<-
->is active in the left side. || 19 || The Season, the Zodiac, and the Month:<-
->the manifestation in the lunar mansion within six months. ||<-

...becomes the enemy's "Death-Vowel." If this is the case, one should engage in battle, and victory will surely follow. Here is an illustration:

Consider the two combatants, **Devadatta** and **Viraja** <note>traditional placeholder names, equivalent to 'Person A' and 'Person B'</note>. In the "Vowel of the Pulse" <note>original: Nāḍī Svara</note> at that time, the sound 'A' counts as two, and the sound 'I' is the measure. By the strength of these vowels, Devadatta's 'A' sounds are compared to Viraja's 'A' sounds, which happen to be in the "Death-Vowel" state. In such a case, if the battle begins at that moment, Devadatta will be victorious in every way. 

Now, regarding the time of victory for these two: if the "Vowel of the Day" is the 'U' sound, and the 'U' and 'V' sounds are associated with the 'O' and 'A' sounds, and both are in the "Death" measure, then at that time, Viraja will conquer Devadatta. 

Furthermore, Devadatta's "House-Vowel" <note>original: Gṛha Svara</note> is 'A'. By that strength, on the seventh day, the 'A' sound arises. It is in the "Old" state. Then the 'I' sound arises; it is also an "Old" vowel. Such a state bestows victory. Devadatta's "Life-Vowel" <note>original: Jīva Svara</note> is 'O'. By that strength, in the months of **Vaishakha** (April-May), **Bhadrapada** (August-September), and **Margashirsha** (November-December), the 'A' sound arises. These are "Equal" or "Adolescent" states. They provide strength to Devadatta in battle.

In the months of **Ashadha** (June-July), **Shravana** (July-August), and **Ashvin** (September-October), the 'U' sound arises. Through the power of the Life-Vowel, these months are called "Youth" months; they bestow victory upon Devadatta. In **Chaitra** (March-April) and **Pausha** (December-January), the 'Ṛ' sound arises. Because of the Life-Vowel's strength, these lead to Devadatta's defeat in battle. In the months of **Jyeshtha** (May-June) and **Karttika** (October-November), the 'E' sound arises. By the power of the Life-Vowel, these are "King" months but bring death to Devadatta in battle. This is the calculation of strength and weakness in the months. 

Because Devadatta was born in the first quarter of the **Revati** <term>Nakshatra (lunar mansion)</term>, he is situated in the **Pisces** <note>original: Mīna</note> zodiac sign. Therefore, the Zodiac-Vowel is 'O' and 'E'. From that strength, the Season-Vowel 'Ḍ' and 'A' arise; there, victory is considered. If the Season-Vowels are 'I', victory is unknown/uncertain. If the 'U' sounds arise during the days, Devadatta faces defeat. In the 'E' sounds...

### In the Strength of Sound: The Strength of the Zodiacal Sun

For **Devadatta**, the sound associated with his birth star <note>original: nakshatra-svara</note> is the letter **A**. According to the rule regarding the strength of the sound **A**, when a sound lacks strength, there is a specific outcome. In the **Southern Course** <note>original: ayana; referring to the sun's path</note>, the sound is in the **Child** <note>original: bāla</note> state. In the **Adolescent** <note>original: kumāra</note> state, it is in the Southern path. In the **Child** state, there is defeat <note>original: bhaṅga; literally "breaking"</note> in battle. In the **Adolescent** state, there is an **auspicious victory**.

According to the rule regarding the **Pinda Sound** <note>original: piṇḍa-svara; the vowel sound derived from the aggregate of a name's letters</note>, Devadatta’s **Pinda Sound** is the letter **Ī**. If the half-measure of that sound arises during the **Vowel Rise** <note>original: svarodaya</note>, it signifies **Death**. If it occurs during an attack, there is death in battle.

Similarly, if the sound **I** arises in the **Child** state, there is defeat. If the sound **U** arises in the annual cycle in the state called **Adolescent**, there is an **auspicious victory**. If the sound **Ṛ** arises in the annual cycle, there is **total victory**. If the sound **O** arises in the annual cycle in the state called **Gate** <note>original: dvāra; a technical term for a specific vowel position</note>, there is defeat in battle.

Now, Devadatta’s **Yoga Sound** <note>original: yoga-svara; a sound calculated by combining specific astrological factors</note> is **U**. If it arises in the **twelve-yearly sound cycle** in the state designated as **Child**, there is defeat in battle. If the sound **Ṛ** arises in the twelve-yearly **Pinda Sound** in the **Adolescent** state, there is an **auspicious victory**. If the sound **Ḷ** arises in the twelve-yearly cycle, and the **Yoga Sounds** are in the state called **Heaven** <note>original: dyu</note>, there is defeat in battle. If the sound **U** arises in the twelve-yearly cycle and the strength of the **Yoga Sound** meets the **Gate** state, there is defeat. If the sound **E** arises in the twelve-yearly cycle in the **Childhood** state, there is an **auspicious victory**.

In this way, the strengths should be applied in battle. **|| 18 ||** Now, regarding the rules for the strength of **All Sounds**, he declares that this strength is more powerful than all others at all times. At all times:

---

> -> Just as the footprints of all animals are contained within the **elephant's footprint**, and just as all rivers flow into the **ocean**... <-
> -> Just as all the gods reside within the body of **Hari** <note>Vishnu</note>, so do all the **Strengths** <note>original: balas</note> reside within the **Sounds** <note>Svaras</note>. **|| 21 ||** <-
> -> The cultivation of **Mantras** <note>sacred chants</note>, **Yantras** <note>mystical diagrams</note>, and the practice of **Tantra** <note>ritual systems</note> should always be performed when the sounds are "Downward-facing" <note>original: adhomukha; likely referring to the breath's direction</note> and have vocalic strength. **|| 22 ||** <-
> -> When the sound is strong, all **auspicious speech** should be performed. It grants success in all undertakings, especially at the **time of battle**. **|| 23 ||** <-
> -> **Killing** <note>term: Māraṇa; rituals to cause death</note>, **Enchanting** <note>term: Mohana; rituals to delude or attract</note>, **Sowing Dissension** <note>term: Vidveṣaṇa; causing hatred between allies</note>, **Expulsion** <note>term: Uccāṭana; driving an enemy away</note>, and **Subjugation** <note>term: Vaśa; bringing someone under one's control</note>—as well as disputes and wars—should be performed when the **Short Sound** <note>original: hrasva svara</note> arises. **|| 24 ||** <-
> -> Activities involving **horses** and other vehicles should be done when the strength is at its peak. The beginning of **education** and **marriage** should be performed when the **Living Sound** <note>original: jīva svara</note> arises. **|| 25 ||** <-
> -> The construction of **temples**, **gardens**, and **palaces**... <-

---

Thus, the strength of sounds should be applied at all times—whether in the twelve-yearly, annual, seasonal, monthly, fortnightly, daily, or the measure of a *nadi* <note>a unit of time approximately 24 minutes long</note> <unclear>7</unclear>—one should act according to the strength or weakness. **20**

Supporting this very point again, he says: "Just as the footprint..." (Verse 21). Those with strength through the strength of sound are **strong**; those without strength are **weak**. What is the purpose of speaking or not speaking of this? He answers: "The cultivation of Mantra and Yantra..." (Verse 22). "When the sound is strong..." (Verse 23). "Killing and enchanting..." (Verse 24). "Horses and vehicles..." (Verse 25). "Temples and gardens..." (Verse 26).

### Detailed Description of the Ten Sounds

When the lunar day, weekday, and star are each analyzed separately, and then found to align in one place, that is a [significant] Rise of Sound. || 1 ||

The lunar day and weekday are considered according to the first letter of a person’s name. If one ignores this on a given day, it will result in loss for that person, or even for a king. || 2 ||

Through this alignment of sounds—considering the weekday, star, and direction—a wise man should perform his actions on that day, even if he lacks the specific [ritual] mantras. || 3 ||

**Thus ends the Chart of Lunar Day, Weekday, and Star Sounds.**

The number of states, such as the Child sound, are described as follows. They arise in sequence according to the measure of their own power. || 1 || 

In units of twenty-four minutes <term>ghaṭikā</term>, in a day, in a month, in a season, in a half-year <note>ayana</note>, and in the ninth and eleventh year, the different "states" are produced by the sounds. || 2 ||

Small gains or heavy losses are determined over two days. The measure of the previous and subsequent states follows in sequence. || 3 || The measure of the sound used...

Just as the state named "Childhood" <note>original: kumārākhyā</note> belonging to the name "Devadatta" <note>a traditional placeholder name, like "John Doe"</note> will bring peace, so too through this example are the fruits of all "states" <term>avasthā; the condition or life-stage of a sound at a specific time</term> explained. Now, these five "clothings" <note>divisions</note> consist of twelve units each. According to their own period of consumption <note>duration</note>, they move through the day and night in the form of *ghatikas* <term>ghaṭikā; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term>. This timing should be understood through the Circle. || 4 ||

Now, the listing of the states: 1. Dead, 2. Child, 3. Infant, 4. Wet-nurse, 5. Maiden, 6. Youth, 7. Royal Consort, 8. Hair-growth, 9. Sleep, 10. Fevered, 11. Traveling, 12. **The Twelve States of the Child-Sound:**
1. Dead, 2. Child, 3. Infant, 4. Wet-nurse, 5. Maiden, 6. Youth, 7. Royal Consort, 8. Hair-growth, 9. Sleep, 10. Fevered, 11. Traveling, 12. Dead. || 5 ||

**The Twelve States of the Prince-Sound:**
1. Healthy, 2. Auspicious, 3. Loss <note>original: moṣa</note>, 4. Great Joy, 5. Increase, 6. Delusion, 7. Peace-making, 8. Good Fortune, 9. Forgiveness, 10. Contentment, 11. Bliss, 12. Auspiciousness. || 6 ||

**The Twelve States of the Youth-Sound:**
1. Celebration, 2. Joy, 3. Effort <note>original: jṛmbha; literally 'yawning' or 'blossoming'</note>, 4. Strength, 5. Resolution, 6. Desire, 7. Nourishment, 8. Happiness, 9. Success, 10. Fortitude, 11. Sovereignty, 12. Peace. || 7 ||

**The Twelve States of the Domestic-Sound:**
1. Disability, 2. Grief, 3. Futility, 4. Hatred, 5. Suffering/Defeat, 6. Hunger, 7. Shattering, 8. Royal Wrath, 9. Emaciation, 10. Non-peace, 11. Sickness, 12. Death. || 8 ||

**The Twelve States of the Death-Sound:**
1. Severed, 2. Bound, 3. Enemy-slaying, 4. Withering, 5. Great, 6. Burning, 7. Hardship, 8. Marked, 9. Splitting, 10. Star-crossed, 11. Dying, 12. Death. || 9 ||

Thus, there are sixty states. When the sound is in a healthy state, it is five-fold auspicious. In all these, the result should be known as corresponding to the name of the state. || 10 || This concludes the sixty states of the Child-sound and others.

The vowel **A** is the Lord of the East; the vowel **E** is the Lord of the South. The vowel **U** is in the West, and **O** is known as the Middle or Northern sound. || 11 || In whichever direction a sound rises, one should avoid the "fifth direction" <note>the direction opposite or calculated as fifth in the sequence</note> for all tasks, and especially at the time of departure on a journey. || 12 || If one persists in a direction where the Sound-Lord is "severed" or "bound" <note>negative states listed above</note>, loss, misfortune, or failure will surely occur; of this there is no doubt. This is the **Circle of Directional Enjoyment**.

---

Regarding the vowels starting with **A**, their respective strengths are described: "In the East," etc. || 1 || "In whichever direction"—this refers to the fifth direction being forbidden. If it is asked which specific sound's direction should be avoided, it is explained here: the decision is made based on which sound is strongest. Whichever sound is the primary initiator for a person, they must avoid its fifth direction. For example, if for "Devadatta" the strongest sound is **O**, and the position of **O** is the center/north, then the fifth direction for Devadatta would be the North. Devadatta should always avoid the North for travel, war, or marriage. In battle, one should instead take refuge in the direction indicated by the name-sound's strength. || 2 || This explains the strength of the vowels starting with **A**. The result is stated: "In the Severed/Bound states," etc. || 3 ||

# The Cycle of Sound-State Consumption || The Directional Sound-Cycle

| Ghaṭikā | Day | Pala | Month | Season | Half-year | Year | Epoch |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Day | 0 | 1 | 2 | 6 | 15 | 30 | 360 |
| Gha | 5 | 25 | 30 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Pala 20 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | Fortnight | Month | Year 1 |

| 0 | Preceding Constellation | 0 |
|---|---|---|
| N. E. <br> **Empty** | Jo. M. <br> **Full** | S. E. M. <br> <unclear>Auspicious</unclear> |
| 0 | N. <br> **Victorious** | 0 |

<note>The table above describes units of time used to calculate how long a specific "sound state" lasts. The grid refers to the four types of lunar days—Riktā (Empty), Pūrṇā (Full), Bhadrā (Auspicious), and Jayā (Victorious)—aligned with directions like North (N) and East (E).</note>

Regarding "at the start of the lunar day" <note>original: tithyādau</note>: At the moment a lunar day <term>tithi; a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> begins, the vowel-sound <term>svara; here referring to the mystical sound or breath state</term> associated with it is known as the "Half-Sound." || 130 || A sound-state lasts for five *ghatikas* <term>ghaṭikā; a 24-minute unit of time</term>. If, for instance, during the first day of the waxing moon in the month of Chaitra <note>the first month of the Hindu lunar year</note>, a specific sound arises, I shall now declare the results for that time. || 11 ||

To provide an example for calculating the time-units of the lunar day: On the first day of the waxing moon of Chaitra, if the "Ga" sound arises, and the elapsed time of the lunar day is fifteen <note>15</note> *ghatikas*, that equals nine hundred <note>900</note> seconds <term>pala; a unit of 24 seconds</term>. Dividing this by twenty-seven <note>27</note>, the remainder determines the active sound. If a person like Devadatta <note>a traditional placeholder name, like "John Doe"</note> asks a question while standing to the East, South, West, or North, the result should be determined using the characteristics of the *Sarvatobhadra* <term>Sarvatobhadra-cakra; a complex square "mandala" chart used for divination</term>.

When a sound arises at the start of a lunar day, it progresses through stages from childhood to death. I shall now state the results of such a query. || 1 || Calculate the elapsed time of the lunar day in seconds <note>palas</note>, then divide by twenty-seven <note>27</note>. The remainder indicates the sound active at that moment. || 2 || The result of the question pertains specifically to the goal for which the query was made. If no specific goal was mentioned, then the sound indicates general good or bad fortune. || 3 ||

1. **Infancy** <note>original: bāla</note>: If the sound is in the "Infant" state, any gain will be very small. One should expect trouble from the king, disease, and potential loss or doubt. || 4 ||
2. **Childhood** <note>original: kumāra</note>: At this time, profit is likely. It brings the destruction of enemies, victory in war, and success in travel everywhere. || 5 ||
3. **Youth** <note>original: yuvā</note>: In the "Youthful" state, one gains royal power. Although there may be momentary distress, one will slay enemies in battle and all travel will be successful. || 6 ||
4. **Old Age** <note>original: vṛddha</note>: There will be no profit; instead, distress increases. In battle, one faces defeat, and one may not return from a journey. || 7 ||
5. **Death** <note>original: mṛtyu</note>: In this state, any task undertaken will fail. One should advise against risky actions or battle, as it indicates fear, defeat, or death.

Alternatively, if Devadatta asks about a specific person or direction, the practitioner should look at the sound associated with that person's name and its current "state" to provide the answer. If no specific person is named, the practitioner should use the sound of the questioner's own name.

One should declare the results after knowing the direction and other factors of the child. **3** Now, he describes the results of the "Infant Sun" <term>Balāditya; a state of the sun or breath representing the earliest stage of power</term> at its rising. **4** Regarding the "Youthful" stage. **5** Regarding the "Old" rising. **6** Again, regarding the "Old" rising. **7** Regarding the "Death" rising. **8** Now, he describes the names and characteristics of the vowel-sounds. Now, the results of the query: "The Odd Sounds." The Odd sounds <term>oja-svara; vowels associated with masculine energy</term> are **A, U,** and **O**. When the even <note>feminine</note> sound is rising, it represents the nature of women, and women have strength. In this way, when one's own respective sound arises, the success of the work associated with that name is attained. **9** Based on that strength, he explains the specific queries regarding pregnancy. When a question is asked regarding "the matter of the womb" <term>garbhārtha; a query about pregnancy or an unborn child</term>, **9** in this context, **9** if the "Odd" <note>masculine</note> state is present, at that moment, if the sounds **A, U,** or **O** are rising, then one should say "it will be a son." If at that time **I** or **E** are 

<margin>rising</margin> 

when the querent asks about their own purpose, then all such things are considered "soft" or feminine. If a transition occurs <note>original: saṅkrama</note>, it indicates death. || **8** || The odd sounds are known to be male; the even sounds are known to be female. When the birth occurs during the rising of their respective sounds, the strength of the male or female is determined accordingly. || **9** || In a query regarding the womb, a son is predicted if a male sound arises, and a daughter if a female sound arises. If the sound is "even" <note>doubled</note>, then twins are predicted; if a transition or "loss" occurs, one should predict the death of the mother. || **20** || When the "pair-channel" <term>yu-nāḍī; a specific configuration of subtle energy channels</term> sound is present for men, a pair of males is born; if for women, a pair of females. If there is a mixture of male and female sounds, one should point to a pregnancy of mixed gender <note>twins of both sexes</note>. || **21** || Such are the variations of queries in the "Momentary Sound-Cycle" from the *Yamala* <note>The Yamala Tantras are a collection of ancient texts dealing with divination and ritual</note>. || || End ||

---

When, in a pregnancy query, a daughter is to be predicted: at the "even" <note>feminine</note> rising, one should predict a pair <note>twins</note> if the male-characteristic sound also rises. If the sound-rising is "lost" or blocked during the query, then the destruction of the pregnancy should be predicted. If a transition occurs at the end of a male-characteristic sound rising, the death of the mother should be predicted. **10** 

Now, he explains the method for the "Even-Sound" rising. The "Pair-Channel" <term>yu-nāḍī</term> sound is the vowel that follows the "Pulse-Sound" <term>nāḍī-svara</term>. At that time, the sound rises in the latter part of the five-ghatika period. If those two are "uneven" <note>odd</note> and have masculine names, then a son should be predicted. If both are feminine, 

<margin>this</margin>

then a daughter should be predicted. If they are male and female—one being male and one being female—then one should say that a son and a daughter <note>twins of opposite sex</note> will be born. If there is a "loss" in either of these, it refers to the loss of that sound's result... **11** Thus ends the Pulse-Cycle.

### Now, he describes the results according to the syllable of the quarter of the birth constellation.

In the birth star, the birth occurs. Depending on which quarter of the birth constellation <note>Nakshatra</note> a person is born in, a specific syllable is assigned—**A, I, U, Ṛ, Ḷ, E, AI, O, AU**, and so on. Based on that syllable, a specific "vibration" <term>Svara; literally 'vowel' or 'sound-force'</term> is established. According to that sound, at the time of birth, one of the five "states," such as the **Infant** and others, must be identified. 

For example, if the person Devadatta is born in the first quarter of his birth star, the vowel **A** is present there. If the "Nadi vibration" at the time of birth was the third state, then the name associated with that state is assigned. Based on the nature of that state, the life results of the birth are determined. **1**

He now describes the results of being born under the various "sound-states" like the **Infant** or **Youth**:

1.  **Infant State:** One is fickle and timid. 
2.  **Youthful State:** One is industrious and skilled in the arts. 
4.  **Young Adult State:** One is endowed with all auspicious marks. 
5.  **Mature State:** One is "conquered by women" <note>highly influenced by or devoted to women</note>. 
6.  **Death State:** One is cruel and performs all actions poorly. 

-> Thus ends the decision on the auspicious and inauspicious results of the Wheel of Momentary Sounds. <-

Now, <term>Bhaṭṭa</term> <note>likely referring to the commentator Bhattotpala</note> provides a specialized explanation on page 66: The consonants starting with **Ka** are the remainder. The seven long vowels are **Ā, Ī, Ū, E, O, Aṃ, Aḥ**. The syllables **Dha, Na, Va, Pa, Va, Ma, Ya, Ra, La, Va, Śa, Ṣa, Sa, Ha** and others are considered strong in their short form... <note>The text here becomes highly technical, correlating specific consonants and vowel lengths with the strength of a person's "breath" or "sound" in various situations.</note>

***

The syllable that corresponds to the quarter of the birth star determines the "Nadi vibration" known here as the innate nature of living beings. **1**

1.  **Infant State:** Fickle, timid, a rogue, or a miser.
2.  **Youthful State:** The child born when this sound is rising is a talker, industrious, skilled in the arts, but can be cruel; yet they are always handsome, long-lived, victorious, and a hero. **3**
3.  **Young Adult State:** One is endowed with all auspicious marks, becomes a righteous king, is always victorious in battle, and remains pure. **4**
4.  **Mature State:** One is "conquered by women," yet religious, lustful, wise, and possesses steady courage. They are a speaker of truth and follow good conduct. **5**
5.  **Death State:** One is cruel in everything, emaciated, has impaired senses, is lazy in all tasks, and is wicked. This occurs when the "Death-rise" of the birth star is active. **6**

-> Thus ends the decision on the auspicious and inauspicious results of the <term>Tatkālika Svara Chakra</term> <note>The Wheel of Momentary Sounds</note> according to the <term>Yāmala</term> <note>A category of ancient Tantric texts</note>. <-

Regarding the vowels from **Ka** to **Da**, there are nine associated with the hand. They are active in the <term>Shukla Paksha</term> <note>the Bright Fortnight/waxing moon</note>; all others belong to the <term>Krishna Paksha</term> <note>the Dark Fortnight/waning moon</note>. **1** I shall separately explain the "Single Sound" system... There, the "Strength of the Fortnight" <term>Paksha Bala</term> must be established, distinguishing between the Bright and Dark moons. **1** 

---

Victory and appearance of warriors: The syllables from **Ka** to **Da** and the vowels **A, I, U, E, O**... these determine the strength and movement of the "Breath" for kings and others. **1**

Now, concerning the victory of a warrior based on the sound, syllable, and the strength of the lunar fortnight: He says that if the sound of the Bright Fortnight is the same for both warriors, then the strength of the "fortnight" is exhausted, and the strength of the ground or the chariot becomes inferior. Even if there is "vibration strength," if the Dark Fortnight strength is present, then on the <term>Shukla Saptami</term> <note>the 7th day of the Bright Fortnight</note>, the weight of the syllables is considered. When the syllable is of the "Short Fortnight," victory or defeat is determined accordingly. **2**

This same meaning is made clear: if a syllable of the Bright Fortnight is a short letter, and for both warriors the sound is of the Bright Fortnight, then the one associated with the Bright Fortnight becomes victorious.

The three letters **A**, **Ka**, and **Cha** belong to the first, sixth, and eleventh <term>Tithis; lunar days</term>. This is the division of letters starting with **I** and the other vowels. || 7 || Now, regarding what should be considered in this diagram, he says: By using the **Letter-Lunar Day** <note>original: "Varṇa-tithi"</note>, one determines the state of the letters within the Sound-Chakra. The names of these states for men are: **Birth**, **Loss**, and **Death**. How are these assigned? He explains:

In the first lunar day, three letters are assigned to **Birth**. Two letters each are assigned to the remaining two states. Thus, for three sets of lunar days, the number of letters should be understood as they move through the cycle. || 7 ||

Through the Letter-Lunar Day, the states of **Birth**, **Loss**, and **Death** are explained:
There is fear in the **Birth** state, sickness in the **Loss** state, and in the **Death** state, death occurs without a doubt. || 8 ||

->॥ This concludes the Special Determination of Sound-Vibrations in the Yamala tradition ॥<-

Regarding **Loss** and **Death**: on the first lunar day, the state is called **Birth**. For the second, the name is **Loss**. For the third, the name is **Death**. This is what is being said: For the group **A**, **Ka**, **Cha**, and **Va**, the first lunar day <note>the 1st, 16th</note> is the **Birth** state; the sixth <note>6th, 21st</note> is the **Loss** state; and the eleventh <note>11th, 26th</note> is the **Death** state. 

For the group containing the letter **Tha**, the sixth lunar day is **Birth**, the eleventh is **Loss**, and the first is **Death**. For the group containing **Ma** and **Ba**, the eleventh is **Birth**, the first is **Loss**, and the sixth is **Death**. In this way, the state of the Letter-Lunar Day should be calculated for the vowels **I** and the rest. 

Now, he describes the results: "Fear in Birth." In the lunar day designated as **Birth**, fear arises. In the **Loss** state, sickness occurs. In the **Death** state, death is certain; one truly meets death. Therefore, during all these inauspicious lunar days, no action should be performed. Following the timing itself, the intention of the poet <note>the author or seer</note> is explained here. To those who listen, the letters **Pa** and **Ṣa** in the lunar cycle are determined by the letters. The lunar days categorized as **Death**, **Birth**, and **Loss** follow the sequence 1, 5, 9, 3, 11, 15... <note>The text here contains a sequence of numbers indicating the mathematical leap between lunar days for different phonetic groups</note>. This is not fruitfulness; rather, it is a warning for your wealth and well-being. || 8 || 

**Thus ends the section on the Sound-Vibration Diagrams.**

### Now, the Timing based on the Name is spoken || 5 ||

To determine the daily results through sound-vibrations, he explains the method for the **Daily Routine** <term>Dincharya; a daily astrological or phonetic forecast</term>. **"As it was said,"** referring to the **"Aggregates of conjunctions."** **"One should write below that"** means: First, one should establish the **Conjunction Sound** <term>Yogaswara; a sound-vibration derived from the combined factors of a name</term>. Then, one should establish the **Aggregate** <note>original: "Pinda"</note> of the Zodiac Sign. Below these, one should establish the knowledge of the **Twelve-fold Timing** <note>original: "Dvadashadi-kala"</note>.

In that grid, below the box for the Conjunction Sound, one should place the Twelve-fold Timing sound. Below the Aggregate sound, place the **Annual** <note>original: "Varshika"</note> sound. Below the **Constellation** <note>original: "Nakshatra"</note> sound, place the **Wealth** sound. Below the **Zodiac Sign** <note>original: "Rashi"</note> sound, place the **Seasonal** <note>original: "Ritu"</note> sound. Below the **Life-force** <note>original: "Jiva"</note> sound, place the **Monthly** sound. Below the **Planet** <note>original: "Graha"</note> sound, place the **Fortnightly** <note>original: "Paksha"</note> sound. Below the **Strength** <note>original: "Bali"</note> sound, place the **Lunar Day** <note>original: "Tithi"</note> sound. Below the **Constellation-Sign** <note>original: "Nakshatra-Rashi"</note> sound, place the **Nadi** <term>Nadi; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> sound.

What should be placed below those? He says that these should be joined with their respective durations: the twelve annual sounds, the eleventh-part sounds, the year, the month, the day, and the twenty-eight Nadi units. These durations should be placed beneath their respective categories. || 3 ||

> As it was spoken in the **Yamala Tantra** <note>An ancient esoteric text focusing on ritual and divination</note> by the grace of the Guru, I shall now declare the light that reveals the daily results of sound-vibrations. || 1 ||
> Conjunction, Aggregate, Zodiac Sign, Life-force, Planet, and the Moving sounds—after extracting these from the name, one should establish these eight in order. || 2 ||
> Below those, write the sounds of the twelve timings, joined with their own durations and the eleventh-part divisions. || 3 ||
> The masters of sound-vibration should understand that the power increases respectively from the Conjunction sounds to the Timing sounds, and the sounds of the rising times. || 4 ||
> The vowels starting with 'A' have the numerical value of 4. By placing them in order, they are known as the **Twenty-fold Strengths** <note>original: "Vinshopaka"</note>. || 5 ||
> Through the Conjunction, twelve years are known; through the Aggregate, the wise know the duration... the Zodiac sounds and their respective sound-vibrations. || 6 ||
> From the Life-force sound, the Monthly sound is spoken; from the Planets...

Now, to explain the results determined by the relative strength of these timing sounds, he says: **"The power increases respectively from the Conjunction,"** and so on. || 4 || **"The vowels starting with A"** means: A = 4, I = 4, U = 4, E = 4, O = 4. Thus, by addition, the total is 20. || 5 || **"Through the Conjunction, twelve years"**... || 6 || **"From the Life-force, the Monthly sound"**... || 7 || **"The twelve-fold result"**... || 8 || **"The sound multiplied by four"**... || 9 || **"Whether far or near"**... || 10 || **"The influence"**... || 11 || **"By twenty"**...

Now, I shall demonstrate the practical application through an example to show how the cause is understood. One should draw four horizontal lines. There, one should write the eight sounds born of the Name and Time according to the prescribed method. Here is the **Daily Routine** for a person named **Devadatta**. In this, we consider the two signs, auspicious and inauspicious. First, the Conjunction sound is the letter 'A', the Twelve-year sound...

<meta>This page continues the technical mathematical breakdown of the "Svarodaya" system. While the previous page established the grid for mapping phonetic sounds to temporal units (years, months, lunar days), this page calculates the specific numerical "strength" of those sounds to determine if a particular time is auspicious or inauspicious for an individual.</meta>

The numerical value for the **Youth** <note>original: "Yuvan"; one of the five 'states' or Avasthas of a sound, representing peak strength</note> state is 4. For the Constellation sound 'O', its lord of the asterism is the sound 'A', which is in the **Child** <note>original: "Kumara"; representing growing strength</note> state; its numerical value is 4. For the Zodiac sign sound 'O', its sound is 'A', which is in the Child state; its numerical value is 4. For the **Life-force** sound <note>original: "Jiva-swara"</note> 'Hro', its sub-period sound is 'Youth', and its value is 4. Its internal sub-period is also 4. The **Letter** sound <note>original: "Varna-swara"</note> is 'U', its period-day sound is the Child state; its numerical value is 4. In this Svarodaya calculation, the Youth value is 4. The **Mantra** sound <note>original: "Matra-swara"</note> is 'A', its pulse sound <note>original: "Nadi-swara"</note> is the Youth state, valued at 4. Thus, the Youth value is 4. In this way, the total **Auspicious** <note>original: "Shubha"</note> count is 36.

Now, for the **Inauspicious** <note>original: "Ashubha"</note> combination: The **Conjunction** sound is 'U', its twelve-year cycle sound is 'I', which is in the **Dead** <note>original: "Mrita"; representing zero strength</note> state; its value is 4. For the **Aggregate** sound 'E', its annual sound is 'I', which is in the **Aged** <note>original: "Vriddha"; representing declining strength</note> state; its value is 4. Here, the rising sound 'U' is 2. The Dead state value is 4. The **Death** sound is 'A'; here the Svarodaya sound 'U' is Dead, its value is 4. The Zodiac sound is 'A', and at the end of its period is the internal sub-period, its value is 4. The **Planet** sound is 'U', and at the end of its period, the five rising sounds are Dead; their value is 8. In this way, the total Inauspicious count is twenty-eight (28). In the union of auspicious and inauspicious:
