<margin>64</margin> In those auspicious contacts, the auspicious count is 8. When the remainder is multiplied by twenty and divided by sixty-four—by this process of multiplication, 160 is produced. Dividing by sixty-four (64), the result is 2 units and 32 parts <note>2:32 Vishopakas</note>. This is the inauspicious result for the letter 'Ka' and others. The auspicious result for 'Ka' is written for that purpose; it was not spoken by the Poet. For an auspicious person, the Youthful twelve-year internal Svarodaya has passed, and those sounds are Youthful. Here, one should give Youthful values. The daily internal rising pulse sounds are Youthful, known as "Awakened Youth." Here, the rising is doubly auspicious. If the master of the auspicious timing has "Dead" sounds, the five (28) should be discarded by people as natural negatives. In that regard, among the conjunction sounds, each subsequent one is stronger: the Pulse sound is stronger than the Conjunction, and the Mantra sound is stronger than the Pulse sound. The author states that the Mantra sound can even indicate the differences in women's temperaments.

For a person named **Devadatta**, at the time of beginning on the first day of the bright fortnight of the month of **Chaitra** <note>the first month of the Hindu lunar calendar</note>, the auspicious result will be five and a half **Ghatis** <term>Ghati; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term>. This result of two and a half is the desired answer for the petitioner. Thus, the purpose of this Svarodaya science is the victory of kings in the conquest of the directions <note>original: "Digvijaya"</note>. Victory comes through courage, strength, and other virtues, and one can conquer a four-fold army <note>original: "Chaturanga"; consisting of elephants, chariots, cavalry, and infantry</note>. Those who are powerful do not grasp this through other means, but only through Svarodaya. Here, having consolidated the various internal results, five threads <note>original: "Sutra"; rules or formulas</note> are written. First, regarding the strength of the letters of a name: although there are three sounds like Mantra and others, the **Letter Sound** <note>original: "Varna-swara"</note> is the most important. The Mantra and others were spoken for the sake of determining the daily results within the twelve-year cycle for a king. And so, it was said by the masters of Svarodaya that the power of the Letter Sound...

While the previous page calculated numerical values for phonetic "states" (child, youth, etc.), this page transitions into the practical application of these strengths—determining when an attacker (Yayi) will triumph over a defender (Sthayi) based on planetary positions, breath patterns, and the location of the Yogini spirits.</meta>

# Ga 96 || Ga-Ja- ||
# || Ja- ||-Hi- ||

<margin>16</margin>
At all times, the strong are considered powerful. For the sake of the **staff-planet** <note>original: "Danda Graha"; a technical term for a specific astrological calculation used to determine the "ruler" of a specific time or action</note>, and for the house, it is said that these are generally unmoving. Likewise, through the strength of the "staff" and the vowels <note>original: "Svara"</note>, victory is proclaimed. First, the five vowels are obtained through the calculation of strength. In the previous section, the lord of the vowel was established as the ruler of whichever direction was mentioned, specifically up to the end of that section. 

Now, the power of other vowels is discussed. The mantra-master says in the *Balalaya* <note>possibly a reference to a specific chapter or sub-text on "the strength of the youthful state"</note>: he who is devoid of the **death-vowel** <note>original: "Mrityu-swara"; a sound associated with zero strength or failure</note> is the one who triumphs. This is achieved through a single mantra... 

Next, the strength and weakness of others are described. At night, the death-vowel should be avoided, and so on. Now, regarding the "staff-planet," the strength and weakness of the "child" and other states are considered. The mantra-master says: in a three-fold dispute, if the vowel is in the **youthful** <note>original: "Taruna"</note> or **child** <note>original: "Kumara"</note> state on that day, it grants victory. Vowels in the "infant" state should be abandoned. 

Now, another calculation of strength: the measurement is called the "unity of strengths." In a conflict between the child and youth states, the **attacker** <note>original: "Yayi"</note> wins. If the attacker is in the forward field and the **defender** <note>original: "Sthayi"</note> is behind, the defender is considered stable. The one who attacks first is the "invader," and the one who attacks later is the "other." In the time of the attacker, the attacker wins through gain; of this, there is no doubt. The defender wins in the stable state; otherwise, there is loss, depending on their respective times. When the vowels move through the eastern and other directions, if one's own vowel is in a "pleasant" position, victory occurs. Victory belongs to the attacker; in the remaining cases, victory does not occur.

Next, another strength calculation: beyond the back, there is no shadow, whether moving to the right or the left. Even if only one person is victorious, the defender is favored if the breath is in the **moon-vowel** <note>original: "Vidhu-swara"; the left nostril/Ida breath</note>. When the shadow of the moon moves to the left and the breath is slow, victory is certain. If the breath is in the **sun** <note>original: "Surya-vahe"; the right nostril/Pingala breath</note>, victory occurs, or if the moon moves to the left.

Next, the characteristics of strength according to the <term>Samara Sara</term> <note>The "Essence of Battle," a classical Sanskrit text on martial astrology and divination</note>: One should act in the east or north. To the king, it shows the power of the western and northern directions at the appropriate time. The **Rahu** <note>The North Node of the Moon, considered a shadow planet</note> and the **Yogini** <note>Fierce directional goddesses whose location determines the luck of a traveler or warrior</note> grant victory when they are three-fold. Victory is granted when the Yogini and Rahu are at one's back. 

Now, another position for the Yogini and Rahu: These four qualities wander through the east and other directions. In the <term>Samudra Danda</term> <note>A specific divinatory technique, likely "The Staff of the Ocean"</note>: By being on the left side of Shiva <note>the lunar breath</note> during the day and night, and then performing actions with the right side <note>the solar breath</note>, one destroys a hundred of the enemy's strengths in battle. 

The names and forms are to be avoided in conflict during specific half-watches <note>original: "Praharardha"; a unit of time roughly equal to 1.5 hours</note> of the day. In the cycle of the years, the sun, Mars, Mercury, the Moon, and Saturn move through the directions. They reside in the center and move through the directions of Jupiter and Venus. They do not wander in the middle of the watch, but move again to the left during the day and night. In the dark fortnight, Rahu <note>original: "Tamah"; darkness</note> grants victory when at the back. According to the order of the days, these should be avoided by those seeking victory. 

According to the <term>Samara Sara</term>: He who goes to battle should avoid the "staff" calculation when the lord of the vowel-direction is in the house of an enemy at the time of rising. One should always avoid the "staff" when it is singular. The rays of Venus move toward the lord of the directions, and the directions of the north-east and others are difficult to reach. In the second watch, the movement is as follows... in the third watch, and again in the third watch of the night... from the north-east, the sun revolves... to the south... in the watch.

# ॥ Now, the Chart of Daily Results ॥

| Astrological Combination | Division | Lunar Mansion | Zodiac Sign | Vital Force | Planet | Class/Color | Fractional Part |

The third and then the third, or the second, are destroyed. <note>In this context, "destroyed" or "killed" refers to one astrological factor negating or dominating another.</note> Twice, in the middle of a watch <note>original: "praharārdhe"; a "prahara" is a three-hour period, so this refers to a 90-minute interval</note>, it does not strike according to the sequence of directions. A journey undertaken during the second period results in a struggle on the sixth day. The divisions of the directions, the sun, and the moon are like horses being led; they follow their own path. On whichever day the Lord of the Day <note>the planet ruling that specific day of the week</note> is present, he strikes the daily wisdom.

The **Sun** strikes **Saturn** and **Venus**; **Mercury** strikes **Jupiter**; the **Sun** goes to **Mars**; **Mars** strikes the **Zodiac**; **Venus** strikes the **Moon**; and **Jupiter** is struck in turn. <note>This "striking" or "killing" (hanti) describes a system of planetary enmity where one planet's influence overcomes another's during combat.</note> From the starting direction to the next, the lord of that direction strikes. In the **planetary hour of the directional lord** <term>Diknath-kala-hora: a specific sub-division of the day ruled by the planet governing a cardinal direction</term>, the essence is purified. During an unfavorable half-watch, the Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note>, the Moon, and the Sun are in the path. The **strength of the Moon** <term>Chandra-balam: a measurement of the Moon's auspiciousness based on its position relative to one's birth sign</term> and the **planetary hour** <term>Hora: a one-hour duration ruled by a specific planet</term> are the most powerful factors. If the names are opposing in the battle, it is considered unfavorable.

Now, regarding the **warrior** <term>Yodha: the combatant or person for whom the calculation is made</term>, the place where the birth sign and the rising sign meet is where great power resides. The "striking" <note>original: "ghāta"; an astrological affliction or a literal blow</term> is determined from the rising sign: the Sun is in the 12th, Shiva in the 11th, the Direction in the 10th, the Sky in the 9th, the Lamp in the 8th, the Veda in the 4th... <note>The author uses poetic synonyms for numbers: Shiva=11, Direction=10, Veda=4, etc.</note> through these positions of the Sun, Moon, Venus, Mercury, and Jupiter, the **affliction** <term>Ghata: a point of vulnerability or danger in a person's horoscope</term> is calculated. It may strike the head, the face, or the heart region equally.

According to the rules, one should abandon Saturn, the Moon, and Jupiter in the 5th and 7th positions. In the calculation table, one should draw twelve vertical lines and six horizontal lines. The wise scholar should not write the "neuter" vowels or classes <note>vowels like ṛ, ṝ, lṛ, lṝ which are considered "powerless" in this system</note>. Excluding the k-class, c-class, and ṭ-class <note>the consonant groupings of the Sanskrit alphabet</note>, one should write the numbers below: 
The Anthill: 5/5; the Deer: 3/3; the Serpent: 6/6; the Elephant: 8/8. 
One should not write these at the end. For the two warriors, one should calculate the count of the vowels and the number of letters separately. 

If the remainder is 1, he is the victor; if <unclear>other</unclear> remainders occur, the warrior is defeated. <gloss>In this case</gloss>, if the remainder is zero (0), four (4), six (6), five (5), seven (7), three (3), or two (2), the warrior who is attacking is the winner. He whose remainder is "Veda" (4) is the victorious warrior. If the remainder is "Veda" (4) or "Shunya" (0), the winner is Jaimini. If the sum is six (6), he is the winner. If the sum is seven (7) or five (5), or seven (7) and three (3), victory is assured.

# ॥ 17 ॥ Narapati-Jayacarya ॥
# Commentary on Victory ॥

<margin>17</margin>
By calculating the sound of the Sun’s Mansion <note>original: "jayabhā-dhvanitaiyā"; the phonetic resonance of the lunar mansion the sun is currently occupying</note>, one determines the heavy slaughter in battle. Or, from the total [calculation], whether one survives. Why then speak of the slaughter of the heavy/great? In the kingdom... [the text lists numerical values for planetary strengths: 3, 4, 8, 8, 4, 3, 0]. The circle of the Yoginis <note>eight directional goddesses whose positions shift and dictate auspicious directions for travel or attack</note>... [numerical values: 6, 12]. The victor of all forces, exceeding the sixteenth, is the group of eight.

Now, the Commander <term>Senāpati: the planetary "general" ruling the day or the specific division of the army</term> of the Wheel is to be determined. In the second [calculation], the flight of the enemy is predicted. Now, the rule for the forces is stated: one’s own body, the wind, the Yogini, the North Node <note>original: "Rāhu"</note>, and the strength of the Earth. Now, whichever master is established there, that force is the stronger. Regarding the vowels: the vowels beginning with ‘A’ <note>the Sanskrit vowels a, i, u, e, o</note> are established in the five directions starting with the East. Now, with the body... when the wind is at the back, it brings victory, just as with the Moon's alignment. As the rays of the Sun are in the East, the strength of the Sun <term>bhānu-valam</term> is like the sun god Martanda himself. Regarding the moon, fire, and others: the Yogini and Rahu are the strength of the Earth. Where the field and the time are primary, there are two Wheels of Victory and Defeat for the embodied.

Now, the strengths with the rising portions are written. One, three, or from where the **Hoda Wheel** <term>Hoḍā-cakra: a specific astrological chart used to determine name-syllables and their compatibility with directions or opponents</term> is established; the staff, the fist, and the great battle; in the palm, in the fort, and the directions are held in sequence.

One should measure the height of the divine for the two warriors... that day should be known for the battle that arises with victory. I shall speak here of the result in the fourfold [army] and the fort. When the battle occurs while the Sun traverses the fixed, the fierce, or the moving mansions at dawn, it is conducive to death. In the other three quarters [of the mansion], the battle leads only to injury. A strike to the head, to the face, a strike to the heart or the stomach. In whichever sign the Sun is located, if it is the ascendant of the two warriors, that sign or ascendant in battle leads to injury or death.

During a siege of a fort <note>original: "durga-rodhe"</note>, the wise [should observe]: if the Sun has entered the seventh sign from the warriors... if it is in the right [quadrant], the strike occurs in the head. From the right of the sun, the breaker of the fort attains victory. If the [Sun's] count is higher, the king is either captured or perishes in battle. A warrior should personally avoid the Sun when it is in the state of "hundred-fold exhaustion."

Now I shall speak of another method via the **Hoda** knowledge based on the Sun’s Mansion <note>original: "sūrya-bhāt"; counting lunar mansions starting from the one currently occupied by the sun</note>:
- If it is the **10th** mansion: Death.
- If it is the **12th**: When the battle occurs, the Sun overcomes [the warrior]; though he is struck, the warrior lives.
- If the battle occurs on the **14th** mansion from the Sun: Injury to the birth-foot <note>a specific part of the natal star calculation</note> or destruction by a heavy wind.
- Likewise, in the **15th** mansion: If the battle occurs, the warrior falls into the hands of the enemy.
- In the **19th** mansion: The soldier is captured in the great war.
- In the **21st** mansion: Death occurs by the casting of weapons.
- In the **24th** mansion: A strike occurs in the warrior’s own field/region by a mantra.

If the birth-foot is in Capricorn or Pisces, one should avoid that time and region for battle, especially the "Staff-Time" <note>daṇḍa-muhūrta: a specific inauspicious time-period</note>. [Repeat of the count]: 10th, 7th, 12th, 14th, the Pushya mansion, the 21st, and the moving 20th from the Sun’s Mansion...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions of the Narapati-Jayacarya (The King’s Victory-Craft), transitioning from general planetary strengths to the specific movement of the Yoginis (directional goddesses) and the calculation of "burnt" or inauspicious dates. # The Jewel of Victory (Jayendram) 10.12.4.4.12.2.24
# On the Movement of the Yoginis

The rotation of the Yoginis <note>eight female deities whose shifting positions dictate the "lucky" direction for a day</note> occurs every four <term>Ghatikas: a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> starting from sunrise. Their movement follows this sequence of directions:
1. North <note>original: "Shakata," the cart/North</note>
20. North-Northwest <note>original: "Dhanade," the direction of the god of wealth, Kubera</note>
3. Northeast <note>original: "Kona"</note>
4. Southwest <note>original: "Nairitya"</note>
5. South <note>original: "Dakshine"</note>
6. West/Water <note>original: "Jale"</note>
7. Northwest/Wind <note>original: "Pavane"</note>
8. North-Northeast/Lord of the Mountain <note>original: "Girishe"</note>

Their rotation enters these positions in order. One who understands the beginning of this rise will achieve victory over a four-division army <note>the traditional Indian army consisting of chariots, elephants, cavalry, and infantry</note>. The Yogini grants victory when she is positioned at the back or to the right of the warrior.

### The Rising of the Current Day
Now, the calculation for the "Time-Day" rising:
On whichever day a specific day-lord rules, his "consumption" <note>the portion of the day he influences</note> is declared. After four Ghatikas, he rises again at the back. Through seven Ghatikas, the current ruling "Time-Day" is known. For example, the sequence moves from the Moon (Monday) to Saturn (Saturday) in steps of seven.
Whichever zodiac sign belongs to a warrior, a specific planet is known as its lord. If the battle occurs on that lord's day or during his specific "rising" time, the effort will become fruitless. Furthermore, if the lord of the current day is positioned in front of the warrior, it is similarly fruitless.

In a conflict between a King and a Priest <note>original: "Indra-Yuddha-Vipra"</note>, the Priest faces defeat in the battle. Just as victory is seen for one, there is ruin and destruction for the other.

### The Numerical Constants of the Zodiac
The fixed constants <term>Dhruvakas: numerical values assigned to zodiac signs for divinatory math</term> for the signs starting from Aries are as follows:

| Zodiac Sign | Constant Value |
| :--- | :--- |
| Aries (Mesha) | 456 |
| Taurus (Vrishabha) | 53 |
| Gemini (Mithuna) | 53 |
| Cancer (Karka) | 36 |
| Leo (Simha) | 54 |
| Virgo (Kanya) | 15 |
| Libra (Tula) | 40 |
| Scorpio (Vrischika) | 42 |
| Sagittarius (Dhanu) | 55 |
| Capricorn (Makara) | 15 |
| Aquarius (Kumbha) | 36 |
| Pisces (Meena) | 46 |
| *Alternate total* | 56 |

In the sequence starting from Aries, one should combine the constants of the two combatants. Divide the sum by three; the remainder determines the clash. If the "Power-Striker" <note>original: "Shaka-prahari"</note> is the victor in a duel, the one with the remaining value loses. If the remainder is one, or five, the warrior will eventually attain victory.
The Power-Striker (15), following the sequence (18), will destroy the enemy. The one at the back (15) will destroy the one at the front (15). In a duel, the direction is clearly specified in this manner.

On the day when the Moon <note>original: "Nishanatha," the Lord of Night</note> is in alignment with the warrior's birth sign, that warrior attains victory; there is no other conclusion. If the birth sign rises at the back or to the right, no defeat occurs. Therefore, one should not fear the enemy, just as a thief does not fear the darkness. When the Sun is in the 27th mansion <note>Nakshatra</note> and the wealth is doubled, the warrior whose birth sign is rising will face death in the struggle.

---

### On the Burnt Dates
Now, the "Burnt Dates" <term>Dagdha Tithis: specific lunar days considered "burnt" or destroyed by solar influence, making them inauspicious</term> are explained for avoiding battle. In the beginning, middle, and end of the Sagittarius sign, and in others, the hours are calculated.
The 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th, and 12th lunar days are particularly "burnt" when the "lotus-awakener" (the Sun) is in certain positions. These must be avoided for the deployment of rutting elephants.

**Death Predictions by Ascendant:**
*   **Aries Ascendant:** Death in the sign of Gemini.
*   **Virgo Ascendant:** Death of the killer himself.
*   **Cancer Ascendant:** Death for the Leo/Scorpio signs.
*   **Libra Ascendant:** Death via a "thorn" (hidden enemy or trap).
*   **Leo Ascendant:** Death occurs in Scorpio.
*   **Virgo Ascendant:** Death occurs in Pisces.
*   **Libra Ascendant:** Death in Scorpio; for Scorpio, death in Aquarius.
*   **Sagittarius Ascendant:** Death in Taurus or Capricorn.
*   **Libra Ascendant:** Death for Aquarius.
*   **Pisces Ascendant:** Death in Gemini.

### Fate of Military Units
Now I shall speak of the combinations of days and military units.
The **Bird** <note>likely referring to omens or specific infantry units</note> meets death if the battle is on a Sunday.
The **Pot-Bearer** <note>Kumbha/Infantry</note> meets death on the day of Mars (Tuesday).
The **Elephants** meet death on Thursdays.
The **Horses** meet death on Mondays.
On Saturdays, death occurs for [the infantry], but the **Elephant** attains victory.
Wise men avoid battle during the solar transition through the 20th degree. That entire day and night are excluded by those who are discerning.

If the battle begins in the first Muhurta <note>a 48-minute period</note>, the middle is held by Gemini. At the 12th Muhurta, death comes to Gemini and Libra. At the 9th Muhurta, death comes to Cancer after the pair. For Sagittarius, Libra, and Leo, death is obtained later.
The shadow of the feet and the Moon (1) combined, divided by the Earth (1), determines the result. The warrior who stands at the end of the Power-Constant meets death; the remaining one rises like the moon from the water. Just as in the case of Shiva <note>original: "Hare"</note>, the body is anointed [for burial] when the numerical constant is 36.

It also lists "burnt dates" (Dagdha Tithis) and specific days when different military divisions, like elephants and horses, are most vulnerable.</summary>

<page-num>18</page-num>
<header>॥ 18 ॥ Na. ॥ Ja ॥ ॥ Ja Hi ॥</header>

<margin>18</margin>
The remaining positions of the lamps are declared here. If smoke moves toward the South or the Northwest, or if it appears in the Northeast or the left side, it signifies the onset of defeat. Within the circle of the Sun and Moon, the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> and months must be calculated. Now, for the placement of the Sun <note>the solar position in the zodiac</note>, consider the month’s constellation and the lunar date <term>Tithi</term>. On a given day, the Moon’s movement <note>original: "bhukti"</note> is calculated as two and a quarter <insert>2 1/4</insert> Ghatikas <term>traditional units of 24 minutes</term>. The wise should always place the Sun in the 15th position. When the Sun is properly placed and the Moon is in the fire-quarter, victory is certain—there is no doubt—even against twenty-one enemies.

### On the Mobile Yogini

The Mobile Yogini <note>original: "Char Yogini," a directional goddess whose position shifts</note> resides in the directions of the East <note>Indra</note>, the Northwest <note>Vayu</note>, the South <note>Yama</note>, the Northeast <note>Isha</note>, the West <note>Varuna</note>, the Southeast <note>Agni</note>, the North <note>Kubera</note>, and the Southwest <note>Nairitya</note>. In this way, she stays in each direction for a specific duration of time <term>Ghatikas</term> starting from the first lunar day. She moves between the Northeast, the South, the Wind-quarter (Northwest), and the Fire-quarter (Southeast), and finally to the Southwest, the North, the West, and the East. For those seeking success, victory is achieved when the Yogini is placed to the left. In battle, both sides are affected by the Yogini's position to the left or right. It is said that the Yogini grants victory when on the right, but causes retreat <note>original: "palayanam"</note> when on the left. However, if she is on the left and facing the practitioner, she grants victory; if on the right, she is auspicious.

### On the Battle Yogini

Regarding the Battle Yogini <note>original: "Sangrama Yogini"</note>, her gaze is calculated through numerical measures: twenty-five <insert>25</insert>, two steps, ten points <insert>10</insert>, the left arm, three <insert>3</insert>, twelve <insert>12</insert>, and twenty-three <insert>23</insert> for the right-moving Moon. At twelve <insert>12</insert> Ghatikas, the wise should always avoid the gaze of the Yogini; scholars know that she must be avoided in all ways for success.

### On Rahu

Rahu <note>the shadow planet or North Node</note> moves through the directions of men, the wind, the demons, the celestial musicians, and the fire-dwellers. Moving through the circle, Rahu wanders through the eight directions. He wanders through the West, the Fire-quarter <insert>3</insert>, the North <insert>7</insert>, the Southwest <insert>4</insert>, the East <insert>1</insert>, the Northwest <insert>6</insert>, the South, and the Ashwin-quarter <insert>8</insert>. By calculating from one’s birth star <term>Janma Nakshatra</term>, the "vehicle" for the day is determined. Count from the birth star to the current star and divide by nine <insert>9</insert>; the remainder reveals the vehicle.

The vehicles are the Donkey, the Horse, the Elephant, the Peacock, the Lion, the Crow, the Jackal <note>original: "ortako"</note>, and the Human.
- On the **Donkey**: There will be loss of wealth.
- On the **Horse**: There will be victory.
- On the **Elephant**: One will attain the company of loved ones and great joy.
- On the **Jackal**: Defeat or failure occurs.
- On the **Lion**: Victory in battle is achieved.
- On the **Crow**: One suffers distress and hardship.
- On the **Peacock**: One gains wealth and prosperity.
- On the **Human**: Success in the journey is indicated.

### Thus ends the Wheel of Vehicles

### On the Sun as the Lord of the Day

One should not write... <note>text becomes highly abbreviated</note>. Where the birth-name starts, the fifth place is the Time-indicator. When the calculated time is auspicious, it is favorable; if the "noose" <note>original: "pashe"</note> is to the left, it is good.

### On the Calculation of Instantaneous Time

The eleven <insert>11</insert> Rudras constitute the body of Time <term>Kala</term>. The four <insert>4</insert> Vedas are the limbs. Through the dates <term>Tithi</term> and the remaining divisions, the directions are assigned in order.

### Now, regarding the Ground: The Eye-Boundary

The Eye-Boundary <note>original: "Netra Pali," a technical term for an astrological grid or visual boundary used in divination</note>. Starting from the month of **Chaitra** <note>the first month of the Hindu calendar, approx. March-April</note> and the others, the four quarters of the day <term>Praharas</term> are calculated. In the middle path, the positions of the months are counted. Having observed the great power of the Eye-Boundary... for those who possess it... for the Lord of the People, 108 in the south.

Indra <note>East</note> is 9, Isha <note>Northeast</note> is 11, Vayu <note>Northwest</note> is 5, and Vaishravana <note>Kubera/North</note> is 7. In these directions, the transition of the Sun <term>Sankranti</term> occurs. Combined with 3, the wisdom of the lunar dates <term>Tithis</term> arises. By dividing the planet Rahu, the desired outcome or the direction of the breath is found.

Shakra <note>Indra/East</note> is 9, the Lord of the World <note>Isha/Northeast</note> is 7, Varuna <note>West</note> is 5, and the Fire-quarter <note>Southeast</note> is 3. In these eight directions, the rotation of the months starting with **Chaitra** occurs. From sunrise, the influence expands: Isha is 11, the Sun is 12, the Southwest is 8—these are the hours <term>Ghatikas</term> of the remaining time.

The King is 8, the circle is 5, the Fire <note>Agni</note> is 3, combined with 7. In the direction of the Rakshasa <note>Southwest</note> it is 4, 5, or 1. Wind <note>Northwest</note>, Fire <note>Southeast</note>, Water <note>West</note>, and the Lord of Wealth <note>Kubera/North</note>. By the rotation of the months starting with **Chaitra**, the knowledge of Rahu is obtained once per day.

In the Wind-quarter 6, Water 5, Rudra <note>Northeast</note> 8, Varuna <note>West</note> 5, Fire 3, and Rakshasa 4. In the East 1, Fire 3, Water and the Moon-quarter 7, the transition of the month of **Chaitra** occurs. The day, the half-day, the watch, and the half-watch are thus declared. By this measure of threes, the one who gives six becomes victorious.

### On the Position of the Iron Vessel

In the "Collection Circle," at the beginning of the heart, in the square of **Krittika** <note>the third lunar mansion, Pleiades</note> and the others, the results of the household's union should be spoken, arising from various falls like the ocean. When staying on a single line, Rahu creates "Iron," and the Crested One <note>Ketu</note> creates the "Earth." When Saturn, the Sun, and Rahu meet, that is called "Iron"; from them, one should point out the "Iron" omen.

In the three lines of **Krittika**, when the Sun, Saturn, and Rahu are present, and the movement of the wheel is in **Aries** <note>Mesha</note>, one should designate it as "Iron." ॥ 11 ॥

In the East, the Deer <note>Mrigashira</note>, and in **Aries**, the moon is wise. When the "Fall" occurs during a swift rising, the "Thunderbolt" <term>Vajra</term> is produced in its entirety. When the fall is in the direction of the North or the Northeast, and Jupiter <note>Jiva</note> and Mars <note>Bhauma</term> are on the line, the "Thunderbolt" resides there. In **Aquarius** or **Pisces**, a fall is produced, like the trumpeting of a glorious elephant.

From the separate positions of the lines and the positions of the stars <term>Nakshatras</term>, Rahu enters the center. When Mars stays in the square of the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term>, and Rahu is in the middle, it is cast as the "Fall of the Thunderbolt." ॥ 6 ॥

One should know the East 11, 1, and 3 as the numbers of the directions. In the Lion <note>Leo</note>, when Mars is equal, fire and wind are produced in the middle step of the star. Redness and gold on a single line indicate a great animal, staying in the tawny-colored zodiac signs. By another's strike, the "Fall of Iron" is produced, and in the middle of the day, the path is destroyed. ॥ 7 ॥

Saturn and the Sun on a single line... truly there is only one. In **Leo**, the Sun, Mars, and Saturn, if in a fixed Ascendant, there is "Iron." In the line of the laboring star, the 5th, 10th, 5th, and 7th positions... the Moon is nourished in the stars; so it should be pointed out. ॥ 21 ॥

On the nineteenth line, Jupiter and the Sun are the kings of the earth. In the first part of the Ascendant, it is "Iron"; in the second part, it is "Thunderbolt." ॥ 22 ॥

When the Moon is on the line where the letters stay, and the planet has entered the Ascendant, one should designate it as the "Fall of Iron." ॥ 23 ॥

Where the three lines of **Krittika** are spread out, in that star-name, "Great Iron" exists in the joined portion of the letters. ॥ 24 ॥

Where the Son of the Earth <note>Mars</note>, the Lord of Speech <note>Jupiter</note>, or the Poet <note>Venus</note> stays on the line, in the Ascendant of the "Water-Book," that is called an "Increase." ॥ 25 ॥

In the triangle of the circle, staying on the first line, the directions are white. When the Moon arrives there on the second line, it is like the time of the fire's appearance. ॥ 36 ॥

At the beginning of a **Moving Sign** <note>original: "Chara-rashi," referring to Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn</note>, there is indeed a **Fall of Iron** <term>Lohapata: a technical term indicating weapon strikes or military conflict</term>. In the middle of a moving sign, there is water and wind. At the end of a moving sign, there is water. This is what is declared regarding the **Triangle** <note>original: "Trikone," a specific structural grouping in an astrological chart</note>. **(17)** In the remaining **Fixed Signs** <note>original: "Sthira-rashi," referring to Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius</note>, they speak of fire. In the middle of a fixed sign, there is water and wind. At the beginning of a fixed sign, there is a fall of iron. Those who are wise speak of this according to the **Uddisha** teachings <note>referring to the Uddisha-Tantra, an ancient manual of ritual and divination</note>. **(18)** When mild planets and **Rahu** <note>the North Node of the Moon, a shadow planet associated with chaos</note> arrive on a single line, it is like the birth of Rahu. From that joy, the wise say, one's hair stands on end <note>original: "Lomaharshanam," a sign of intense spiritual or physical thrill</note>. **(19)** If **Saturn** and the **Moon** arrive on a single line, then in the collision of war, one should not point towards the right side. **(20)** When a cruel planet arrives on the line of the Moon, it may signal a battle in some places. In the ten parts of a flaw, three are in the triangle, according to the teachers. **(21)** In a state of helplessness or in the "lower path," when two or three cruel planets <term>Krura-kheta: planets like Mars or Saturn that exert a harsh influence</term> fall on a single line, a state of war arises. **(22)** When joined with the rays of a favorable day, and with a cruel planet or with death, why would it signal a battle? Unless another cruel planet is present. **(23)** When **Rohini** <note>a prominent lunar mansion or star</note>, **Saturn**, and the Moon, or the "Mist-Maker" <note>the Moon</note>, arrive on a single line, there will be doubt regarding a **Fall of Water** <term>Jalapata: an omen for heavy rain or floods</term>. **(24)** From the fire of **Ketu** <note>the South Node of the Moon</note> and **Mars**, and through **Jupiter** and **Venus**, there is water. Without the "mist-fall" <note>heavy fog or snow</note>, the Moon, being joined with speed, causes this. **(25)** From the **Sun** and Rahu, one should fear; however, by my fame, there is splendor. When joined with Jupiter, the Moon brings peace and evenness. **(26)** Houses of wisdom, courage, and expenditure (12th, 7th, 6th, 6th, 2nd, 1st). Moving counter-clockwise from the Moon, this is remembered as a source of strength. **(27)** Having understood the strength and weakness of these cruel and mild planets, one should speak of "the fall" and "the strike" accordingly. **(28)** When cruel planets, Rahu, **Venus**, and the Moon are on a single line, there will be a **Fall of Blood** <term>Raktapata: a grave omen of violent slaughter</term> for as long as the Moon is joined with them. **(29)** Whenever the Moon is joined with Jupiter and Venus, or if they are on a single line, a Fall of Water occurs then. **(30)** When mild planets are joined at the beginning, or on a single line, there is peace on earth. **(31)** If two planets are at war in one place, or if the Moon is there, and if the Sun is also there, it could dry up the very ocean. **(32)** When the Moon and the **Son of the Earth** <note>original: "Dharani-suta," a name for the planet Mars</note> are on a single line, if Jupiter goes there, there is no doubt it brings life. **(33)**

->**Now he speaks of the Lunar Diagram**<-

Starting with the "Fire" positions, the wise one should write counter-clockwise, multiplied by the directions and subtracted by one. **(34)** When the "Fall of Iron" occurs, the battlefield is established. When "Fire" and "Fall of Water" occur, one should speak of the "Fall of Wind" in the same way. **(35)** In the case of Fire or in the most excellent of the signs, the wise one should speak of the "Fall." When the Sun is in two [places], the planets should be written in those signs where they stay for five **Ghatikas** <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>. After that, they should be placed in the second sign. Again, after ten **Dandas** <note>time units</note>, they should be placed in the third sign. One should contemplate the placement in this manner.

->**Thus ends the Five Falls in the Triangle**<-

The wise one should write the **Three-Nadi Diagram** <term>Tri-nadi Chakra: a grid based on three "channels" or lines of energy</term> starting from the Brahmin year. From the combination of the day and the birth star <term>Nakshatra</term>, one should then speak of the Fall of Iron.

->**Thus ends the Three-Nadi Diagram**<-

When there is a "piercing" <term>Vedha: an astrological aspect where one planet 'afflicts' a point</term> in a single region of the triangle, it indicates fear. Wherever the Sun is in two, the wise designate that as the planet's name. The wise one moves from ascendant to ascendant, measuring five Ghatikas each. Rahu and Ketu, in the same path, are the slayers in the destruction. Rahu is the killer...

...moving quickly. When a malefic planet <note>original: "Krura-graha," such as Mars or Saturn</note> is situated in the Ascendant, one should predict **Iron** <term>Loha: literally 'metal,' here referring specifically to weapons, injury, or the clashing of blades</term>. Depending on whether the planets are slightly more or less powerful, the effects come from the Sun and others. Through conjunction with an enemy, it is terrifying; through a friend, it does not occur.

->**Thus ends the Twelve-Spoked Iron.**<-

### Regarding the Collection of Rites for the "Drinking of Iron"

Now, the rules for bathing and preparing for the **Drinking of Iron** <note>Lohapana: likely a ritual involving the consecration of weapons or a metaphor for the first blood drawn in battle</note>. On the lunar mansions of Ashvini, Hasta, Pushya, Shatabhisha, Chitra, Punarvasu, and the two Bhadrapadas, or in the fields of the Vishvedevas <note>the lunar mansion Uttarashadha</note>, these four are for satisfaction.

When seeking the path of **Iron**, if one is near a village or water, the "dust" of the **Drinking of Iron** is described as follows:

1. Take the lunar day <note>Tithi</note> and multiply by 5.
2. Add the value of the day's planet.
3. Divide the Tithi by 3.

If the remainder is one, it indicates "Water-Iron" <note>blood shed near water or a naval clash</note>. If the remainder is two, it indicates Iron in the village or the sky <note>aerial/hill combat</note>. If it is zero, it belongs to one's own self. 

### Predictions for Battle

In the first fifteen minutes <note>Ghatika</note> of the lunar day, the goddess Bhairavi's gaze falls upon the Ascendant, and **Iron** <note>warfare</note> is certainly to be feared.

## The Sage Diagram (Kavi Chakra)

Now, the **Sage Diagram** <note>original: "Kavi Chakra," often used to determine the "spirit" or "voice" of an army</note> is explained. Whether at sunrise, sunset, or when the time of midnight has passed; at the end of grief, or when greed arises in the heart; in marriage, meeting friends, or the gathering of enemies; at a pilgrimage site, a temple, a sacrifice, or when an army is struck—the Leader <note>Vinayaka</note> is the one who decides.

In many ways, the **Sage** <note>the Kavi or the "Poet-seer"</note> is found at the time of speech. Knowing these, the Sage should perform his duties day and night. This Sage Diagram is declared as a square <note>original: "Chaturasram"</note>.

### Table of Omens in the Kavi Chakra

| Omen Bird | Planetary Day | Condition | Result |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Peacock** <note>Shikhi</note> | Tuesday | Conjunction with Sun's mansion | Fire and battle |
| **Owl** <note>Pingalika</note> | Friday | During entry | Fear of fire for the traveler |
| **Pigeon** <note>Kapoti</note> | Tuesday | On Punarvasu mansion | Death of the traveler |
| **Crow** <note>Kavi</note> | Saturday | On Rohini mansion | Death of the one who has gone |
| **Sparrow** <note>Chataka</note> | Sunday | On Hasta mansion | Destruction of the traveler's army |

If the Moon is in the birth sign or the seventh house <note>the house of partnerships and open enemies</note> on the day of the Moon <note>Monday</note>, one should avoid all mental exertion. If a malefic enters that direction, there is fear. If a benefic <note>Saumya</note> planet departs, there is a peaceful departure in that direction.

If the Moon is in the birth sign, the seventh, the fifth, or the ninth house, it indicates the breaking <note>defeat</note> of the supreme leader. Having calculated the 19th mansion <note>the "Ghaata" or fatal mansion</note>, one should conduct the Sage’s War, leaving behind the direction of the fatal strike. 

When the Moon is in Rohini, those who reside in the city may be slain at midnight. By this policy, when the planets join, the King of men <note>the King</note> or the place itself may be conquered.

->**Thus ends the Sage Diagram (Kavi Chakra).**<-

### General Calculations for Success

One should calculate the remainders:
*   **Man:** 2
*   **Joy:** 1
*   **Delusion:** 3
*   **Bondage:** 9
*   **Success:** 10

Through the **Man** calculation, delusion is turned away by ten-fold qualities. Through **Success**, achievement is born in the four types of battle.

The Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Saturn, Jupiter, Rahu <note>original: "Tamas," the shadow planet</note>, and Venus. These are the planets classified by their nature and the days they rule. They are either "cruel" <note>Krura: malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note> or "soft" <note>Mridu: benefic planets like Venus or the Moon</note>. One must observe the birth-star, the malevolent spirit star <note>Dakini-bha: a specific unfavorable lunar mansion</note>, and the position of Rahu to predict death or destruction of a position. 

When a planet is in a favorable position, success is achieved; otherwise, there is great suffering and disease. **Now, from the *Dialogue of Bhairava and Gauri*, concerning the Collection of Triangles.** 

In a triangle marked with nine rods <note>Danda: lines or divisions in an astrological chart</note> and joined with the Moon, the Goddess should observe the Moon passing through the constellations from Krittika onwards, joined with malefic planets. The Moon may move counter-clockwise <note>Apasavya</note> or clockwise <note>Savya</note>, just as the Sun does. One should point to "Iron" <note>Loha: signifying weapons or bloodshed</note> if these movements align in a specific way.

Clockwise movements begin from Krittika, and counter-clockwise from Magha. Friendship-based movements are clockwise, while those fixed in the Vasus <note>Dhanishta nakshatra</note> are counter-clockwise. One should calculate these clockwise and counter-clockwise movements for all auspicious works. When Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and the Moon are joined with Rahu, there will be a "Flame of Fire" <note>Agni-jvala</note> caused by the wind of iron <note>the clash of weapons</note>; of this, there is no doubt.

When it is said that a "Single Channel" <term>Ek Nadi: a specific astrological alignment where two planets occupy the same subtle energy path</term> occurs between Ketu and Mars, on that day a great battle between four-fold armies <note>Chaturanga: elephants, chariots, cavalry, and infantry</note> will occur. If Saturn and the Sun, or Mercury and the Moon, are so aligned, a terrible war breaks out. 

If the "Thunderbolt-Sun" <note>Vajra-Arka</note> is seen or if there is a "Slow Line" <note>Manda-rekha</note>, and if the Moon and Mars <note>original: "Dharani-suta," son of the Earth</note> enter a Single Channel, then life itself is scorched, and stones fall like water. Multiply the divisions starting from the rising sign by nine and divide by twenty <note>20</note>. Combine this with the pearls <note>likely a count of points</note>; the remainder indicates the "Planets of Time" <note>Kalika-graha</note>.

**Thus ends the Collection Diagram.**

The commander of the army is situated at the feet; a person of poor form is at the... <unclear>gantavyatih</unclear>. In the belly, the Khadira <note>possibly referring to the resilient wood used for stakes or weapons</note> is said to be placed; in the heart, the carriers. On the shoulders are the elephants; on the fingers of the hands are the archers. The wind-instruments are in the... <unclear>kadeke</unclear>; the advisors and the tongue are the teeth. 

The deity is at the... <unclear>tni</unclear>; in the ears are the scouts. Banners, umbrellas, whisks, and flags are represented by the hair. In the movements of the commander, all services are issued. In setting out, one should see:
*   In the ears: 2 omens
*   In the eyes: 1 omen
*   In the nose: 1 omen
*   On the tongue: 1 omen
*   In the teeth: 4 omens
*   In the... <unclear>sthe</unclear>: 2 omens
*   On the shoulders: 2 omens
*   In the mouth: 2 omens
*   On the fingers: 2 omens
*   In the heart: 4 omens
*   One each in the belly and the private parts.
*   Four in the two feet.

Such is the placement of all equipment in the form of the army. Whichever body part of this diagram a "cruel" planet occupies, that corresponding part of the army will be pierced or broken by enemies. Whichever part a "soft" planet occupies, that part shall obtain victory. 

If Rahu is in the path, the vehicles and elephants are falsely lost. If Mars is in the position of the infantry, or if two elephants are in the position of... <unclear>mada</unclear>, then the four-fold army is crowned with pain. If Ketu is in its place, victory is born. On whichever body part all the cruel planets gather, death will surely come to that part of the army. 

Mixed planets give mixed results. If the soft planets and Saturn are in the path of the Sun, or if Saturn and the Swift-Planet <note>Mercury</note> are double-attained, the King is destroyed by time. Here, Rahu and Ketu <note>original: "Shikhin"</note> cause imprisonment at night. Mars causes the destruction of infantry and the breaking of chariots. 

Cruel planets situated below the navel bring victory if they are "soft" in the belly; otherwise, they bring death to the soldiers and the King. If the malefic planets are empowered, they cause false retreats and breaks in battle. If the "soft" planets are more powerful, victory is achieved at night. 22

->**Thus ends the Army Diagram.**<-

The Lord of Yoga, the mind, the body, and the physical limbs are known from friends.

**The Praise of the Science of Breath**

It is the secret of secrets, 
The essence, the light made manifest. 
This Science of the Rising Breath <term>Swarodaya: the practice of observing the flow of breath through the nostrils to predict outcomes</term> 
is situated within the highest of all knowledge. || 1 || 
It is the subtlest of the subtle, 
A jewel, easy to understand, and providing true proof. 
It is a wonder in a world of unbelievers, 
Yet it is the very foundation for those who do not believe. || 2 || 
To the wicked, the evil, 
the petty, the liar, and the violator of the teacher’s bed <note>original: "guru-talpa-ga," one who commits the grave sin of adultery with the teacher's wife</note>, 
To those lacking truth and of bad conduct, 
This knowledge of breath must never be given. || 3 || 
To the peaceful, the pure, the well-behaved, 
and to those whose minds are solely devoted to the teacher— 
To them, this entire Science of the Rising Breath 
should be given. || 4 || 

->**Thus ends the Praise of the Breath in the *Yamala*.**<-

**The Internal Anatomy of Breath**

Now I shall speak of the final truth: 
The rising of the breath within the body. 
Through the form of the **Movement of the Swan** <term>Hamsachara: the "Ham" and "Sa" sounds associated with exhalation and inhalation, often symbolized as a swan</term>, 
Knowledge of the three times <note>past, present, and future</note> is born. || 1 || 
The Great Power, Kundalini, 
takes the form of the "Hitva" <note>unclear: possibly referring to a specific phonetic seed or state</note>. 
From there, ten channels <term>Nadis: energy channels in the subtle body</term> go upwards, 
and ten channels go downwards. || 2 ||

---

**Commentary**

Regarding the verses "Yoga..." (33) and (34): This concludes the **Diagram of Daily Outcomes**. Having finished the determination of the breath, the author now demonstrates the "Yoga-Knowledge" for all others. 

Regarding "Secret of secrets..." (1): And "Subtle..." (2): To the atheist or liar, this knowledge is a cause of wonder or astonishment. (Verses 2, 3, 4). Thus ends the **Praise of the Breath**. 

Now, following the explanation of the five breaths, I shall provide the explanation of the verses concerning the "Movement of the Swan"—the nature of the left and right breath-flows within the body as spoken by the ancient sages—in their proper place. 

Regarding "Now, the final...": The word "Now" <note>original: "atha"</note> signifies the sequence of knowledge. (1) Now, after the explanation of the five breaths, I shall speak of the rising of the breath within the body through the "Movement of the Swan," specifically the rising of the right and left breath. 

Regarding "Kundalini...": For example, I shall explain how the right breath or the left breath always rises. Among the powers <note>shaktis</note>, the **Great Power** <note>Mahashakti</note> is located in the navel region of embodied beings. Her name is **Kundalini**. What is her form? She has the form of "Hitva" <note>original: "hitv-rupinī"</note>. She is located at the heart-center <note>hrid-sthale</note> within the wheel of the body. She is the root of the channels located in the body. 

And so, "From there...": From that Kundalini, ten channels have gone upwards and ten channels have gone downwards. (2) Two channels go horizontally. From that same Kundalini, there are twenty-four channels born from the primary path of the Kundalini-power. (3) Regarding "From those...": From those twenty-four channels, three hundred and seven <note>307</note> sub-channels are born.

These <note>referring to the channels</note> are the great nourishers of the body because they act as the conduits for the essence <term>rasa: the vital fluid or "juice" derived from digested food</term> of the food and water that has been consumed. Wherever these channels extend and conclude, they carry that essence of food; through these paths, the essences nourish the physical body. || 4 || **Ten are primary** means that among all those channels, ten specific channels are considered the most important. Within those ten, the ten types of "winds" flow. From these, the ten winds emerge from the body. I shall now state the names of those ten channels. || 5 ||

**Now, the Names of the Channels**

The names "Ida" and "Pingala" are easily understood. || 6 ||

**Now, the Names of the Winds**

"Prana," "Apana," and so on. These are the ten winds. The channel named **Ida** starts from the heart and faces toward the right nostril <note>original: "dakshina nasaputam." Traditionally in many yoga texts, Ida is associated with the left, but this manuscript specifies the right.</note>; within it, the wind called **Prana** flows. The channel named **Pingala** starts from the heart and faces toward the left nostril; there, the **Udana** wind resides.

> Proceeding in pairs, the channels are twenty-four in number. 
> These exist in the physical path of the Great Power known as Kundalini <term>Kundalini: the primordial energy coiled at the base of the spine</term>. || 3 || 
> From those, thousands of channels arise which nourish the body. 
> Seven hundred arise, plus seven in number <note>original: "sapta-shatani... saptottara." This describes 707 channels, though other traditions often cite 72,000</note>. || 4 || 
> However, ten channels are primary, acting as the conduits for the ten winds. 
> I shall now speak of those channels and those winds. || 5 || 
> 1. Ida, 2. Pingala, 3. Sushumna, 4. Gandhari, 5. Hastijihvika, || 
> 6. Pusha, 7. Yashasvini, 8. <unclear>Chamusha</unclear>, 9. <unclear>Chakuruh</unclear>, 
> and 10. Shankhinika. || 6 ||

> perceived within the middle of the body. 
> These are understood by the wise through the three channels: 
> Ida, Pingala, and Sushumna. || 8 || 
> The Moon is situated in the Ida channel, and the Sun in the Pingala.

The **Gandhari** channel goes toward the left and downward; the **Apana** wind is there. In the channel called **Jihvika**, which is in the region of the navel, the **Samana** wind resides. Within the **Sushumna**, the **Naga** and the other four sub-winds <note>Naga, Kurma, Krikara, Devadatta, and Dhananjaya</note> are well-situated. One meaning has been stated, and another meaning is also taught: the ten winds emerge from the paths of the two nostrils, the two ears, the mouth, the navel, the anus, and the genitals <note>original: "payu-vasta-randhra," referring to the lower excretory and reproductive openings</note>. On this matter, the lineage of sages like Sanaka is the authority. These meanings are easy to understand because they have no further hidden purpose. || 7 || 

Now, among the ten channels, three are most famous. Through these three, the winds are perceived. Regarding that, the wind is like a bird <note>original: "shakune vayu," likely a metaphor for the flight or movement of breath</note>; this is easy to understand. || 8 || The meaning of the **Ida** channel shall be explained further ahead. || 9 || 

**Regarding the Form of Power** 

Here, the wind that moves in the left nostril is the **Moon**. The wind in the right nostril is the **Sun**. The one in the form of the Sun is the Great Lord Shambhu <note>Shiva</note>. The one in the form of Power <term>Shakti: the feminine creative energy</term> is the Moon. These two are called Shiva and Shakti. Shambhu is himself the **Hamsa Mantra** <note>original: "hamsa mantra," a natural mantra formed by the sound of inhalation ('ham') and exhalation ('sa')</note>. || 20 ||

Whether the breath flows through the left or the right nostril, if it flows in the center, that is the element called Earth. That which flows below the nostril is the "elder" element, **Water**. **Fire** flows upward. The wind that touches the sides of the nostrils as it flows is called **Air**. The diagonal flow of air is also associated with this. These are: Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Ether. || 22 || Earth flows in the middle, Water flows downward, and Fire flows upward. Air moves tangentially, and Ether flows during the transition. || 23 || For each element, there is a rise of one *ghatika*. ->### || The Auspicious Rise of Shiva’s Sequence || 25 || ###<-

The time must be explained here. He describes the great positions of the left and right breath. The left breath is known as the Moon. When the left channel flows, starting from the Earth element, it is associated with the planets Mercury, Venus, and Jupiter. The Sun, Mars, Saturn, and the "Snake" <note>Rahu</note> are associated with the right channel and move through all the channels. While the left breath flows, as long as the Earth element flows, the planet Mercury is rising. When it flows in the Water element, the Moon is rising. When it flows in the Fire element, Venus is rising. When it flows in the Air element, Jupiter is rising. Similarly, when the right breath flows, the Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Rahu rise in the sequence starting from the Earth element. One should predict excellent results based on these risings. For example, the results are like those of a "rising sign" <term>lagna: the ascendant in astrology</term>—as the result of Mercury is, so is its rise. In this way, there are auspicious and inauspicious results for all planets. || 13 ||

Now he states the duration of Earth and the other elements. "For Earth, each element..." this is easily understood. || 14 ||

Now he states the time for the flow of the breath in the right and left. "At the start, the Moon in the bright fortnight..." means for three days each, starting from the first day of the two fortnights. Therefore, over the fifteen lunar days, there are specific risings of the left and right. Thus, for three days from the first day, the Moon (left) flows; from the fourth day, the Sun (right) flows; from the seventh day, the Moon; from the tenth day, the Sun; then for three days, the Moon rises first. This is fixed for the Sun and Shiva (Moon). On the first day, the Moon's flow is to be led by the method of Shiva. From the measurement of five *ghatikas* each, this occurs during the day. First the Moon, then the Sun in the bright fortnight, then the Moon. In this sequence of the two breaths, these twelve transitions are easily understood. || 25 ||

### Now he describes the results of the reverse rising of the breath.

Now he describes the results of the reverse rising of the breath. When the Sun breath <note>original: "Suryodaya," referring to the breath flowing through the right nostril</note> rises during the time of the Moon, or the Moon breath <note>original: "Chandrodaya," the breath flowing through the left nostril</note> rises at the time of the Sun; specifically, if during the bright fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha: the waxing phase of the moon</term>, starting from the first day, the Sun breath is active at sunrise, there will be loss, inauspiciousness, or some kind of agitation without cause during that day. Likewise, in the dark fortnight <term>Krishna Paksha: the waning phase of the moon</term>, if the Moon breath rises in the morning, there will also be loss and agitation. || 16 ||

Now he speaks of the power of attraction <note>original: "vashikarana," often meaning "mastery" or "influence" in this context</note> over the left and right breaths. One should make the Moon breath <note>original: "Shashanka," the "hare-marked" moon</note> flow at night; the Right breath is the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note>. Therefore, the Sun is the lord of the day and the Moon is the lord of the night. Regarding the instruction to "make the Moon flow," this refers to the "Wind-grasping" <note>likely referring to a specific breathing technique</note> where, for three days at a time starting from the first of the month, the Moon breath is dominant first in the bright fortnight, and the Sun breath is dominant in the other <insert>dark</insert> fortnight. This is known by observing the time from sunrise onwards at the start of the lunar day <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Vedic calendar</term>. Thus, it is a characteristic of the lunar day. Therefore, at the start of the bright fortnight, the Moon breath rises first, and the Sun breath follows. Here, regarding the instruction to "make the Moon flow at night," one should practice until it becomes natural and balanced, or by following the instructions of a teacher.

-> When the Sun breath flows during the Moon's time, or the Moon flows at the Sun's time, <-
-> loss and agitation will certainly arise on that day. || 16 || One should <-
-> make the Moon flow at night and the Sun flow during the day. <-
-> The one who is constantly devoted to this practice is a Yogi, <-
-> without a doubt. || 17 || In travel, giving, marriage, and for mobile gain, <-
-> in auspicious work and in making peace, the Moon or Left breath is auspicious. || 18 || <-
-> In conflict, in wars between sons, in bathing, eating, and sexual union; <-
-> in dealings, in fear, and in destruction, the Sun channel is praised. || 19 || <-
-> In fire rituals, in ceremonies for peace, and for divine medicines or elixirs; <-
-> in beginning education and in stable works, the Moon breath should be used. || 20 || <-
-> The Moon is victorious in distant warfare; the Sun in close combat. <-
-> One should act according to whichever channel is flowing; it will surely grant success. || 21 || <-

By practicing, one ensures that at night, the Left breath <note>original: "Vama Svara"</note> flows just as it should through careful effort. During the day, the Sun or Right breath should remain flowing. Through such practice, one becomes a Yogi. || 17 || Now he describes the specific importance of the tasks for the left and right breaths. Regarding "In travel, giving, etc.": the intention is that one should perform these while the Left breath is flowing. || 18 || Regarding "In conflict, etc.": "In fear" means that if fear arises and one asks <note>original: "prashrau," perhaps a consultation or a moment of inquiry</note> while the Right breath is flowing, then fear will manifest; the same applies to "destruction" <note>original: "bhange," also meaning defeat or breaking</note>. || 19 || The section on fire rituals and transitions <term>Sankranti: the passage of the sun from one sign to another</term> is easy to understand. || 20 || Now he says something else. Regarding "In distant warfare": in a war at a distance or a war lasting a long time, the Moon is the victor. What does this mean? The Left breath is victorious. "Distant warfare" is an illustration; for success in matters of distance, one should travel using the strength of the Left breath. In a "close" or nearby battle, one should travel and act for victory using the flow of the [Sun] breath. However, as it is said, "whichever channel flows"—whichever breath is active, whether left or right—one should proceed with that. If the left flows, let the left side lead; if the right flows, let the right side lead. || 22 ||

Regarding the movement of the feet, he states a specific rule: **When the Sun breath flows...** <note>original: "Surye vahati," referring to the breath flowing through the right nostril</note> this is easily understood. || 22 || Now, another point. **Regarding Earth and Water...** Here, in response to specific inquiries about whether a task should be undertaken: if the Earth element <term>Prithvi-tattva: the solid, stabilizing state of energy</term> or the Water element <term>Jala-tattva: the fluid, cohesive state of energy</term> is flowing during the time of the left breath, he declares success for the task. If the Fire element <term>Tejas-tattva: the radiant, transformative state</term> is active, the result is mixed. If the Ether <term>Akasha: the space element</term> or Air <term>Vayu: the gaseous element</term> elements are flowing, there is loss—loss of the work, or even death and great suffering. ### . . Na. Ja. . . Ja. Ti. . . 23 . .

When the Moon <note>original: "Chandra," referring to the breath in the left nostril, the Ida Nadi</note> resides in the Northeast and the Sun <note>original: "Bhano," referring to the breath in the right nostril, the Pingala Nadi</note> resides in the West and South, the one situated there becomes the victor in battle; this applies to the defender <term>Sthayi: the one who stays or defends</term> and the attacker <term>Yayi: the one who moves or invades</term> respectively. || 28 || When the breath arises in the left channel <note>original: "Vama-nadya," the Ida Nadi</note>, the Moon should be kept facing forward. When the Sun-breath flows, the Sun should be behind or to the right to ensure victory. || 30 ||

Whether in sleep, during a specific occasion, or while embracing a young woman, he who "drinks the Moon with the Sun" <note>original: "Suryena pivec-candram," a yogic practice of transitioning or balancing the flow from one nostril to the other</note> becomes like the God of Love <note>original: "Makaradhwaja," a name for Kamadeva, symbolizing supreme virility and beauty</note>. || 31 || 

Life comes and life goes; life is indeed life. Life follows life; the child is the maker of life. <note>This verse plays on the word "Jiva," which means both the individual soul and the vital breath</note> || 32 || At night, during the watch when women are asleep, if one "drinks" through the life-breath while sleeping, that man becomes full of gain. || 33 || When the wind flows through the Sun-channel <note>original: "Arke," the right nostril</note> at the time of sleep or during love-making, and the Moon is given into that element, it creates an intense delusion of desire <note>original: "Mohamaya nikamini"</note>. || 34 || 

The flow of the Moon destroys poison; the Sun-flow brings others under one’s control. Liberation is found primarily in the mouth; in what way does the God reside in these three states? || 35 || Immediately after eating, when digestion is slow <note>original: "manda-agnau"</note>, or in the business of gaining wealth, sleeping with the Sun-breath flowing <note>original: "Surya-vahena"</note> should be practiced to remove all defects. || 36 || For the sake of the wisdom of the "Shree-Netra," <note>likely a reference to the Netra Tantra or a specific yogic vision</note> or for the awakening of the noble-minded, one should allow the Moon-breath to flow. || 37 || When a messenger arrives from the upper-left...

<column-break/>

**|| Now, another explanation ||** Regarding "Sleeping, etc." [Verse 31]: He who "drinks the Moon with the Sun" means one should cause the Moon to flow at night and the Sun during the day, for one who desires success. 29 <note>The commentator notes this refers back to the general rule</note>. Here, the Moon-rise occurs at night, and its face is toward the right. What does this mean? One should cause the right breath to flow both day and night. This is our meaning: during the day, the rise of the right breath occurs. By that, one should perform the "drinking" of the left breath with the right. One becomes like the God of Love <note>original: "Makaradhwaja"</note>, meaning one becomes beautiful. 

The word "or" <note>original: "va"</note> connects both contexts. In the context of sleep or embracing a woman, if one is infatuated, then one should "drink" that left breath with the right breath. This means it should occur in the breath even at night. || 31 || 

Regarding "Life" [Verse 32]: By the movement of life, "Jiva" is seen in the scriptures. What is the meaning? If the right breath is flowing, or if one is ready to be infatuated, then one should drink the left with the right. When the left arises, one should make the right steady. Or when the left is flowing, one should drink the right. This is the explanation of "Life following life." Thus, the right follows the steady one, or the left follows the right. Because of that time-element, the child <note>the new breath</note> is the maker of life. The "mouth-infatuation" is the maker of the place of death. This means he conquers. The wind increases; by holding the wind, the wind increases. This is the meaning they clarify. || 32 || 

Regarding "At night" [Verse 33]: At night, one should abandon sleep and practice the yoga of strength. By practicing the breath then, one triumphs. At that time—at the time of the mouth—the person who sleeps becomes a "Jiva-ha" <note>a destroyer of life-force</note>. And so it is said in the Yoga Shastras, when Shiva is embraced, the light shines in three ways... the Moon is given into the element of "Mohamaya" <note>delusion/desire</note>. || 33 || 34 || 

"The Moon-flow..." [Verse 35] is easy to understand. || 35 || 

Now he describes when the right breath should be used. "Immediately after eating..." [Verse 36]. || 36 || "Shree-Netra..." [Verse 37] refers back to the instructions after eating.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions from the previous page regarding the Svarodaya system. It shifts from combat and virility to the practical application of breath-reading for divination (Prashna), specifically how the position of the questioner and the prevailing element (tattva) determine the timing and success of an endeavor.</meta>

In those instances where the right breath <note>original: "dakshina svare," referring to the solar breath in the right nostril</note> flows, those specific actions [should be performed]. Thus, for one who is confused and so on... <note>The transcription here "mlichandra" is likely a corruption of a technical term regarding the flow of breath or a specific type of person/action</note> is explained. **|| 37 ||** 

Now, the necessity of the purpose of the left and right breaths is stated: "It moves upward and to the left." Now, the reason for the astrologer <term>Jyotirvid: one who knows the lights/stars, a diviner</term> to understand the two breaths is given. He should investigate whether the breath follows the desired path or not. Regarding the settled meaning there: in the "upward position," wherever the place of the "upward seat" is relative to oneself, the one desiring a result should be placed. If situated on the left, and having moved to the left—as stated "in front"—the knower [of the breath] should understand it as being in the left portion. 

Similarly, in that same manner, whether situated in the upper position, the right position, or the lower position, because the self has proceeded toward the lower state from the self, the wise ones [understand] the higher and higher positions. Thus, when the right [breath] is seen and the person has gone to the right, it is accomplished. Even at the time of the question, if the breath has gone to the right, whether the person is above and to the left, or below and to the left, [it is considered] right. <note>This refers to the "Full" (Purna) side—the side of the active nostril.</note> **|| 39 ||** 

The meaning of this is favorable only through the answers to the questions. When the Sun <note>the solar breath</note> is active, [the result is like] the orb of the sun; when the Moon <note>the lunar breath</note> is active, it is the feeling of the Moon and Varuna <note>the god of oceans/water</note>. Then there is gain, otherwise not. Whatever "good or bad" one asks while situated in the full channel <term>Purna Nadi: the nostril through which the breath is currently flowing</term>... Now...

---

-> Standing behind. Whether behind, to the right, or below, it is moved by the flow <-
-> thought to be **|| 38 ||** <-
-> Standing in the full channel, whatever one asks regarding <-
-> good or bad results, all that will reach success; <-
-> by experience, there is no doubt. **|| 35 ||** <-

---

When the Sun <note>the right breath</note> is flowing, and the person has gone to the right hand, then among the odd [numbers/factors]... <note>The OCR "mili sit" and "vismanyam" suggests a discussion on odd/even outcomes based on the breath side</note>. When the five elements are present, it is then best. One should speak of it as "full" and so on. Similarly, when the Moon <note>the left breath</note> is flowing, and the person is on the left, then from the sixth [factor] onwards, when the question is asked, one should say "it will happen." Otherwise, it belongs to the "dead" <note>empty or inactive</note> channel. 

The meaning of this is that even an intended result—such as a village, battle, or desired object—of a questioner who comes to the full channel <note>the active side</note> is heard [to be successful]. Here, in the phrase "upward and to the left," if the questioner comes to the "void" <note>the inactive nostril side</note>, then whether the result is good or bad, the desired outcome does not happen; it does not reach success. In this matter, no doubt should be made. **|| 35 ||** <note>The manuscript repeats verse numbers or uses them out of sequence.</note>

Now, the knowledge of gain or loss and good or bad results is stated: "By the three elements beginning with Earth." Now, regarding the question: at the time of the question, regarding gain, loss, victory, or defeat for one who has come to the full [channel] of the left or right: if the Earth element <term>Prithvi Tattva</term> is present at the time of the question, or if there is Water <term>Ap</term> or Fire <term>Tejas</term>, then one should declare the time for the good or bad or the sorrow. 

What is that time? It is said: The result [comes] in days, months, and so on. If the Earth element flows, the result will be within a day. If the Water element flows, the result will be within a month. In the Fire element, the result is by another [measure]. However, whether the element is good or bad—victory, defeat, gain, or loss—it is all known. For example, a question from a Brahmin: "When shall I obtain a livelihood from the king?" If at that time his [breath] is full and the Earth element is flowing in the right, he will obtain it within a day. So also for a living being...

<summary>This page explains how to predict the success of a question based on whether the questioner stands on the side of the "full" (active) or "void" (inactive) nostril. It also introduces timing for results based on the prevailing element: Earth yields results in a day, and Water in a month.</summary>
<keywords>Purna Nadi, divination, Prithvi Tattva, Ap Tattva, Tejas Tattva, solar breath, lunar breath, success prediction, timing of results</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions from the previous page regarding the Svarodaya system. It shifts from the spatial positioning of the questioner to the specific elemental nature (tattva) of the breath and how it dictates the timing and success of outcomes, particularly regarding enemies and general inquiries.</meta>

<margin>
|| Na. Ga. Ja. ||
|| Ja. Ga. Shri ||
24
24
</margin>

One should declare "yes"; <note>original: "namiti," a positive affirmation</note> this indeed indicates an auspicious meaning. Likewise, a "bad" or difficult result is indicated by the elements of Ether, Air, and Fire. <note>The five elements—Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Ether—are central to this system.</note> If the questioner is situated on the "Empty" <term>Shunya: the side of the body where the nostril is not currently breathing</term> side, it is always bad. If the breath flows only on the right side and it is one of those [elements] like Air or Fire, then one should predict a bad result. Life itself should not even be asked about in such a case, as it is considered "spoiled." 

Now, if the breath flows in the "Full" <term>Purna: the side where the breath is actively flowing</term> state, and the questioner is on that same side, yet it flows with Ether, Air, or Fire, a bad result should still be spoken. Why? Because of the contradiction [of the subtle elements]. This is the well-known opinion of the poet-sages: when the breath flows in the **Ether element**, the result manifests within a single day.

->The results for Earth and the other three elements do not come in a day or month. <-
->Both the auspicious and the bad are determined by Ether, Air, and Fire. || 40 ||<-

In the **Air element**, the result comes in a month; in the **Fire element**, it takes a year. Suppose someone meets the practitioner and asks: "When will my enemy be defeated?" <note>original: "mrito," literally "dead" or "destroyed"</note> If the questioner is on the "Full" side and the **Earth element** is active, the enemy will be defeated in one day. If in the **Water element**, then in a month; if in the **Fire element**, in a year. This is considered a good outcome for the questioner. If the questioner is on the "Empty" side, the death of the enemy will still occur [eventually], which is also considered good. 

Furthermore, regarding contradictions: the "Full" breath gives a full result, and the "Empty" breath gives an empty (null) result. The three elements starting with Earth <note>Earth, Water, and Fire in some contexts; here Earth and Water are the primary "good" ones</note> give auspicious results. Ether, Air, and Fire give inauspicious results. If the breath is "Full" but consists of Ether, Air, or Fire, then the result is "mixed"—somewhere between auspicious and inauspicious, such as a gain that leads to a later loss. 

This is the result for a messenger <note>original: "duta," the person bringing the question</note> who is on the "Full" side. If the question is auspicious and Earth or Water is active, the result is fully auspicious. If the question is auspicious but the breath is "Full" with Ether or Air, the result is only middling. Similarly, for a questioner on the "Empty" side, one should calculate the result as middling even with the five elements. 

Now, one might ask: "Since Fire is among the five elements, and it can be both auspicious and inauspicious, how do we decide?" In response to this inquiry, it is explained: Although this is the nature of the **Fire element**, if the breath is in the first half of its duration, one should predict an auspicious result; in the second half of its duration, one should predict a bad result. This distinction is mastered through the practice of breath-awareness. || 40 ||

->At the moment the breath is inhaled, that which is desired or spoken is grasped.<-
->All of that reaches success; however, during exhalation, there is nothing beautiful.<- 

Alternatively, one may answer other unspoken questions based on the power of the right breath alone. Regarding the verse "At the moment the breath is inhaled": during a question, the practitioner of breath-science should observe the beginning and end of the flow. At the moment the breath enters—whether through the left or right nostril—whatever the questioner desires will certainly come to pass. This is the meaning. "During exhalation" <note>original: "nirgame," the breath leaving the body</note> means if the question is asked while the breath is exiting the right nostril, the desired outcome is not "beautiful"—meaning your task will not be accomplished. Even here, the presence or absence of enjoyment should be predicted according to the specific element <term>Tattva</term> such as Earth and the rest. || 41 ||

<summary>This page details how the five elements (tattvas) and the timing of inhalation/exhalation determine the success of a question. Earth and Water elements bring quick success, while Ether, Air, and Fire involve delays or negative outcomes, and questions asked during inhalation are likely to succeed.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions of the Svarodaya (Science of Breath), specifically focusing on the transition between the "Full" (active) and "Empty" (inactive) nostrils.
