<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on "Argha-nirnaya" (the determination of prices). It moves from the general principles of market fluctuations to the "Mahaphala" (Great Results), specifically focusing on how to identify which planet acts as the primary "Lord" of a country at a given time to predict its prosperity or hardship.</meta>

# Now, the Great Results are described, beginning with "Venus" and so on, and the Lords of the countries such as Saturn, Jupiter, etc. || 80 ||

The lords of the various countries have been mentioned separately. For some countries, three lords are named; for others, two; and for some, four. A doubt may arise: does the lordship happen collectively, or is it determined by the passage of time or the relative strength of the planets? To address this, the following deliberation is given: "When a great planet is in retrograde or rising..."

In the **Year** <note>original: "varshe"</note>, the lords are Rahu, Ketu, and the Sun. **Jupiter** <note>original: "Jiva"</note> is the lord of the **Month** <note>original: "masa"</note>. Mars, the Sun, Mercury, and Venus are the lords of the days, and the Moon is also a lord of the day. || 77 ||

The lords of **Metals** <note>original: "dhatu"</note> are Saturn and Mars; Jupiter and Mercury follow them. The lords of **Roots and Vegetation** <note>original: "mula"</note> are Ketu and others. These are the planetary lords of the different divisions. || 78 ||

Saturn <note>original: "Pangu," literally "the lame one"</note>, Rahu, Ketu, the Sun, Jupiter, and Mars are considered male. The two **Female Planets** <note>original: "stri-grahau"</note> are Venus and the Moon. Saturn and Mercury are considered **Neuter** <note>term: Napumsaka; planets that are neither masculine nor feminine in astrological classification</note>. || 79 ||

Venus and the Moon are **White** <note>original: "sita-varna"</note> in color. Saturn is **Black** <note>original: "krishna"</note>. Mars and the Sun are **Red**. Mercury and Jupiter are **Gold**. Rahu and Ketu are said to be **Smoky** <note>original: "dhuma"</note> in color. || 80 ||

When a great planet is in retrograde, in its own portion, or rising, its influence is seen in the world. Between the various lords of a country, who is the actual master? It is determined by the planet's own state and position. || 81 ||

A planet that is **Retrograde** <note>term: Vakri; appearing to move backward in the sky, which increases its astrological power</note>, in its **Exaltation** <note>term: Ucha; the sign where a planet is at its most powerful</note>, or in its own house/aspect possesses **Full Vitality** <note>original: "purna-virya"</note>. These signs include Aries, Taurus, Virgo, Cancer, Pisces, and Libra. || 82 ||

The degrees of exaltation for the Sun and other planets are found where they are in the seventh sign from their **Debilitation** <note>term: Nicha; the sign where a planet's influence is weakest</note>. These specific degrees are ten, twenty, and fifteen <note>The text describes the mathematical points for "Parama-ucha" or highest exaltation</note>. || 83 || Twenty-seven, twenty, and seven... <note>The text continues listing technical degrees for planetary strength</note>. || 84 || Between the points of exaltation and debilitation, there are intermediate positions. The strength should be calculated using the **Rule of Three** <note>term: Trairashika; a mathematical proportion used to find an unknown value based on three known values</note>. || 85 ||

A planet in its highest exaltation has **Full Strength**. If it is in its sign of debilitation, it has **Half Strength**. If it is in its own house, it has full strength, and in a friend's house, it has three-quarters strength. || 86 ||

Regarding the lords like Saturn and Jupiter: the one who is currently in retrograde or has just "risen" <note>term: Udita; becoming visible after being obscured by the Sun's rays</note> is considered the "Lord" for that time. If among the three potential lords of a country, one is stronger due to being in its exaltation or its own house, then that planet is designated as the ruler for that specific period. This is how the determination of the Lord is made. || 81 ||

Now, he considers strength again: "The one in retrograde or exaltation in its own house..." A planet in its own house has full vitality. A retrograde planet also has full vitality. If it is both retrograde and in its exaltation, its strength is even greater. One should calculate the strength based on its distance from the exaltation point using the Rule of Three. || 82-85 ||

Furthermore, concerning the consideration of strength: "In exaltation, its strength is full." In the sign of debilitation, the strength is halved. For a detailed explanation, one should look to the methods of **Horoscopy** <note>original: "Jataka-ukta"</note> and the **Standard Manuals** <note>original: "Jataka-paddhati"</note>. Strength is considered through six factors: **Position, Direction, Time, Nature, Motion, and Aspect** <note>term: Shadbala; the six-fold system of determining planetary strength</note>. Whichever of the three potential lords is strongest according to these six factors, or by its presence in its own house or divisional chart, is considered the Master. If two are equally strong, both are lords. The one with even a slight excess of strength is the victor. One should determine the influence on the country based on the Lord’s strength...

->...and then speak of the time. || 86 ||<-

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of planetary "Lords" from page 33. While the previous section categorized planets by their gender, color, and dominion over months or materials, this page focuses on the "Vedha" (astrological influence or "piercing") they exert on specific countries to determine their fortune.</meta>

# || 67 || Now, the Deliberation on Results || "Thus, the Lords of the Countries," etc. || "Thus" refers to...

By considering the strength of **Retrograde** <note>original: "vakra"</note> motion and other planetary states, those who are currently the Lords of countries and regions should be positioned within the **House-Vedha** <term>Vedha: literally "piercing"; in Indian astrology, it refers to the lines of influence or "aspects" planets cast across a grid or chart toward specific letters or signs</term>. They are positioned to influence through the Left, Front, and Right directions. These Guardian Deities of the countries influence the "Five-fold Set beginning with the Name-letter" <note>original: "svavarnadi-panchakam." This refers to the five markers of a place's identity: its initial letter, its birth star (Nakshatra), its zodiac sign (Rashi), the lunar day (Tithi), and the day of the week (Vara).</note>. They influence the ruler of the country and the zodiac sign associated with those lands. When such an influence (Vedha) occurs, one must exert great effort to determine whether the planet causing the influence is a **Friend** <note>original: "suhrid"</note>, an **Enemy** <note>original: "shatru"</note>, **Neutral** <note>original: "madhyastha"</note>, or **Indifferent** <note>original: "udasina"</note> to the Lord of that country. || 88 || Having reached this point, what should be done? It is said:

If the Lords within the circle of countries are the masters of their own domains, and if the one influencing the country's ruler is indeed the Lord of that country and is auspicious...

->One should know that half-results occur in a neutral planet's house, and a quarter-result when situated in an enemy planet's house. || Based on the power of the zodiac sign,<-
->through the instruments and the strengths of the influences (Vedha). || 87 || Thus, those who are the Lords of countries,<-
->positioned in the House-Vedha. Whether they are friends, enemies, neutral, or friendly-leaning...<-
->from that. || 88 || The states of being a self-friend, neutral, or enemy in the influence (Vedha) of the countries and so on is enjoyed<-
->henceforth. Benefic planets grant auspiciousness in four, three, two, or one quarters (padas). || 89 ||<-
->The state of being a self-friend, neutral, or enemy in the influence of countries and so on, in sequence. A wicked<-
->planet may cause harm in one, two, three, or four quarters. || 90 || Seeing with a full gaze (aspect),<-
->the planet gives its result. Otherwise, it occurs differently,<-
->this is the estimation based on the observation of the Time. || 91 || ||<-

In that regard, a planet grants auspiciousness to the country in **four quarters** <note>meaning a 100% full result</note> if it is in its own house. It grants auspiciousness in **three quarters** if in a friend’s sign. It grants only **one quarter** if in an enemy’s sign. Thus, by considering the strength of **Benefic Planets** <note>original: "shubha-graha"</note> through various divisions (vargas), and by considering the lords of **Metals, Roots, and Living Beings** <note>original: "dhatu-mula-jiva"</note>, as well as friendships, one should calculate the auspiciousness based on the four-fold quarters of the benefic planet’s influence. || 89 ||

Now, the deliberation on **Malefic Influence** <note>original: "krura-vedha"</note> is stated. The consideration of a malefic planet is the same: in its own house, it influences with one quarter; in a friend's house, with two; in a neutral's house, with three quarters; and in an enemy's house, it influences with **four quarters** to produce a wicked result. || 90 || Now, the visible evidence of the result of the influence (Vedha) is proven: "Seeing with a full gaze..." etc. If the planet causing the influence on the country's lord—which produces results in quarters based on friendship—actually "sees" (aspects) that lord, then at that time, the result will certainly come to pass according to the quarter of influence.

For example: if a planet in its own house, capable of producing a result in four quarters through influence, "sees" the target, then it sees all the "Five-fold set" with a **Full Gaze** <note>original: "purna-drisha"</note>. How does the result manifest? A planet in a friend’s house, giving a result of three quarters, also sees with a "three-quarter gaze"; then the result is born of its natural temperament. The result is declared to be exactly three quarters. A planet in a neutral house influences with two quarters and sees with a "two-quarter gaze"; then naturally the result is two quarters. A planet in an enemy’s house influences with one quarter and sees with a "one-quarter gaze"; then the result is specifically explained as such. If a planet in an enemy’s house exerts influence by one quarter and sees with a "full gaze," then that quarter-result is fully realized. There is an equivalence between the strength of the gaze (aspect) and the strength of the influence (Vedha). || 92 || ||

It details a "quarter-system" (padas) where results range from 25% to 100% depending on the planet's placement and its astrological "gaze" or aspect.</summary>

Now he describes the planetary aspects <term>Drishti: the "sight" or gaze a planet casts upon a specific zodiac sign or degree</term> on the five-fold set beginning with the name-letter. In the circle of the zodiac beginning with Aries, or in the twelve-spoke wheel starting with the Nakshatra <term>Nakshatra: a lunar mansion or star cluster</term> Magha, when a planet looks upon a certain sign, whatever letter and other markers are present there—as previously shown—become afflicted or "struck" <note>original: "hantam"</note> by that planet. || 52 ||

Showing the five-fold set starting with the sign and letter, he says: "Vowels, letters, etc." The vowels and letters are described in the **Vowel-Chart** <note>original: "Svara-chakra"</note>. In the chapter on the five moving forces <note>original: "Pancha-chara"</note>, it is said that the vowels and letters mentioned in the Vowel-Chart become afflicted when "pierced" <term>Vedha: literally "piercing"; a technical term for when a planet’s position aligns with and negatively impacts a specific letter, sign, or star</term> by a lunar day <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term>.

Take, for example, the deliberation regarding the country of **Magadha**: the initial letter is 'M' <note>original: "ma-kara"</note>; the vowel associated with the letter is 'i'; the zodiac sign is **Leo** <note>original: "Simha-rashi"</note> because of the star Magha. If one asks how the lunar day—for instance, the twelfth day <note>original: "Dvadashi"</note>—affects this, it is because these five markers are described in the Vowel-Chart as a unified set. This five-fold set is afflicted by the "piercing" of its own lunar day. Now, how does the "fifth aspect" work? He explains that the lunar day, the letter, and the sign are all shown together. For example, the letter 'M' is pierced by the twelfth lunar day.

->**Regarding the vowels, signs, and the five-fold set beginning with the letter in the zodiac starting with Aries...**<-
->**Through the power of planetary aspects, that "piercing" influence also falls upon the letters and other markers.**<-

->**full result; if the influence is weak, the result is likewise small. || 54 ||**<-
->**The full aspect, as determined by the desired degrees of the planet, is always**<-
->**recognized by the wise. If the aspect is lacking, one should not declare**<-
->**the influence as either good or bad. || 55 ||**<-

The letter 'M' is afflicted by the "piercing" of the twelfth lunar day. The vowel of the letter 'M' is 'i'. This is what is meant: the five-fold set consisting of the initial letter, vowel, and so on, becomes afflicted. A separate piercing can occur for each of the five markers. Because of the aspect of a single planet, the vowels and signs are not described as separate from the letter. When a "piercing" planet is seen in the sign of Leo within the twelve-spoke wheel, the entire five-fold set is considered "seen" or impacted. It is not as if the vowels and signs are in different places; when one is aspected, the whole set is affected. || 53 ||

Now, he describes the results of the aspects with subtle and specific <margin>and</margin> particular detail. "Now, the results..." etc. When it is said they are afflicted in three ways, it means that in the twenty-seven-fold piercing, if it falls in three ways, one obtains the full result <margin>55</margin>—the specific result calculated. || 54 || He then describes the characteristics of the eight-fold divisions of the planets <note>original: "Varnashtaka"</note>: "Of the planet..." etc. || 55 ||

"In this way..." By this, all the results indicated by the aspects and piercings are determined. An aspect on one's own vowel or sign is a "full aspect." Now he discusses the calculation of results when an aspect is present: "The five-fold set beginning with the letter..." || 56 ||

A **Benefic** <term>Saumya: literally "gentle"; refers to auspicious planets</term> has a full aspect. The Benefics are said to be **Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and the Full Moon**. The **Malefics** <term>Papa: literally "sinful" or "cruel"; refers to inauspicious planets</term> are the **Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Rahu** <note>Rahu is the North Node of the Moon, associated with eclipses</note>. One should calculate the results of these auspicious and inauspicious planets based on a scale of twenty points <note>original: "vimshokah"</note>. Among those, for the four types of auspicious...

<meta>This page continues the technical mathematical calculations for determining the strength of planetary influences on a location. Following the introduction of the "piercing" (Vedha) concept on the previous page, it now quantifies these influences using a scoring system called Vishopaka to balance auspicious and inauspicious forces.</meta>

<page-num>35</page-num>
<warning>Some show-through/ink bleeding from the reverse side of the folio.</warning>

Based on the aspects <term>Drishti: the "sight" or gaze a planet casts upon a specific zodiac sign or degree</term> of the letters and other markers, the numbers five and four should be set down separately. If four planets look with a full gaze, then five times four equals twenty; thus, the total number becomes twenty. The same calculation applies to the aspects themselves. || 37 || Regarding the "piercing" <term>Vedha: a technical term for when a planet’s position aligns with and negatively impacts a specific letter, sign, or star</term> of the letters and the rest: in the five-fold set beginning with the letters <note>The five markers are: the letter, vowel, zodiac sign, lunar day, and day</note>, the piercing occurs according to where the planets are situated. Planets in their own house <note>original: "svagrihasthah"</note> cast a full gaze; those in a friend's house cast a three-quarter gaze; those in a neutral house cast a half-gaze; and those in an enemy's house cast a one-quarter gaze. The strength of the piercing is determined by these numbers. The aspect is calculated according to its position in the tenth, third, second, and fifth houses (10, 3, 2, 5) and the fourth and eighth houses (4, 8). It also gazes upon the spouse-house <note>original: "kalatram," referring to the 7th house</note> and provides three-quarters of its strength. This is the understanding of aspects. In the determination of piercing, the calculation of the piercing is done by estimating the **Vishopaka** <term>Vishopaka: a traditional unit of measurement in Indian astrology, representing 1/20th of a whole, used here as a numerical point or "weight"</term> score. In a beneficial house, the piercing equals...

-> Thus, every result has been specified according to the differences in aspects. <-
-> If the five-fold set beginning with letters is pierced, it is not spoken of as favorable. || 96 || <-
-> A gentle planet <note>original: "Saumyah," referring to a benefic planet like Jupiter or Venus</note> viewing the five-fold set of letters <-
-> with a half-gaze gives five Vishopaka points; <-
-> with a full gaze, it gives twenty. || 97 || As much piercing as there is <-
-> on the letters and markers, there is an equal amount of aspect. <-
-> Wise men should calculate the Vishopaka points based on that estimation. || 98 || <-
-> In this way, wherever these auspicious and inauspicious Vishopaka points <-
-> occur, one should subtract them from each other to know <-
-> the final balance of good or bad fortune. || 99 || <-
-> One should then write down the calculation of the current twenty-part divisions <-
-> in the proper order. These results for the current time <-
-> should be given for both the auspicious and inauspicious. || 100 || <-

---

The aspect, however much it is, must be calculated separately. The Vishopaka points for the "piercing" of a full aspect from an auspicious planet are forty (40). For the piercing of an aspect that is missing one quarter, the Vishopaka points are thirty (30). For a half-gaze, twenty (20). For a one-quarter gaze, ten (10). In this way, having calculated the points for the auspicious planets separately, one should combine them into a single total. One should also calculate the Vishopaka points for the inauspicious planets separately and combine them into one total. || 98 || Then, what should be done? He answers: "In this way, the Vishopaka..." which is easy to understand. || 99 || "The current parts..." || 100 ||

<summary>This page details a numerical scoring system (Vishopaka) used to weigh the strength of planetary aspects on a location's identifying markers, such as its name-letter and zodiac sign. It instructs the practitioner to subtract inauspicious points from auspicious ones to determine a final astrological forecast.</summary>

<meta>This page concludes the discussion of the Sarvatobhadra Chakra within the "Narapati-jayacharya," a significant 12th-century manual on martial and political astrology. It transitions from the mathematical calculations of planetary "piercing" (Vedha) to the tangible worldly consequences—both disastrous and prosperous—of those calculations.</meta>

Now for the appendix: if the "piercing" <term>Vedha: a technical term for when a planet’s position aligns with and negatively impacts a specific letter, sign, or star</term> and the "aspect" <term>Drishti: the "sight" or gaze a planet casts upon a specific zodiac sign</term> of malefic planets result in a specific score <term>Vishopaka: a traditional 20-point system used to measure the strength of astrological influences</term>, the text describes the outcomes. The phrase "destruction of the country" is easy to understand || 201 || as are "untimely distortions" || 202 || and "foreign armies" or "conflict within the royal family" || 203 || and "earthquakes" || 204 ||. 

Now, if the remaining points of the score are provided by auspicious planets, the text describes those results: "prosperity is born" || 205 || and "one's own circle" <note>original: "svachakram," referring to internal security or the king's own domain</note> || 206 ||.

***

### The Results of Inauspicious Piercing

Destruction of the land, suffering of the people, and great obstacles occur. Where this astrological aspect falls, a famine clearly manifests in that territory. || 201 ||

Untimely abnormalities and a multitude of diseases are produced. There may even be the "eating of the flesh of one's own kind" <note>original: "svajati mamsa bhukti" — a harrowing metaphor for the extreme desperation and social collapse during a severe famine</note>; a hellish famine occurs there. || 202 ||

The arrival of foreign armies and conflict within the kingdom follows. Wherever these various afflictions occur, famine will surely befall that region. || 203 ||

Earthquakes, dust storms, and rains of blood <note>original: "rakta vrishti" — an ancient omen of war or catastrophe, often explained today as rain mixed with red dust or spores</note> are produced. A wise person should predict that all happiness in the land is lost based on these "piercings" of the planets. || 204 ||

### The Results of Auspicious Piercing

Prosperity is born and growth occurs at all times. Abundance, safety, and health arise for the subjects in that place. || 205 ||

Threats from one's own army or foreign armies never arise. All things become friendly when the "piercing" is caused by auspicious planets. || 206 ||

### The Significance of the Chart

Just as a lamp illuminates a house from all sides, so too does the **Sarvatobhadra Chakra** <term>Sarvatobhadra Chakra: literally the "All-Auspicious Wheel," a complex 9x9 grid used to predict the fortunes of kings and nations</term> illuminate all knowledge. || 207 ||

Without an offering, without a fire sacrifice, and without the ritual worship of a maiden, and especially without the guidance of a teacher, O Goddess, one should not practice this "King of Charts." || 208 ||

Without analyzing the aspects in the chart, any efforts are wasted. This results in obstacles, O Goddess, there is no doubt about this. || 209 ||

The birth date, the lunar day, the zodiac sign, and the name must be clearly known. For those whose birth details are unknown, the calculation is made through their common name. || 210 ||

I have explained this in great detail according to the scriptures in the *Layamala* <note>original: "Layamale" — likely referring to the Brahma Yamala or similar tantric sources the author synthesized</note>. This chart is highly certain and should not be given to just anyone. || 211 ||

Thus, in the *Narapati-jayacharya* (also known as the *Svarodaya*) within the *Sarva-Layamala*, the section on the Sarvatobhadra 

->॥ Chart is completed. Prosperity. ॥<-

***

As stated: "Just as a lamp..." [Verses] 207, 208, 209, 210, 211. 

Thus ends the commentary titled **Jayalakshmi** <note>The "Victory-Lakshmi" commentary, a popular explanatory text on this work</note> on the Sarvatobhadra Chart within the *Narapati-jayacharya-svarodaya*. May there be auspiciousness. May prosperity prevail.

<term>Raja Parikshit: A legendary monarch in Indian history and the grandson of the hero Arjuna. According to tradition, he was the last great king before the onset of the Kali Yuga (the current age of spiritual decline). After being cursed to die in seven days, he renounced his throne to listen to the sacred Bhagavata Purana.</term>

### Narrative Illustration: The Assembly of the Sages

The illustration on this page depicts a pivotal moment in the life of King Parikshit. 

In the **upper section**, the King <note>original: "Raja Prikhshat"</note> is shown seated in his royal splendor upon a golden throne, sheltered by a ceremonial canopy. He is attended by servants holding fly-whisks, symbols of his temporal power. He is engaged in deep conversation with a seated sage—most likely **Shukadeva**, the narrator of the *Bhagavata Purana*.

In the **lower section**, the scene shifts to a more somber, scholarly atmosphere. The King is no longer on his throne but sits on the floor among a group of six sages or royal advisors <note>original: "Rishis" or "Mantris"</note>. This transition from the throne to the floor symbolizes the King's humility and his shift from a worldly ruler to a seeker of divine knowledge.

<note>This painting is rendered in the Pahari style, likely from the late 18th century. This style is known for its lyrical lines and vibrant use of color. The inclusion of this scene within a manual on "Martial Astrology" serves as a reminder that while stars may predict the fate of kingdoms, spiritual wisdom is the ultimate refuge for a ruler.</note>

<note>The manuscript shows significant ink bleed-through from the reverse side. Faint grid lines, representing the 100-square chart discussed in the text, are visible beneath the central verse block.</note>

### The Construction of the Hundred-Square Chart

Regarding the **Hundred-Square Chart** <note>original: "Shatapada-chakram"</note>: The quarters <note>original: "padas"</note> of the lunar mansions <note>original: "Nakshatras"</note>, beginning with the star **Ashwini**, total one hundred and twelve (112). I shall now describe the chart of one hundred squares that arises from them, beginning with the letters of a person's name.

Now, the placement of the chart is explained.

**The Horizontal and Vertical Grid**

The wise practitioner should draw the lines moving horizontally and vertically. In the four cardinal directions, starting with the **Northeast** <note>original: "Ishana"</note> corner, one should place the nine letters of the **"A-Va-Ka-Ha-Da"** <note>original: "Avakahada"</note> system into five squares each.

The letters **A, Va, Ka, Ha, Da** are placed in the Northeast.
The letters **Ma, Ta, Pa, Ra, Ta** are placed in the five squares of the **Southeast** <note>original: "Agneya"</note>.
The letters **Na, Ya, Bha, Ja, Kha** are placed in the five squares of the **Southwest** <note>original: "Nairutya"</note>.
The letters **Ga, Sa, Da, Cha, La** are placed in the five squares of the **Northwest** <note>original: "Vayavya"</note>.

Next, what should be done? It is said: **"With the Five Vowels."**

One should combine the five base letters (A, Va, Ka, Ha, Da, etc.) with the five vowels: **A, I, U, E, O**. Each of the five letters should be combined separately with these five vowels. 

For instance, in the group of names starting with the letter **"A"**, when the letter-form is combined with the five vowels, five sounds are produced: **A, I, U, E, O**.

---

-> I shall describe the Hundred-Square Chart for men, based on the letters of their birth-quarters. <-
-> These are to be known by the letters of the lunar mansions, according to one's desires. (1) <-
-> The wise should draw five lines moving horizontally and five lines moving vertically. <-
-> There is no doubt that one hundred squares are thus created. (2) <-
-> Place the "Ka-Ha-Da" groups starting from the East and the intermediate directions in order. <-
-> Here, one must join the five-by-five letters of the seal. (3) <-
-> Through the combination of the five vowels, turn each single letter into five. <-
-> In the appropriate place, the lord should put three letters each. (4) <-
-> The group "Ku-Gha-Da-Cha" belongs in the "Rudra" area, which is the Northeast sector. <-
-> The group "Kha-Cha-Tha" belongs to the hand in the direction known as Southeast. (5) <insert> 00:0 </insert> <-

---

By combining them with the five vowels, the letter **"Va"** becomes fivefold: **Va, Vi, Vu, Ve, Vo**. 

Similarly, combining the letter **"Ka"** with the five vowels produces five versions: **Ka, Ki, Ku, Ke, Ko**. 

For the letter **"Ha"**, the five-vowel combination results in: **Ha, Hi, Hu, He, Ho**. 

And for the letter **"Da"**, the five-vowel combination results in: **Da, Di, Du, De, Do**. 

By doing this, a total of twenty-five (25) variations are produced for the "A-Va-Ka-Ha-Da" group. 

In the same way, for the groups starting with **"Ma-Ta-Pa-Ra-Ta"** located in the Southeast and other directions, twenty-five variations are produced for each. Through the combination of vowels and consonants, the total number of letters becomes one hundred (100).

In the specific positions, such as the position of the letter **"Ku"**, the three letters **Dha, Nga, and Tha** are to be placed. In the position of the letter **"Pa"**, the letters **Va, La, and Tha** are placed. In the square for the letter **"Bha"**, one places **Dha, Ma, and Dha**. In the position of the letter **"U"**, the letters **Tha, Ja, and Tra** are placed. 

By following this method, the letters for the third-quarter chart are produced (4). As the verse says: "Ku-Gha-Da-Cha shall be..." (5). "Dha-Ka-Tha is first..." (6). This pertains to the lunar mansions of **Ardra** and **Hasta**. 

Thus, the fourfold division is achieved. The previous five and the subsequent five... <unclear>...for the month of Ashadha, and those stars associated with Ashadha, the total of one hundred and four...</unclear>

It details how five base letters in each cardinal direction are multiplied by five vowels to populate the grid's squares.</summary>

# Twenty-eight <note>Section 28</note> 39
### Page 39

The twenty-eight vertical lines are equal and slightly raised at the top, such that above those lines, the strength of the 112 quarters <note>original: "charana-balam"</note> of the lunar mansions starting with **Krittika** <note>The Pleiades</note> are written together. || 1 || For that purpose, he says: || "Krittika and the others..." || 2 || "The conjunction..." || 3 || Now he describes the piercing <note>term: "Vedha" (the astrological concept of one celestial body 'piercing' or aspecting a point or letter)</note>: || Now, whichever star it stands in... || 4 || "Whose other name..." || 5 || "Situated in both malefic positions..." || 6 || And regarding the "object of piercing" <note>original: "vedhyam"</note>: when one is pierced by malefic planets on both sides, then the people of Kalinga and other regions may face destruction.

Twenty-eight vertical lines and twenty-eight horizontal lines. || 1 || And the quarters...
This should be understood as trembling <note>unstable or flickering</note>, as mentioned in the first chapter. || 2 || The lunar mansions starting with **Krittika**,
In the order of the letters of their quarters. Including **Abhijit** <note>The intercalary 28th lunar mansion</note>, all human stars should be placed, totaling twenty-eight in number. || 2 || Wherever a planet resides in a quarter of a star, a wise man should place it right there.
The malefic <note>original: "krura"</note> or even the benefic <note>original: "saumya"</note> planet aspects the syllable directly in front of it. || 3 || The first quarter [aspects] the ninth, third, and fourth quarters, and the first. The second [aspects] the third.
The third [aspects] the second. || 4 || Those who know the "Division Chart" <note>term: "Amsha-chakra"</note> understand its other name.
When planets are in this chart, if they are hit by malefics or benefics, there is no doubt about the results. || 5 || When a malefic is situated on both sides of the point being aspected, it kills the subject through twenty types of misfortune. When a benefic is on both sides of the aspected point, there is sickness and pain, but [eventually] relief for the person. || 6 || Regarding the subject of the aspect: if it occurs during a marriage or a journey, one does not return.
In illness, it is the cause of death; there is no doubt that a malefic aspect causes failure. || 7 || Even the kings, the oceans, the rivers, the gods, and the nights—
All are destroyed by a malefic aspect <note>term: "Krura-vedha"</note>; there is no need for further deliberation on this. || 8 || When an aspect occurs at the Moon’s position in a star-quarter, look!
Therefore, on that day, one should avoid all auspicious actions. || 9 ||

->Thus ends the **Division Chart** <note>original: "Amsha-chakra"</note> in the *Swarodaya* of the *Narapati-jayacharya*. || 3 ||<-

---

Regarding the power of the aspect in the Division Chart, he says: "The kings, the oceans..." etc. In whichever chart a malefic aspect occurs, if even kings and such are destroyed, then what need is there to speak of common men? Among the *Sarvatobhadra* <note>A famous "all-auspicious" grid chart</note>, the Hundred-Square, and the Arrow charts, in whichever chart the "Lamp of Strength" is known, the kings and others achieve success. That chart is the one where the Moon travels through the lunar mansions; thus, at the time of an undertaking, it "pierces" <note>aspects</note>. If at that very time an aspect from another planet occurs, then one should avoid auspicious work during that day or that night.

->Thus ends the **Division Chart** in the *Jayalakshmi* commentary, written by Sri Harivansha Mahadeva, on the *Swarodaya*.<-

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological charts used for royal success and divination. It moves from the general construction of charts to the "Chamar-chakra" (Fly-whisk Chart), which classifies the lunar mansions and geographical regions into domains ruled by the Lord of Horses, the Lord of Elephants, and the Lord of Men.</meta>

# The First Chart || 1 ||

Regarding the "First Chart": the first group of nine lunar mansions <note>original: "nakshatras"</note> starting with **Ashvini** is situated in the North. The nine-star group starting with **Magha** is situated in the East. The nine-star group starting with **Mula** is situated in the South. Here is the division of these three groups: in those regions, the origins and movements of power are located. Their names are explained individually through their "power centers." The name of these powers is "Ashvya-balam" <note>the strength of the Ashvini group</note>. Following that are those born from them, and then the givers. In that context, the rising of the centers should be observed and marked.

As stated in the *Shalihotra* <note>An ancient Indian treatise on horses and hippology</note> composed by **Nakula**:
"The glory, fortune, righteousness, desire, and wealth of that King flourish, in whose stable stand <term>horses</term> <note>original: "vajino"</note> endowed with all auspicious marks."

Similarly, there is a saying of **Lakshmi** <note>The Goddess of Wealth and Prosperity</note>:
"In whatever house horses reside for even a single day, I, Lakshmi, will leave the chest of **Vishnu** to dwell in that home for a long time."

## Now, the Fly-whisk Charts

-> First, having drawn the chart, let the three stars be beautiful. <-
-> The nine zones starting with Ashvini total twenty-seven. || 1 || <-
-> The Ashvini-group, the Magha-group, and the Mula-group charts. <-
-> In the North, East, and South, with three stars at the end. || 2 || <-
-> From the western middle line to the northeast, the King of Horses. <-
-> In the southeast, the Lord of Men; in the middle, the <term>Lord of Elephants</term> <note>original: "Gajadhipa"</note>. || 3 || <-

---

<margin>And</margin>
Furthermore, why are elephants of lesser power than horses? Seeking the answer to that, he says: "Warfare is indeed conducted by elephants, who resemble clouds split apart. They are difficult to conquer and hard to restrain..." <note>The text compares the battlefield utility of the three branches of the military</note>. In this system, horses are considered superior to elephants, and elephants superior to the infantry or men. Here, the horses are associated with the lunar mansions starting with **Ashvini**. The elephants are situated in the zone of **Magha**. The men are situated in the zone of **Mula**. This division of the "body" of the army exists in all twin-systems and others. || 2 ||

Regarding the division of the three horse-groups in the four directions, he says: "The western middle line..." In this example, we see the division of the land. As stated in the *Rajavijaya* <note>The "Victory of Kings," a political or astrological text</note>: "The Lord of Horses, the Lord of Elephants, and the Lord of Men appear in that specific order." This should be understood through the rules governing the three groups of stars.

On this matter, the acquisition of the chart is found in the <term>Vijayakalpalata</term> <note>The "Wishing-Vine of Victory," a manual for kings</note>: "The horse is the wave, the man is the direction... the army becomes the chart." One should know that the Eastern Lord is for the King, while for common humans, the Lord of the direction is different. Therefore, the Lord of Horses, the Lord of Elephants, and the Lord of Men are the kings who have set forth. Here, the three groups of lunar mansions are merely technical names.

Regarding the western middle line and the assigned lordship, these are the deities dwelling in those places. Here is the geographical division within the zones of the chart: <term>Ayodhya</term>, <term>Mithila</term>, <term>Koshambi</term>, <term>Koshiki</term>, <term>Ahichhatra</term>, <term>Gaya</term>, <term>Vindhya</term>, <term>Avanti</term>, <term>Chedi</term>, and <term>Mekhala</term>. <term>Kanyakubja</term> is also mentioned, and the Middle Country <note>Madhyadesha</note> is destroyed <note>likely referring to a specific astrological omen of ruin for the central region</note>. In these countries, where the Koshiki is located, the western direction follows. Therefore, in the Horse-section, the Lord of Horses [extends] to the Northeast...

<summary>This page describes the Chamar-chakra, an astrological system that categorizes lunar mansions and Indian cities into three domains: Horses, Elephants, and Men. It emphasizes the royal importance of horses, citing the Shalihotra and the goddess Lakshmi as authorities.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the "Fly-whisk Chart" discussion from the previous page, specifically detailing the "Kurma-vibhaga" or the geographical division of the world. It maps various kingdoms, tribes, and natural features of the Indian subcontinent to the three royal domains: the Lord of Horses (North), the Lord of Men (South/West), and the Lord of Elephants (East).</meta>

Moving from the middle line <term>Madhyarekha</term> <note>The ancient Indian prime meridian passing through Ujjain</note> toward the South and reaching as far as the Southeast are the domains of the **Lords of Men**. In the middle of this region lies the **Koshiki** <gloss>original: "ki"</gloss> River.

The area between the Northeast and the Southeast includes the territory of **Magadha** and other regions, which is the domain of the **King of Men**. Between them is the territory of the **Lord of Elephants**. Now, from the southern middle line toward the North, reaching the Northeast, are the **Seven Rivers** <gloss>original: "Saptasindhu"</gloss>. To the Northwest and North, up to the Northeast, many kings reside. Likewise, in the West are the **Kakonkaras**, the **Mlechhas** <note>a term used for foreign or non-Vedic peoples</note>, the **Haihayas**, and the **Shatakas**. In the Northwest direction are the **Mandavyas**, the **Tukharas**, and the **One-eyed people**.

Then there is the **Kingdom of Women**, the **Shamakas**, the **Long-bearded ones**, and the **Ashramas of the Hermits**. The northern locations are as follows: the **Sindhu** region, the **Maru** deserts, the **Kaikeyas**, **Gandhara**, **Madra**, the **Yaudheyas**, the **Jaihvayanas**, and the **Vasatis**. In the Northeast are the **Kiras**, the **Kashmiris**, the **Ganjas**, the **Kharsanas**, and the **Kagas**. Also included are the **Khasas**, the **Singhas**, and the city of **Kanthagalam**. The presiding deity of all these is the **Lord of the Moon** <note>original: "Somapati"</note>. His domain is the **Territory of the Lord of Horses** <note>original: "Ashvapati"</note>.

Now to the West, the **Huns**, and the Southwest direction, reaching toward the Southeast, are various kings. In that Southwest direction are the **Kambojas**, the **Dravidians**, and those considered **Women-ruled**. There are the **Surashtras**, the **Pavanas**, and those who dwell on **Mount Raivataka**. Now, in the South and other regions, are the capitals of kings such as **Shantakampa**, the **Sriparvata** mountain, the **Malaya** mountains, **Purakoruma**, **Kerala**, and the **Remmakas**. There are also the **Vanavasis** and the **Marushashas**. To the Southeast are **Kachchh**, the **Bhojas**, **Magadha**, **Kalinga**, **Vanga**, **Vidarbha**, and the **Vatsas**. Included also are **Kosala**, the **Vidyas**, and the **Lalajikas**. These countries end at the Southeast. Their presiding deity is the **Lord of Men** <note>original: "Manaspati," likely a variant of "Narapati"</note>. His domain is the **Territory of the King**. To the East and South, where the **Koshiki River** flows, is the direction where the **Uda**, the **Chara**, and the **Sovereign of Gold** reside. These are situated near the banks of the **Lohitya** <note>The Brahmaputra River</note>. There are also the **Udayagiri** (Mountain of the Rising Sun), the **Bhadrasvas**, the **Gopoda**, and the **One-eyed ones**.

There are also the **Talakas**, the **Malakas**, and **Vardhamana**. Their presiding deity is the **Lord of Elephants** <note>original: "Gaja-adhipati"</note>. Regarding this, some say that between these regions, toward the North and Northeast, lies the domain of that specific Overlord. Others say the King of Men resides toward the Southeast. In the middle are the Lords of Elephants. This has been explained clearly here. Wherever a King's name-star <note>the Nakshatra corresponding to the first letter of his name</note> is situated, that person should perform their royal duties in that corresponding territory.

---

-> For other kings, wherever their star is positioned, <-
-> that star provides auspicious or inauspicious results for that king. || 4 || <-

---

For others, in whatever territory the King's star is located, that star becomes the source of his fortune or misfortune within that specific domain.

-> This is the territory. || 4 || <-

It transitions into a specific method of categorizing lunar mansions (nakshatras) into nine functional groups and predicts the mundane effects—such as famine, war, or the fate of the crown prince—based on the transit of the planet Saturn through these groups.</meta>

<page-num>31</page-num>
<header>The Compendium of the Spear and the Pot, Chapter 2</header>
<meta>Manuscript in Devanagari script. Rubrication (red ink) is used to highlight headings, verse beginnings, and dandas. Some characters show cursive features or regional variants typical of the 17th-18th centuries.</meta>

### # Now, the Ninefold Names of the Lunar Mansions

<margin>31</margin> Regarding the Fly-whisk and other groups: among the <term>Lunar Mansions</term> <note>original: "Nakshatra"</note> starting with <term>Ashvini</term> <note>the first of the 28 lunar mansions in Hindu astrology</note>, Ashvini is designated as the **Fly-whisk** <gloss>original: "Chamara"</gloss>. The star **Krittika** <note>the Pleiades</note> is known as **Villa**. **Hasta** is known as... <note>the text here categorizes the stars into nine specific types: Fly-whisk, Pot, Villa, Umbrella-staff, Receptacle, Seat, Wedge, and Rope</note>. These nine classifications apply to the stars in succession. Similarly, these nine categories from the **Fly-whisk** onwards should be understood to apply to the nine groups of signs starting with Aries, as well as to the nine items of the King's regalia, such as the Umbrella.

Regarding those situated in auspicious or inauspicious houses, he describes each group starting with Aries. When a planet like <term>Saturn</term> <note>original: "Sauri," a name for Shani/Saturn</note> stays in three stars like the staff of an umbrella, one should predict the "breaking" or ruin of those stars. He further explains the results of other positions based on the movement of Saturn.

> The Fly-whisk, the Pot, the Villa, the Umbrella-staff, the Receptacle,
> the Seat, the Wedge, and the Rope: these are declared as the nine divisions. (5)
>
> When Saturn stays in three stars, one should point to their ruin.
> I shall now speak of the specific effects of the Fly-whisk and the others. (6)
>
> In the **Fly-whisk**, there is fierce wind and a lack of rain occurs.
> A terrible famine will arise, and there is no doubt the people will suffer. (7)
>
> In the **Pot**, there will be war and a great fear of diseases.
> Fatalities, falls, and all such calamities occur here without a doubt. (8)
>
> In the **Villa**, there is the loss of sons and the earth is troubled.
> The King becomes anxious and the world is filled with fear. (9)
>
> When Saturn is in three stars like an **Umbrella-staff**,
> then there is no doubt a "breaking" or ruin of those stars. (10)
>
> There is destruction of the **Seat** when Saturn is situated there.
> Know that the **Crown Prince** is affected in the **Wedge**, and bondage occurs in the **Rope**. (11)
>
> Released from benefics and in rapid motion, Saturn's cruel fruit is not felt.
> But if retrograde and joined with malefics, Saturn gives his most cruel results. (12)

-> The destruction of the Seat occurs when in its own seventh star <-

What is the meaning? It means one should abandon the earth. In the star designated as the **Wedge** <gloss>original: "Kile"</gloss>, if Saturn is present, the **Crown Prince** <note>original: "Yuvaraja"</note> meets his end. The Crown Prince is praised as the one whose father is the living King. In the ninth star known as the **Rope** <gloss>original: "Rajju"</gloss>, when Saturn is present, there is imprisonment or "bondage," either in one's own circle or abroad. The results mentioned for when Saturn stays in these stars are certain to occur if they coincide with a transition into those stars. This is the instruction to be observed there.

Now, he explains a specific detail regarding Saturn's ability to give good or bad results. When Saturn is in the three stars of the Umbrella-staff, etc., it causes the "breaking" of the stars. In the Fly-whisk, it causes fierce winds, and so on. However, if Saturn is joined with a benefic planet <note>such as Jupiter or Venus</note> or is in **Rapid Motion** <note>original: "Atichara" - moving faster than its average speed through a zodiac sign</note>, the sage **Vashishtha** says that Saturn does not produce his cruel results. In such a case, even in the Fly-whisk star, the fierce wind does not blow, there is no drought, and the people do not suffer. When Saturn is in the Pot, war and death do not occur. But if Saturn is **Retrograde** <note>original: "Vakra"</note>, then the effects of the Fly-whisk, etc., become extremely cruel. Finally, regarding the influence of other "cruel" planets like the Sun, Mars, or Rahu: if Saturn is joined with them, the results are as stated. Even when joined with Mercury, he gives cruel results, because Mercury takes on the nature of the cruel planet he is joined with. (12)

<vocab>Bhalli-Kalasha-Samhita, Nakshatra, Shani, Sauri, Bhanga, Chamara, Kalasha, Rajju, Kilaka, Phalashruti, Navabheda</vocab>

<summary>This page describes a system where the 28 lunar mansions are divided into nine symbolic categories (Fly-whisk, Pot, etc.) to predict mundane events. It specifically details how the transit of Saturn through these categories signals disasters like famine, war, or the death of a crown prince, unless mitigated by fast motion or benefic planets.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of "Chhatrabhanga" (the Breaking of the Umbrella), a term used to describe the overthrow or downfall of a monarch. While the previous page categorized the lunar mansions into functional regalia like the "Fly-whisk" and "Staff," this page details the specific planetary alignments—combinations of Saturn, Rahu, Mars, and the Sun—that trigger political catastrophe for the "Lords of the North, South, and East."</meta>

# Now, he explains the specific results of the positions of conflict within the three fields.

Now, he explains the specific results of the positions of conflict within the three fields. Regarding the verse starting with "Saturn, Rahu, and Mars..." Now, if the Lord of the <unclear>Ku-field</unclear> <note>original: "kugāyā," possibly referring to a specific regional or astrological division</note> is joined with Jupiter and the Moon, they cause the **Breaking of the Umbrella** <term>Chhatrabhanga: a metaphor for the destruction of sovereignty or the death of a king</term>. Without Jupiter and the Moon, if the ruler of another field is destroyed, <unclear>what</unclear> occurs there? Whoever is the Lord of the Circle <note>a regional ruler or governor</note>, even if they are favored by the gods, if they are situated in another lord's field at the time of a specific <term>Lunar Mansion</term> <note>original: Nakshatra</note>, that country is affected. || 13 ||

Regarding the four lunar mansions in the field of the **Lord of Elephants** <note>Gajapati: a traditional title for a king, often associated with the eastern regions of India</note>, he confirms this via Mercury and the Moon. When the four **malefic planets** <term>Krura-graha: literally "cruel planets," referring to Saturn, Mars, the Sun, and Rahu</term> are present, as previously stated by ancient sages to be the destroyers of the umbrella, it is determined according to the sequence written for the eastern direction of the Lord of Elephants' field. || 14 ||

Thus, regarding the four-fold... <note>referring to a group of four planets</note>. From the beginning, the destroyers of the umbrella are listed starting with Ashvini, Magha, and others in the arrangement of the cycle. <unclear>Here...</unclear> The Lord of the North, the Lord of the East, and the Lord of the South are described through the three-fold division of their positions. || 15 || Now, regarding the status of other kings...

-> When Saturn, Rahu, Mars, and the Sun are joined with Jupiter and the Moon, <-
-> they cause the **Breaking of the Umbrella** for the King who is the **Lord of the North**. || 13 || <-
-> A cycle of four malefic planets, joined again with Mercury and the Moon, <-
-> was declared by the ancient sages to cause the prior destruction of the umbrella. || 14 || <-
-> Thus: <-
-> The four malefics joined with Mercury and the Moon cause the <-
-> destruction of the umbrella for the **Lord of the Southern Direction**—so say the wise. || 15 || <-
-> Likewise for other kings, wherever and for whomever this may occur: <-
-> When the aforementioned planetary conjunction is certain, the breaking of the umbrella is determined. || 16 || <-
-> Just as there are planets of evil influence, so there are benefics who provide good. <-
-> A "cruel" Venus or Mercury becomes auspicious when joined with the Moon and other benefics. || 17 || <-
-> Just as Saturn <note>original: "Sauri," a name for Saturn meaning "son of the Sun"</note> has a wicked influence, so **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> has a gentle one. <-
-> Mars and Mercury, Jupiter and Rahu, and the Sun and <-
-> Moon are equal in their results. || 18 || <-

---

Thus for others, the situation is as before. The breaking of the umbrella is determined; otherwise, for any king, or by analogy even for a **crown prince** <note>original: "Rajaputra"</note>, the breaking of the umbrella is determined as previously stated. He explains the cause: "Wherever..." In whichever field a person’s birth constellation or name-constellation <note>the star associated with the first letter of a person's name</note> occurs, if it falls within the three umbrellas, then that becomes "his" umbrella; this is the intention of the author.

What if that constellation is in the field of the **Lord of Elephants**? When Saturn, Rahu, Mars, and the Sun are joined with Jupiter and the Moon, and that constellation is in the cycle, then his umbrella shall also break. Or, if that constellation is the field of the Lord of Elephants and it is joined with Mercury, the Moon, and the four malefics, then his umbrella breaks. Or, if the birth or name constellation is in the field of the **Lord of Horses** <note>original: "Avanikshetra"</note> and that constellation is joined with Venus, Mercury, and the four malefics, then his umbrella also breaks. In this way, the **Royal Yogas** <note>astrological combinations indicating power</note> of those born under the constellation Ashvini and others in that country are affected; even there, the presence of Mercury and the four malefics in the field of influence is rare. One should consider the same for other kings. Next, the three umbrellas are explained to provide knowledge for the King. || 19 ||

Next, he states the capacity for auspicious and inauspicious results: "Just as there are evil influences..." This is easy to understand. || 20 || He states the specific detail: "Just as there is evil fruit..." This is also easy to understand. || 21 ||

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of "Chhatrabhanga" (the Breaking of the Umbrella), a term for the downfall of a ruler. This page details how specific planetary transits through these divisions—particularly the movement of Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu—predict specific fates for a monarch, ranging from military defeat to assassination by poison.</meta>

# The Naming of Regalia and the Movement of Saturn

|| **18** || **20** || Within the kingdoms of the **Lord of Horses**, the **Lord of Elephants**, and the **Lord of Men** <note>original: "ashvapati gajapati narapati," traditional titles for the rulers of the North/West, East, and South of India respectively</note>, the state of affairs is determined by the specific **Lunar Mansion** <term>Nakshatra: one of the 28 sectors of the sky along the moon's path</term>. The name of the omen is derived from the king's fly-whisk <note>original: "chamaradya," referring to the *chamara* or yak-tail fan, a symbol of royalty</note> and other regalia. || **19** || 

Then, he explains what action should be taken. In that direction, if the Lunar Mansion is of a "gentle" nature and Saturn enters the mansion of **Ashvini** <note>the first lunar mansion, associated with the Lord of Horses</note>, then it brings a command of suffering for that king. If that king's birth name is associated with Ashvini, then within that kingdom, whatever "breakage" or ruin occurs is understood by those who know the traditions <note>original: "agamavidah," those learned in the sacred texts or traditional sciences</note>. Here, for example, if Saturn enters the mansion of **Bharani** <note>the second lunar mansion, often associated with restraint or death</note>...

---

Just as malefic planets are harsh and cruel, so too are the **benefic planets** <term>Saumya: literally "gentle" or "lunar" planets, specifically Jupiter, Venus, and a well-placed Mercury</term> auspicious and bringers of good. The Moon becomes cruel when joined with a malefic, and auspicious when joined with a benefic. || **18** ||

When benefic and malefic planets are situated together in a single Lunar Mansion, the **Breaking of the Umbrella** <term>Chhatrabhanga: a metaphor for the destruction of sovereignty, loss of the throne, or the death of the king</term> occurs for him, though not necessarily through the arrival of a foreign army. || **20** || 

For other kings, wherever the planets may be in the three fields <note>the divisions of Horses, Elephants, and Men</note>, the name of the omen is born from the titles of the fly-whisk and other regalia. || **21** || 

The specific result should then be declared for the ruler of that other land. The result regarding the "Breaking of the Umbrella" should not be pronounced based solely on the names of the three types of kings. || **22** || 

If **Rahu** or **Ketu** <term>Lunar Nodes: the shadow planets responsible for eclipses, usually considered highly malefic</term> are situated in the King's birth mansion, the **Breaking of the Umbrella** occurs for him by means of **poisoning**. || **23** ||

When a malefic planet resides in the "Umbrella" position, the king should abandon hunting, daring journeys, and the riding of dangerous elephants or horses, as well as avoid all conflict. || **24** || 

Knowing this, if the king performs the "Rites of Appeasement" <note>original: "graha shantikam," ritual remedies to pacify unfavorable planetary influences</note> as prescribed in the **Yamala** texts, peace shall be restored to the king. || **25** || Thus ends the "Umbrella Charts" in the *Svarodaya* section of the *Narapati-Jayacharya* <note>The "Victory-Conduct of the Lord of Men," a 12th-century manual on martial and political astrology</note>. || **25** ||

---

If a king of a hostile land is known to have his birth in the **Bharani** mansion, one should predict war for him. As it was said: "The result regarding the Umbrella should not be pronounced based solely on the names of the three kings." || **22** || 

Now, he explains the result of the "Umbrella" based on the birth mansion. Regarding the verse starting "In the King's birth mansion...": For whichever king the birth mansion coincides with the "Umbrella-Staff" position <note>a technical calculation in the Chhatra-Chakra</note>, and if Rahu or Ketu is situated there, then one should declare that the **Breaking of the Umbrella** for that king will occur through the administration of poison. By the "Breaking of the Umbrella," death is implied. || **23** || 

Regarding the king's daily conduct, he says: "Hunting, daring..." || **24** || 

"Knowing this, if the king..." || **25** || Thus, in the **Jayalakshmi** commentary <note>The "Victory-Goddess" commentary, a popular explanation of the Narapati-Jayacharya</note>, the section on the Three Umbrellas is completed. || **552** ||

<meta>This page continues the astrological discourse on "Chhatrabhanga" (The Breaking of the Royal Umbrella), moving from the movement of Saturn across the broad regions of India to the specific structural diagram known as the "Three-Throne Diagram" (Simhasana Chakra). It details how to calculate the safety and success of a king based on his coronation date and the alignment of the stars.</meta>

# The Three-Throne Diagram

### Commentary on the Structural Alignment
Now, therefore, I shall fully describe the following: “Now I shall speak...” <note>referring to the opening of Verse 1 below</note>. Regarding the “twenty-seven Lunar Mansions” <note>referring to Verse 2</note>, the northern part of **Ashvini** <term>Ashvini: the first of the 27 Lunar Mansions, associated with the star Beta Arietis</term> is where the three-throne complex is to be understood <note>referring to Verse 3</note>. In other matters <note>referring to Verse 4</note>, through the “piercing of the five pulses” <term>Nadika-pancha-vedha: a technical method of calculating astrological intersections or 'piercings' using five specific units of measurement</term>, one calculates the intersection for each individual throne. 

The name for these five pulses relates to their specific sides. The Throne is constructed as a five-stepped square. On that square, two lines for the steps are placed, though not given at once, for the purpose of calculating the intersection (*vedha*). Thus, the placement of the intersection is as follows: 
*   The lowest step is called the **Base** <note>original: *adhara*</note>.
*   Above that is the **Seat** <note>original: *asana*</note>.
*   Above that is the **Vehicle** <note>original: *vahana*</note>.
*   Above that is the **Lion** <note>original: *simha*</note>.
*   At the very top is the **Lion-Throne** <note>original: *simhasana*</note>.

This is where the King resides and becomes like a lion. Although the intersection is mentioned here, the instruction for its specific arrangement is also stated. The pulse of the Base...

---

### The Root Verses of the Throne Diagram

Now, therefore, I shall fully describe the diagram of the three thrones. Merely by knowing this, a king is made fearless. || 1 ||

Using the twenty-seven **Lunar Mansions** <term>Nakshatras</term>, each being soulful, **Ashvini** is the middle pulse, divided into five pulses. || 2 ||

Starting from **Ashvini**, situated in the North-East; in the southern half of **Mula** <note>the 19th Lunar Mansion</note>, the results are extraordinary. || 3 ||

In other kingdoms, one should speak of the results accordingly. All this—good and bad—depends on where **Saturn** <note>original: *Shani*</note> is situated. || 4 ||

Through the piercing of the five pulses, each will be on a "seat": the **Base**, the **Seat**, the **Vehicle**, the **Lion**, and the **Lion-Throne**. || 5 ||

I shall speak of all these in order, starting from the Base. Based on the power of the planetary intersections—whether by the **Moon** <note>original: *Soma*, representing gentle planets</note> or by cruel planets—comes good or evil. || 6 ||

When the **Base** is pierced at the time of a man’s **Coronation** <term>Abhisheka: the ritual sprinkling or anointing of a king during his enthronement</term>, it makes his kingdom fall under the control of others; of this, there is no doubt. || 7 ||

---

### Analysis of the Results

<margin>4</margin>
When an intersection (*vedha*) occurs between the "Hand" <note>the constellation **Hasta**</note> and the "Serpent" <note>the constellation **Ashlesha**</note>, the Lunar Mansion is situated at the head of the men on the throne of the current ruler. When the intersection moves from the Base, it is a great defect. An intersection in the ninth position from **Mula** is to be understood as being caused by "fixed-enemy" planets. || 5 || 

Now, the author explains the results of the positions starting from the Base. Regarding the "Base lineage" || 6 || and the "Base of men" || 7 ||: 

If a coronation for a kingdom is performed, and on that day the star is in the **Base** position, then the king so anointed will enjoy his kingdom only while being dependent on others. What does this mean? It means he will exist only to serve others. || 7 ||

Next, the result of the **Seat** is explained: If a king is anointed while the stars are in the **Seat** position—specifically in **Bharani** <note>the 2nd Lunar Mansion</note> or **Pushya** <note>the 8th Lunar Mansion</note>—and a "six-fold intersection coronation" is performed, he then becomes beloved. Even for a lowly man, his leftovers become like the sacrificial fire. With a peaceful voice and pleasing words, he protects his subjects. || 8 ||

<summary>This page introduces the Simhasana Chakra (Throne Diagram), a predictive tool used to determine the fate of a king based on the astrological alignment of the 27 Nakshatras during his coronation. It specifically warns that a king crowned when the "Base" of the throne is afflicted will remain a vassal to others, while certain stars in the "Seat" position ensure a popular and protective reign.</summary>

# Now the results of the Broad Mansion are spoken
### Regarding the “Vehicle Mansion” — Verse 9
### Now the results of the Lion-Throne Mansion are spoken
### Regarding the “Fearsome Lion” — Verse 10; “Residing on the Lion-Throne” — Verse 11

Now, the manner in which an anointed king becomes radiant, a protector of his subjects, and a conqueror of the world is described: <term>When a malefic planet enters at that time...</term> <note>original: tat-kalendu-gate-krureti</note>. At the time of the royal coronation <term>Pattabhisheka: the formal ritual of anointing a monarch with consecrated water</term>, one must determine the lunar mansion of the Moon at that moment and ascertain its position on the "Seat" or other parts of the throne diagram. One then checks the "pulse-piercing" <term>Nadi-vedha: an astrological calculation used to determine if a sensitive point in the diagram is "pierced" or afflicted by a planet</term>. If that moment is free from the affliction of malefic planets <term>Kruragraha: "cruel" planets such as Saturn or Mars that are thought to bring hardship</term>, the king’s coronation should be performed. 

Regarding the "Base" <note>original: adhara</note>, if one is anointed while the Moon is in the mansion of the Base, the general result is poor and should be avoided. As stated in the *Vijaya-kalpataru* <note>The "Wishing-Tree of Victory," a classical Sanskrit text on royal rituals and law</note>: when the mansions are free from the movement of malefic planets—

> 8. When the mansion is situated on the **Seat**, the man becomes filled with splendor and righteousness. The primary prediction of these mansions brings peace to kings.
> 9. Regarding the **Vehicle** <note>original: vahnrksheti</note>: When a king sits upon this, his kingdom initially remains stable and he rules the earth for a long time.
> 10. A king situated on the **Lion** mansion becomes like a lion himself. He always loves battle and is forever difficult for his ministers to manage.
> 11. In the mansion of the **Lion-Throne**, the king is radiant and of a terrifying appearance; he becomes fickle-minded, prone to anger, and a source of suffering for his subjects.
> 12. If the Moon's mansion is "pierced" by malefic planets in the pulses at that time, but the Ascendant is auspicious, the king will find happiness upon the throne.
> 13. He who sits upon the throne during the "Lord of this Earth" alignment <note>a specific auspicious combination of planets</note> will destroy the host of his enemies and bring the world under "one umbrella" <note>a metaphor for universal sovereignty</note>.
> 14. If one sits upon the throne while it is occupied by a malefic planet, imprisonment, loss of land, and death will surely occur.
> 15. If Saturn <note>original: Sauri</note> occupies the **Base** mansion, it brings ruin; or else, the king is afflicted by disease, injury, and sorrow.

If at that time the Ascendant is occupied by the Moon and the king is radiant... <unclear>hi bo diya pavakeh</unclear>... [corrupt text] ... If the throne is occupied by malefic planets, but viewed by benefics, the peace is restored. If he sits while accompanied by what is desired, he rules the earth for a long time. (Verses 13–14).

# Now the results of the Inauspicious Pulse-Piercing are spoken
### Regarding the “Residence of Malefic Planets” — Verse 15; The “Base” Mansion

Even on the Lion-Throne, if Saturn occupies the mansion designated as the "Seat" or other royal mansions, one must predict famine and terrible, horrific suffering in that kingdom. (Verse 15). Regarding the "Seat" mansion, if Saturn is present... (Verse 16). Regarding the "Vehicle" mansion: this result applies to the "Three-Throne" system of the King, or when the King's own name-mansion is in the "Vehicle" position. (Verse 17). Regarding the "Lion" or "Lion-Throne": if the mansion associated with another king's name occupies these three positions, or the "Lion-Throne" itself, such results will occur. (Verse 18).

<meta>This page concludes the specific astrological prognostications for the Three-Throne Diagram (Simhasana Chakra). It shifts from the general descriptions of the diagram's parts to the dire consequences of malefic planets—specifically Saturn (Sauri)—occupying these positions during a royal transition. When **Saturn** <note>original: sauri; literally "the son of the Sun," a common name for the planet Saturn</note> is positioned on the **Seat**, he causes defeat in battle. Alternatively, he brings about the affliction of disease, physical injury, and great suffering. **(16)**

When Saturn is present there, the royal lineage perishes. Whether it be a loved one or a person of high status, the three-fold goals of life <note>original: trivarga; referring to Dharma (duty), Artha (prosperity), and Kama (pleasure)</note> are destroyed. **(17)** When the Son of the Sun <note>original: suryaja; another epithet for Saturn</note> resides upon the **Lion** or the **Lion-Throne** itself, then death will surely follow, even if the King is as powerful as his enemies. **(18)**

When **Saturn, Rahu, the Sun, and Mars** <note>original: shani-rahu-arka-maheya; Rahu is the north node of the moon, Arka is the Sun, and Maheya is Mars</note> are joined with the **Moon**, and these occupy the **Seat**, they become the "slaughterers" <note>original: vadhakarah; those who cause death or execution</note> of the king. **(19)** When a malefic conjunction occurs, or if a planet is in retrograde <note>original: vakra; the apparent backward motion of a planet, often considered an intensification of its influence</note>, or enters a malefic "pulse-point" <note>original: krura-nadi; a sensitive point in the astrological diagram that can be "pierced" by a planet's influence</note>, Saturn takes on the form of **Time** <note>original: kala-rupi; Time in the sense of the Great Destroyer or Death</note> himself through his union with the Moon on the Seat. **(20)**

In this way, the royal advisor should certainly provide the results of the lunar mansions. He <note>referring to a benefic planet like Jupiter, as clarified in the commentary below</note> creates a vast kingdom for the one whose **Seat** he occupies. **(21)** Thus ends the Throne section in the *Yamaliya Shastrodaya*.

->**|| The Diagram is Complete ||**<-

Regarding the phrase "Saturn, Rahu, Sun, and Mars": even for other constellations, if they occur within the three levels of the Seat, then destruction of the sacrificial ground occurs. **(18)** Regarding "malefic conjunctions" and "very retrograde": **(20)** Now the result of **Jupiter** <note>original: guroh; referring to Guru or Jupiter, the planet of wisdom and expansion</note> is spoken: "In this way the result of the mansions is given." Jupiter is the bringer of fear to all enemies and the giver of what is dear; this is easy to understand. **(21)** 

Thus ends the section called the "Three Thrones" in the *Jwalanmodika* <note>The "Lamp of Joy," likely the title of the overarching compendium or commentary from which this page is drawn</note>. **(3)**

<meta>This page transitions from the specific planetary effects on a King’s throne to the macro-cosmic "Tortoise Diagram" (Kurma Chakra). While the previous page focused on the "Lion Throne" and the malefic influence of Saturn, this section introduces the foundational structure used to predict the fate of entire nations and geographic regions by mapping the world onto the body of a divine tortoise.</meta>

Now I shall explain the **Tortoise Diagram** <note>original: Kurma Chakra; an astrological map shaped like a tortoise used to predict events for specific regions</note>. The statement "I shall proclaim the Tortoise Diagram" is easily understood. **(1)** Here, the greatness of the Tortoise Diagram is described by explaining how it supports the Earth. Through the knowledge of this... **(2)** Such are the mountains... **(3)** Of this kind... **(4)** It is the Lord... **(5)** In this way, it supports all living beings... **(6)** The body and shoulders... **(7)** Beneath that... **(8)** Within the eyes... **(9)**

---

I shall proclaim the **Tortoise Diagram** as it was told in the *Kaushala Agama* <note>an ancient technical or ritual manual</note>. By the mere knowledge of this, one understands national upheavals <note>original: desha-viplava; refers to political collapse, revolution, or natural disasters affecting a kingdom</note>. **(1)**

Thirty million gods reside in its various parts. **Mount Meru** <note>the sacred mountain at the center of the universe</note> rests in the middle of the Earth like the eye of a bull. **(2)** All such mountains, oceans, islands, and the **Elephants of the Directions** <note>mythical elephants that support the corners of the world</note> are sustained by me; through this, those gods are perfected. **(3)**

<margin>Mouth 5</margin> Such are all the oceans, and the Earth is held in this way. By digging at the root and touching the water, they do not... <unclear>legibility affected by staining</unclear>. **(4)**

This great-bodied Lord is like **Varaha** <note>the divine Boar avatar of Vishnu</note> atop the head of **Shesha** <note>the infinite cosmic serpent</note>. At the peak of its crest, it stands situated high above like a mosquito <note>a metaphor for the immense scale of the cosmic tortoise compared to other beings</note>. **(5)**

In this way, the coiled one <note>original: kundali; referring to the tortoise’s circular or protective posture</note> who supports all living beings is situated upon the Earth. They dwell on a portion of the tortoise's back like... **(6)**

The body, shoulders, head, tail, and claws—I shall describe these according to their measurements and their respective functions in order. **(7)** The body is situated at a distance of one hundred thousand *yojanas* <note>a term for a traditional unit of distance, roughly 8-9 miles</note>. Half of that is its width, and the tail is two units... **(8)**

The life-force consists of ten million units, and the head of seventy million. The distance between its two eyes is measured at ten million units. **(9)** Its feet are twice that, and the measurements extend to the tips of the nails on its toes. **(10)**

Thus, the measurements of the Tortoise were described in the *Adi Yamala* <note>one of the primary tantric texts</note>. Situated upon it is the Earth with its **Seven Islands** <note>original: Sapta-dvipa; the traditional Puranic geography of the world</note>. **(11)**

One should draw the diagram in the shape of a tortoise, complete with all its limbs. Its face is in the eastern part, and its tail is in the western circle. **(12)** One should draw lines from East to West and from North to South. Likewise, lines should be drawn from the Northeast to the Southwest, and from the Southeast to the Northwest. **(13)**

At the head and the four feet... situated on the back... for humans...

Regarding the fingers... **(10)** Situated above that... **(11)** In the eastern part... **(12)** From East to West... **(13)** At the head... **(14)** From that vision... **(15)** In the pericarp, twice... **(16)** **Ahichhatra** <note>an ancient Indian city/region, modern-day Ramnagar</note>... **(17)**

<meta>This page continues the description of the Tortoise Diagram (Kurma Chakra), moving from the general cosmic significance of the divine tortoise to a specific geographical mapping of the Indian subcontinent. The text correlates groups of lunar mansions (nakshatras) with specific regions, cities, and landmarks to predict where destruction or "upheaval" will occur based on celestial movements.</meta>

Excessive rain, drought, locusts, rats, parrots, internal rebellion, and invasion by a foreign army—these seven are known as the **Calamities** <note>original: itayah; these were considered the primary threats to a kingdom's prosperity</note>. **15**

When the moon or planets occupy the constellations of Krittika and the two Rohinis, they correspond to the navel of the Tortoise. This affects **Saketa** <note>Ayodhya</note>, **Mithila**, **Kaushambi**, and **Kashi** <note>Varanasi</note>. **16**

<margin>43</margin> **Ahicchatra**, **Gaya**, the **Central Altar** <note>the region between the Ganges and Yamuna rivers</note>, **Mekhala**, **Kanyakubja** <note>modern Kannauj</note>, and **Prayaga** <note>modern Prayagraj</note>; the **Middle Country** <note>original: Madhya-desha</note> faces destruction. **17**

The constellations Ardra, Punarvasu, and Pushya are situated on the **head of the Tortoise**. The left, the right, the back, and the north are thus considered. **18**

One should know the regions of Charendra, **Magadha** <note>ancient Bihar</note>, and **Malava** <note>Central India</note>; the Khatam and **Nepal**; the **Eastern Country** is destroyed. **19**

Facing the constellations of Purvashadha, Krittika, and Ashvini... the **foreigners** <note>original: mleccha; often referring to Greeks, Scythians, or later Islamic invaders depending on the text's age</note> and those who know the weapons of fire. **20**

The constellations Ashlesha, Magha, and Purvaphalguni fall within the **South-Eastern quadrant**. The regions of **Anga**, **Vanga** <note>Bengal</note>, **Kalinga** <note>Odisha</note>, and the people of **Koshala**... **21**

**Delhi** <note>original: Dahali</note>, Jayanta, Sulanjika, **Odisha** <note>original: Odiyale</note>, and the ancient Dancha and Kami countries are destroyed. **22**

The constellations Uttaraphalguni, Hasta, and Chitra have come to the **Southern belly** of the tortoise. These include Mount Dardara, Mount Mahendra, the region of Vanavasa, and **Sri Lanka** <note>original: Simhala</note>. **23**

The **Tapi** and **Bhimarathi** rivers, **Lanka**, Mount **Trikuta**, the **Malaya** mountains, **Shriparvata**, and the **Vindhya** range; these are destroyed in the **South**. **24**

The constellations Swati, Vishakha, and Anuradha fall within the gaze of the **Tortoise's eyes**. This includes **Nashik**, **Bhrigukaccha** <note>modern Bharuch, a major ancient port</note>, and the Tamala Forest. **25**

Varyali, Prakasham, Bhrigukaccha, and **Kerala**; Vetaram and Kotairam; these countries are destroyed suddenly. **26**

The constellations Jyeshtha, Mula, and Purvashadha are situated on the **back of the Tortoise**. These affect Varayavantaka, Avanti, and Eastern Malava. **27**

The Paravata, the **Yavana** <note>Greeks or Westerners</note>, and the island of **Saurashtra** <note>coastal Gujarat</note>; those dwelling in the water and the **Kingdom of Women** <note>original: stri-rajya; a legendary kingdom often placed in the Himalayas or distant North-West</note> are destroyed. **28**

The constellations Uttarashadha and Shravana fall within the **North-Western quadrant**. This affects the **Gujarat** kingdom, the **Yamuna** river, the **Desert region** <note>original: Maru-desha; the Thar Desert/Marwar</note>, and the **Saraswati** river. **29**

**Jalandhara**, Varada, and the **Ocean of Sand** <note>likely the Rann of Kutch or the Great Desert</note>; the peaks of **Mount Meru** and others situated in the North-West are destroyed. **30**

The three constellations beginning with Shatabhisha <note>original: shatabhaditrayam; specifically Shatabhisha, Purva Bhadrapada, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> and the three beginning with Krittika <note>Krittika, Rohini, and Mrigashira</note> correspond to the **North**. **31**
