The regions of the **Kekayas** <note>original: kekapa</note>, the "Elephant-Gems," and the lands of horses—all these countries situated in the **North-East** quadrant face destruction <note>when afflicted by malefic planets</note>. **34**

Wherever **Saturn** <note>original: Sauri; literally "the son of the Sun"</note> enters his own corresponding region in this diagram, he causes the destruction of men. If the presiding ruler is engaged in conflict <note>original: vigraha</note>, he will surely bring ruin to these lands. **35**

Wherever Saturn is positioned, he brings affliction; when he passes through the very center of these countries, he is the giver of ruin—of this there is no doubt. **36**

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the "Tortoise Diagrams" (Kurma Chakras). Having defined the geographical "Earth Tortoise" on the previous page, the author now introduces a multi-scale system of divination. This system applies the same tortoise-shaped grid to smaller entities: countries, cities, villages, fields, and individual houses, primarily to track the movement and influence of Saturn (Sauri).</meta>

### Commentary

<margin>55</margin> "Regarding the Earth Tortoise..." refers to verse 37. "Regarding the Previous Diagram..." refers to verse 38. Regarding City and others: In the **Country Tortoise** <note>original: desha-kurma</note>, one should write the first letter of the country’s name in the middle of the diagram. Here also, the sequence begins with the head in the East. In whatever division of the country the planet **Saturn** <note>original: Sauri; a common name for Saturn, the son of the Sun</note> is located, great fear will arise in that direction. This is the seed of the interpretation. The phrase "hand to hand" refers to the possibility of conflict. Where it "pierces" <note>term: vedha; an astrological phenomenon where a planet's position negatively affects a specific point or star in a diagram</note> the center or the front of a site, that location is where the events occur. As before, one should consider the planet **Jupiter** <note>original: Guru</note> as the provider of inauspicious results only if it is affected by the "piercing" of Saturn. If Jupiter is strong—meaning it is in its sign of exaltation—it is considered powerful during the day and other times. This concludes the second tortoise diagram. **2** Regarding the **Village Tortoise**... refers to verse 40.

---

### The Five Tortoise Diagrams

The **Earth Tortoise** <note>original: Prithvi Kurma</note> has been fully explained, beginning with the constellation **Krittika** <note>the Pleiades</note>. For countries and other entities, there are four additional tortoise diagrams. **37**

These Tortoise Diagrams consist of the **Country**, the **City**, the **Field** <note>agricultural land or a specific plot</note>, and the **House**. Having drawn the diagram as before, one places the first letter of the country's name within the grid. This constitutes the **Country Tortoise**; wherever Saturn is situated within it, destruction follows. **38** <gloss>Thus ends the Country Tortoise</gloss>

For a **City**, one should write [the name or letters] starting with the constellation **Krittika** and so on. The position held by Saturn becomes "corrupted" <note>original: dushta; spoiled or malevolent</note>, as does the point that he "pierces." **39**

Having drawn the **Village Tortoise**, use the first letter of the village's name. As before, wherever Saturn is located, one should predict "breaking" <note>original: bhanga; refers to the collapse of social order, defeat, or physical ruin</note> starting from the center. **40** <gloss>Thus ends the City Tortoise Diagram</gloss>

In the **Field Tortoise**, [the stars] are arranged starting from the field's own constellation. In the place where Saturn resides, destruction occurs and great fear is born. **41** <gloss>Thus ends the Field Tortoise Diagram</gloss>

Having drawn the **House Tortoise**, it is positioned facing the entrance of the house. By first identifying the constellation associated with the house's name, one determines the auspicious and inauspicious effects. **42**

If Saturn enters the center of the house, he causes grief and intense suffering. If he "pierces" the doorway, he is known to bring about fire and conflagrations. **43**

In the **South** <note>original: yasye/yamyam; the direction of Yama, god of death</note>, he is known to grant death and fear of demons. In the **West** <note>original: varunye; the direction of Varuna</note>, he is considered a giver of auspicious results. In the **North-West**, he brings emptiness and suffering. **44**

In the **North**, Saturn provides the gain of wealth. In the **North-East** <note>original: shambhave; the direction of Shiva/Shambhu</note>, he grants all success. A Saturn with high "strength" <note>astrological potency</note> is malevolent here, while one with little strength brings auspiciousness. **45**

Whenever Saturn <note>original: Bhanuja; "born of the Sun"</note> afflicts the Five Tortoises during the seven time-periods...

---

### Commentary

Regarding the Field... verse **41**. Regarding the House Tortoise... verse **42**. "If Saturn enters the center of the house..." refers to verse **43**. "Known to grant death..." refers to verse **44**. "Saturn provides the gain of wealth..." refers to verse **45**. "Whenever Saturn in the seven time-periods..." Regarding the Earth Tortoise: In a beloved country, Saturn is its destroyer in that direction. The constellation **Mula** belongs to that country. Between those two—the name of the constellation and the location—Saturn in the center affects the country, house, village, forest, and kingdom...

<margin>46</margin> In that place, a great obstacle arises; of this, there is no doubt. **46**

<margin>47</margin> When the **Moon** has moved into a bad position, one should not engage in the finality of battle <note>original: ranantika; refers to the decisive or final stage of a conflict</note>. This *Yamala* <note>original: Yamala; a class of ancient tantric texts upon which this astrological system is based</note> text is the destroyer of all obstacles. **47**

<margin>48</margin> The **Tortoise Diagram**, the Great Diagram, was first described in the *Yamala*. Knowledge of the three divisions of time <note>past, present, and future</note> is not achieved through mere imagination or conjecture. **48**

Thus, in the *Victory of the King through Breath-control* <note>original: Narapatijayacharya Swarodaya; a 12th-century manual on martial astrology and divination</note>, the Five Tortoises are recorded.

->॥ **Concluded** ॥ **5** ॥<-

->॥ **The Chapter on the Five Tortoise Diagrams** ॥ **5** ॥<-

<margin>45</margin> "In the shape of a lotus..." (1). "Starting with Krittika..." (2). "This earth has nine divisions..." (3). (4) (5) (6) (7). Thus ends the Lotus Diagram. "One should write [the stars] starting with Krittika..." (1). "Each channel..." (2). "Jambu-island..." (3).

### ॥ The Lotus Diagram ॥
One should draw a diagram in the shape of a **lotus** <note>original: Padma-chakra</note> with three circles and beautiful triangles. In the ninth chamber in the middle, the diagram arises. **1** One should place the stars <note>nakshatras</note> starting with **Krittika** <note>the Pleiades</note> three by three in succession. Starting from the center chamber, one should arrange the twenty-seven stars. **2** This earth has **nine divisions** <note>original: nava-khanda</note> situated in nine parts. Wherever **Saturn** <note>original: Sauri</note> is located, he brings destruction to his own region. **3** **Bhadrashva**, **Kuru-varsha**, and **Harivarsha** likewise; **Vaishrubhadra** <note>likely a variation of Ketumala or a specific sub-region</note>... these divisions are struck by three stars each. **4** **Ramyam**, **Hiranyayam**, and the **Kuru** division as well; these are said to be north of the back of **Mount Meru** <note>the sacred mountain at the center of the universe</note>. These three directions are said to be spotless. **5** The division named **Ketumala** is west of Meru. **Bharata** <note>India</note> is famous, situated in the eastern direction from Meru. **6** **Ilavrita** is the name of the division situated in the very center. These nine divisions are thus declared, as told in the *Adi-Yamala* <note>an ancient tantric source text</note>. **7**
॥ Thus ends the Lotus Diagram ॥

### ॥ The Auspicious Diagram of the Seven Islands ॥
One should draw the diagram starting from **Krittika** in the shape of a **serpent** <note>original: sarpakaram</note>, O Lord of Rama. There one should observe the affliction <note>original: vedha; an astrological "piercing" or negative influence</note> joined with seven channels <note>original: nadis</note>. **1** In the conjunction of each channel, **Jambu-dvipa** <note>the "Rose-apple Island," the central continent inhabited by humans</note> and the others are counted. The seven islands are in order; one should consider the position of **Saturn** there. **2** **Jambu**, **Shaka**, **Kusha**, **Krauncha**, **Shalmali**, **Gomedaka**, and **Pushkara**—these are the seven islands that suffer through the affliction of Saturn. **3** <insert>Done, Done</insert>

-> ॥ The determination of the auspiciousness and inauspiciousness of the Seven Islands. ॥ <-

### ॥ Now begins the Rahu-Kalanala Wheel ॥

Now, therefore, I shall fully describe that which was explained in the *Valyamala* <note>original: "valyamale"; likely referring to the Brahmayamala or a similar Tantric astrological treatise</note>. The **Rahu-Kalanala** <note>literally "The Fire of Time of the North Node"</note> regarding travel gives immediate proof of its efficacy in battle. ॥1॥

Journeys determined by the combinations of planets are contemplated in the science of astrology. These are achieved by the wise, yet they are not always effective for providing victory in the heat of combat. ॥2॥

I shall now describe the power of that **Rahu** in the deeds of war—the one by whose body's shadow the Sun and Moon are eclipsed. ॥3॥

Some speak of the planet **Rahu** as terrifying; however, they say that his lordship is not seen over the specific signs of the day or the ascendants. ॥4॥

Yet, those who hold lordship over the year, month, or day are considered less significant in battle than the rules of **Rahu**, the King, as described in the *Niyamala* <note>A specific astrological tradition or text</note>. ॥5॥

In the *Vedanta* and the *Puranas* <note>The primary philosophical and mythological scriptures of India</note>, the celestial wanderer **Rahu** is well-explained. Therefore, a true knower of this knowledge speaks the truth and does not babble falsehoods. ॥6॥

In all evil portents and in the calculation of birth horoscopes, **Rahu** gives immediate evidence of his influence. How then could he be considered without cause? ॥7॥

He who wanders in the form of Time (*Kala*) during destruction on the battlefield is the most terrible and fierce of all afflictions; know him to be **Rahu**, without a doubt. ॥8॥

I shall now speak of his names according to the sequence of his head and tail, as he wanders through the parts of the planetary watches <term>Yama: A three-hour period of the day; there are eight Yamas in twenty-four hours.</term>. ॥9॥

**Rahu**, **Enveloping Darkness** <note>original: "tamaḥ"</note>, **The Descending Node** <note>original: "pātaḥ"</note>, **Svarbhanu** <note>The "Light-Obscurer"</note>, **Son of Simhika** <note>Simhika was a lioness-demoness, the mother of Rahu</note>, **The Daitya-Bodied**, **The Crooked-Headed**, and he who is held in the **Great Night**. ॥10॥

His tail is **Ketu**, the **Crested One**, the **Serpent**, the **Staff**, the **Tormentor**, the **Portent of Calamity**, the **Poison-Bellied**, and the **Smoke-Bodied** as named by the great souls. ॥11॥

Thus, from the earth, **Rahu** is situated in all these forms. I shall now describe his wheel, which consists of the nature of the "Fire of Time." ॥12॥

One should draw a wheel of seven lines <note>original: "śalākā saptakaṃ"; a grid formed by seven vertical and seven horizontal lines</note>. Starting from the Northeast direction, one should write the star **Krittika** and the others. All twenty-eight stars <term>Nakshatras: The lunar mansions; usually 27, but here including the 28th, Abhijit, for technical precision.</term> including **Abhijit** should be written equally. ॥13॥

Wherever **Rahu** is situated, that direction and sub-direction should be indicated as his "face." Ten stars are placed in his face, and the remainder are arranged toward his tail. ॥14॥

Wherever the number counts to one hundred and one, they pervade the entire world with a brilliance equal to a thousand suns. ॥15॥

---

"Thus from the earth" ॥12॥ "Seven lines" — one should write them in sequence starting from the Northeast and Southeast directions. ॥13॥ ॥14॥
"One hundred and one" ॥15॥ Now, the **Rahu** of the directions is discussed:

The **Rahu** wheel is the boundary. From the beginning of the cosmic cycle, **Rahu** is established in the wheel of the stars. Therefore, from the beginning, the mathematical rule proceeds; by that calculation, whichever star he arrives at, that becomes the starting point for the stars of the "face."

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Rahu-Kalanala Wheel. It focuses on how to divide the twenty-eight lunar mansions (nakshatras) into two zones: the "Side of Life" and the "Side of Death," based on the current positions of the shadow planets Rahu and Ketu. The bleed-through mentioned in the metadata is significant but the text remains legible.</meta>

Starting from **Revati** <note>the 27th lunar mansion</note>, five lunar mansions are consumed. Starting from **Ashvini** <note>the 1st lunar mansion</note>, those to be consumed... <note>The text here describes the mathematical calculation of planetary movement through the zodiacal signs</note>. From the lunar mansion currently occupied by **Rahu** <note>the North Node of the Moon, depicted as a celestial dragon's head</note>, ten lunar mansions constitute the **Side of Life** <term>Jiva-pakscha: the auspicious zone of the diagram</term>. Fourteen mansions belong to **Ketu** <note>the South Node, the dragon's tail</note>. That is to say, from the mansion of Rahu, the fifteenth mansion... those ten mansions are the **Side of Death** <term>Mrtyu-pakscha: the inauspicious or "dead" zone</term>.

Furthermore, when Rahu is in **Revati**, then starting from the first quarter of **Ashvini** up to **Hasta**, it is the Side of Life. Beyond that, in **Chitra**, is Ketu. For fifteen [mansions]... then from **Svati** onwards, thirteen mansions are the Side of Death. Thus, in whichever mansion Rahu is stationed, five mansions are behind it and thirteen are yet to be consumed; therefore, it is the Side of Death. In the Side of Death is his "face"—meaning the mansion where Rahu actually sits is in the Side of Death. Where Ketu sits, that is the Side of Life. Including **Abhijit** <note>an intercalary 28th lunar mansion often used in divination</note>, fourteen mansions are consumed in the Side of Life, and fourteen are to be consumed in the Side of Death. ॥26॥ And so, the "face" mansion is in the Side of Death. ॥27॥

---

->The lunar mansions already consumed by Rahu are thirteen on the Side of Life.<-
->Thirteen are yet to be consumed; this is declared the Side of Death. ॥26॥<-
->His face is in the Side of Death; his tail, Life, is in the middle.<-
->In this way, these two [states of] Rahu must be understood by the practitioner of Phonic Science. ॥27॥<-
->Setting out for battle during the Side of Life, or likewise for battle in the Side of Death:<-
->A journey at the [correct] time is auspicious; if reversed, it grants great ruin. ॥28॥<-
->When the Moon and Sun are both stationed and established in the Side of Life,<-
->Then there is victory and gain for them at the time of the journey, without a doubt. ॥29॥<-

---

Regarding the result of these two, the text says: In the **Side of Life**, the kings... that is, if the **Moon** <note>original: "Chandra"</note> is traversing the Side of Life and the **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi"</note> is traversing the Side of Death, at that time there is victory. For the sake of victory, one should undertake a **journey** <term>Yatra: a formal departure, often for a military campaign</term>. "If reversed" means a journey in the opposite configuration grants ruin. What does this mean? In the Side of Death, while the Moon traverses the fourteen mansions to be consumed by Rahu, and the Sun traverses the fourteen mansions already consumed in the Side of Death—at this time, a journey for the conquest of the directions results in ruin. ॥28॥

Now the text explains the fruit of the journey if both the Sun and Moon are in the same side. Now, regarding the Lord of the Side of Life: if both the Moon and Sun are traversing the Side of Life at the time of the journey, then victory arises from that. There is victory in battle. Furthermore, there is "gain"—the acquisition of the Goddess of Victory. From the enemy's garden [or camp], horses, elephants, and wealth are captured and obtained. ॥29॥

->॥ Fortune ॥<-

<vocab>नक्षत्र (Nakshatra): Lunar mansion or star cluster, राहु (Rahu): North Node of the Moon, केतु (Ketu): South Node of the Moon, जीवपक्ष (Jiva-pakscha): Side of Life, मृतपक्ष (Mrtyu-pakscha): Side of Death, स्वरशास्त्र (Svarashastra): Science of Phonic/Breath Divination, यात्रा (Yatra): Expedition/Journey, चन्द्रादित्यौ (Chandra-Adityau): The Moon and the Sun</vocab>

Now, concerning the time when the Sun and Moon are positioned in the **Saptapate** <term>Saptapate: a technical calculation involving seven specific points or "lords" in a divinatory grid</term> and are within the "cleft" <note>likely referring to a transition point or a void between lunar mansions</note>... (Verse 5). For this very reason, if a person sets out on a journey at night when the Sun and Moon are in a position known only to the very wise, travel should not be undertaken. At such a time, even those possessing great wealth or elephants will face signs of conflict; the result will be retreat from battle and even death. ॥ 20 ॥ Now, regarding the auspiciousness of travel when the Moon is in the **Side of Life** <term>Jivapaksha: the zone of the diagram associated with vitality and success</term>, it is said: "When situated in the Side of Life..." ॥ 21 ॥ Now, regarding the division of the **Five Limbs** <term>Panchanga: literally 'five limbs,' here referring to a specialized division of Rahu's influence</term>... (Verse 1). Regarding the "Limbs of Rahu": although Rahu is traditionally considered to be only a head, the ancient sages in the *Brahmayamala* <note>an important early Tantric text</note> conceived of a five-limbed body for Rahu made of the **Lunar Mansions** <term>Nakshatras: the 27 or 28 divisions of the sky the moon passes through</term>. And so... (Verse 2). Regarding the "Mouth and other limbs": here, within the field of the Five Limbs of Rahu, I shall first describe the mouth and then the remaining limbs and their results in sequence according to the ritual method.

---

### When the Sun and Moon are both situated in the "Seven-fold" position at that time,
### Then loss, the breaking of safety, or even death shall surely occur. ॥ 20 ॥
### When the Moon is situated in the Side of Life, one's work will be firm and excellent.
### One should avoid the "Seven-fold" effort, considering its relative strength and weakness. ॥ 21 ॥

->Thus the Directions of the Rahu Wheels.<-

# ॥ Now, the Five-Limbed Rahu ॥

> Now I shall speak of the Rahu Wheel according to its five-fold division:
> The Mouth, the Belly, the Anus, the Tail, and the Skull is the fifth. ॥ 1 ॥
> Rahu is Time, the Shadow-disc, the planet that moves backward during the dissolution of the world;
> He is the smoky-black eclipse-maker; the South Node is called the Seven Sages. ॥ 2 ॥
> I shall also expertly explain the limbs starting from the Mouth;
> The wise person knows the Sun’s five limbs in the same way, derived from its rotation. ॥ 3 ॥
> I shall now state the names of Rahu as he is positioned within the mystical diagram:
> The Melting, the Scissor-like, the Tongue, the Lame, and the Agony. ॥ 4 ॥
> Those thirteen lunar mansions released ahead of the Rahu Wheel are:
> The Belly, the Casing, the Life, the Limbs of Desire, the Stomach, and the Heart. ॥ 5 ॥

---

<margin>
Thus ends 
the Wheel of Faults 
for the Five Limbs 
of the Seven Rahus.
</margin>

॥ Second, concerning Mars ॥ ॥ Thus ends the Wheel of Faults for the Five Limbs of the Seven Rahus ॥ I shall now describe the movement of the Moon and Sun through the individually divided lunar mansions of the Five Limbs and the results thereof. ॥ 3 ॥ Regarding the individual limbs situated in the movement-diagram: the seven names starting with "Melting" and "Scissor-like" refer to the lunar mansion where Rahu is currently stationed. ॥ 4 ॥ Regarding the division of the lunar mansions starting from the Mouth: "Those thirteen lunar mansions released ahead of the Rahu Wheel" are the Belly, the Casing, the Life, the Limbs of Desire, the Stomach, and the Heart. This means that the thirteen mansions located ahead of Rahu’s current mansion constitute the **Side of Life**; their names are "the one known as the Belly," "the one known as the Casing," "the Life," "the Limbs of Desire," "the Stomach," and "the Heart." ॥ 5 ॥

Thus ends the second limb. Now he speaks of the third limb, beginning with the words "The Belly-Fire constellation." From the current constellation <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions</term> where Rahu <note>the shadow planet or North Node of the Moon</note> is positioned, the fifteenth constellation is given names such as the "Burning Anus" and so on. || 6 ||

Thus ends the third limb. The rule regarding "Those of jaggery and grain" follows. || 7 || The remaining constellations are in that place, and so on. || 18 || <margin>48</margin> Now, to deliberate upon the "Time-Fire" <term>Kalanala: a divinatory diagram used to predict the fate of kings and armies</term>, he says: Regarding the "Fixed Form" <term>Sthira-rupa</term>, the Sun <term>Bhanu</term> and the Moon <term>Chandra</term> should be understood. The "Fixed Form" refers to the Sun's stay in a single constellation for thirteen and a half or fourteen days. The Moon, however, stays in one constellation for only about one day. Therefore, the Sun is designated as the "Fixed Form." || 9 || He speaks of the five combinations <term>Yoga-panchaka</term> of these two: "When Rahu is in a retrograde position..." || 10 || "When situated in the Moon's limb..."

The Belly-Fire constellation is the fifteenth from the Mouth.
This is the "Burning Anus" <term>Guda-prajvalita</term>,
which causes the breaking of an army in battle. || 6 ||
Thus ends the third limb.
The constellations related to jaggery and grain are five in number.
The wheel is like the Sun’s body, the limbs [calculated] in order. || 7 ||
The remaining constellations are elsewhere, in the middle of the face and tail.
Like the likeness of a skull, the head is the upper limb, the circle to be enjoyed. || 8 ||
<gloss>The Sun and Moon represent the fixed-form positions.</gloss>
I shall now describe the five combinations of these, in their proper order. || 9 ||
When the Sun is in the retrograde position of Rahu, and the Moon is in its own limb,
The traveler <term>Yayin: the attacking or marching king</term> is victorious there, 
and the stationary one <term>Sthayin: the defending or home-based king</term> is defeated in combat. || 10 ||
When the Sun is situated in the Moon's limb, and the Moon resides in the Anus,
The non-traveler [the defender] wins in battle when the time-fire has moved. || 11 ||
When the Sun is in the "Burning Limb" and the Moon is in the "Thunderbolt Limb,"
The strength of both armies perishes; consider the conqueror carefully. || 12 ||
When the Sun goes to Rahu's constellation and the Moon goes to the Rudra <note>Fierce or Shining</note> Limb in battle,
The stationary one wins the war, and death is ordained for the traveler. || 13 ||
When both the Sun and the Moon are situated in Rahu's retrograde limb,
There is destruction for both sides, but without doubt, the traveler wins. || 14 ||

---

Regarding the "Moon's limb" <term>Chandranga</term>, one should understand the current constellation of Rahu. The "Anus" <term>Guda</term> constellation is known to be the fifteenth from the current constellation of Rahu. This is easy to understand. || 11 || Regarding the "Sun," by "Thunderbolt Limb" <term>Vajranga</term>, he refers to the sixteenth constellation from the current position of Rahu. || 12 || Regarding "Rahu's constellation," by "Rudra Limb" <term>Rudranga</term>, the sequence beginning from the Thunderbolt constellation relative to Rahu is intended. || 13 || When both should be... || 14 ||

->॥ Thus ends the First Limb, consisting of the Five Combinations of the Mouth and other parts. ॥<-

# The Five Combinations of the Belly and Others

Regarding "The Sun is the Lord of Life" || 15 || When the Sun <note>original: "Bhanu"</note> is situated in the belly, this refers to the "Thunderbolt" position. Count fifteen constellations <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions</term> from Rahu's position to find the Thunderbolt || 16 || Regarding "The Limb of Desire," this refers to the current constellation of Rahu, known as the "Leaf" constellation, which is hidden || 17 || Regarding "The Casket," by this casket is meant the Tongue <note>original: "Jivha"</note> of the Lord of Life; the remaining constellations of Rahu follow || 18 || When the Sun is in the stomach <note>original: "Jathara"</note>, by this stomach is meant the thirteenth position for the Lord of Life, signifying the Sun and Moon for the side of the living <note>original: "Jiva-paksha"</note> || 19 ||

Thus ends the Five Combinations of the Belly. Now, the second set of combinations: "When the Moon is the Sun," meaning in the limb of the Moon. By this, the fifteenth constellation from Rahu is the "Conjunction," the fifteenth constellation || 20 || Regarding the "Second System," in the diagram... || 21 || "The Sun situated in the Sickness..." || 22 ||

---

->The Sun is in the place of life, and the Moon is in the sickness;<-
->It is declared the defeat of the stationary defender,<-
->And the constant victory of the marching invader. || 15 ||<-

->When the Sun is situated in the belly and the Moon in the thunderbolt;<-
->The defender is defeated in battle, and the invader conquers in the fight. || 16 ||<-

->When the Lord of Day <note>the Sun</note> is in the limb of desire, and the Moon is in the [position of] the...<-
->There, victory belongs to the defender, and the invader suffers great loss. || 17 ||<-

->When the Sun is in the casket, and the Moon is in the tongue;<-
->The defender meets with ruin, and the invader is somewhat victorious in battle. || 18 ||<-

->When the Sun is in the stomach and situated in the male system;<-
->The invader is victorious over the gods, and again against the opposing force. || 19 ||<-

->When the Sun is in the limb of the Moon, and the Moon is in the Sun’s place;<-
->At that time of destruction, who is the one who finds a little peace? || 20 ||<-

->In the diagram of the second system, when the skull is...<-
->The invader comes under the power of the Lord of Life, while the defender conquers the invader. || 21 ||<-

->When the Sun is in the sickness <note>original: "Gada"</note> and the Moon is in the mouth of Rahu;<-
->Then there is a great and terrible war, where the defender may be somewhat victorious. || 22 ||<-

<margin>no</margin> ->When the Moon is in the constellation of the Sun and the Sun is in the...<-
->It brings death to the invader and profit of victory to the one who stays. || 23 ||<-

->When Saturn is situated in the tongue while in the Sun’s path;<-
->It shall give victory to the invader and decree the defeat of the defender. || 24 ||<-

---

### The Five Rudras and Results

Thus ends the Five Rudras and others. Regarding "Situated in the Sun’s path," this means from the constellation where Rahu is situated, three constellations... and from the Sun's constellation, the combination known as the "Ape" constellation. These three are the designated constellations. Here, "Sun" means the Sun [position] 29 and "Moon" 7. In this combination, the invader <term>Yayi: the king who attacks or marches</term> acts, while the defender <term>Sthayi: the king who stays to defend</term> becomes [victorious]. The one who goes <note>the invader</note> meets death, while the one who stays, the defender (1), gains victory and profit. Victory in battle brings great greed <note>desire for spoils</note> from the king's speech, or rather, in the battle itself, even with very little, there is the gain of horses, swords, and armor. || 23 || Regarding "The Sun's Path": the Sun’s path and the Tongue [of Rahu] are the same. This is the current constellation of Rahu; the rest is the same. || 24 ||

It focuses on the "Five Combinations of the Belly" and provides specific outcomes for the defending (Sthayi) and invading (Yayi) parties.</summary>
